- (pt) mr president, ladies and gentlemen, let me briefly highlight two points mentioned in these reports which are of vital strategic importance in our view of the union. the first concerns the essential and central importance which we continue to attach to the principle of economic and social cohesion. we are concerned about the rumours that the commission is not taking this objective seriously. we continue to believe that economic and social cohesion remains a central objective of the union. secondly, i agree with what a colleague has said here on the subject of islands and i would also like to draw attention to the outermost regions. we would like in future to see more boldness in dealing with the issue of the outermost regions, which in my country concerns the azores and madeira. i take this opportunity to ask whether the commission can explain to us why the commission's report on the outermost regions, which parliament has been awaiting for a long time, is delayed.
- (pt) mr president, for years parliament has been calling for specific and uniform criminal protection of financial interests in the union. the truth is that frustration grows when one sees the fragility of the convention (and the protocols relating to it). five years after its signature, it has still not been ratified and has not yet entered into force. moreover, even the most voluntary attempts, such as those made by olaf at present, are not sufficient to dispel the legitimate concerns surrounding the system of guaranteeing individual rights. the proposals in mrs theato's report to encourage the commission to present a specific legislative act on the criminal protection of financial interests with a classification of offences, in particular fraud against the community budget, represent in our view a serious attempt to create a new criminal law, which is increasingly necessary and uniform at community level. the commission also called for the creation of an independent european body to coordinate and monitor the regularity of olaf investigations, independent of the judicial administration of each member state and under the supervision of the european court of justice. finally, in the wake of the tampere agreement, the report reaffirms the role of the european prosecutor, invoked at the time by a decision by commissioner antónio vitorino, who asked the igc to open its agenda to the creation of this new role, which everyone now considers indispensable. this report deserves to be adopted because it maintains the need to discipline the life of the institutions, from the legal and efficiency point of view, by completing the financial system of the union with the help of a substantive law and a european procedure adapted to the credibility of community economic life. but some very simple questions arise. will the legal basis today suffice to legally justify the creation of a new specifically community criminal law which, although subsidiary, will still compete in practice and in certain areas with the traditional and fragmentary criminal law of the member states? given the doubts, would it not be more appropriate to put judicial reform on the igc agenda, by introducing these proposals into the more general reform of the judicial system of the union, such as what is being asked for the european prosecutor? are these matters not of the utmost importance for a revision of the treaties which contributes to the strengthening of the area of freedom, security and justice in the union?
in writing. - (pt) as the structural funds and the cohesion fund are the main instruments available to the commission to resolve regional disparities, it is essential that the european parliament be involved in the elaboration of their general indicative guidelines without calling into question the principle of subsidiarity, the definition of the development strategy of each country being a matter for the respective member states. unfortunately, the european commission has already made progress in its guidelines and parliament's position does not carry much weight in the programmes for the period 2000-06. however, it must be stressed that these funds must give priority to the development of the countries and regions which are experiencing the most difficulties and the outermost regions, such as the azores and madeira in portugal, with a view to achieving economic and social cohesion. the truth is that the commission's guidelines do not entirely meet these objectives. it is also important that there are clear indications and sufficient funds in the areas of the creation of quality jobs with rights, as well as the effective promotion of equal rights and opportunities, in support for the social and solidarity economy, rural development, small and medium-sized enterprises, the improvement of the quality of life of urban populations in disadvantaged areas, in particular in the social housing sector, with a view to sustainable urban development.
in writing. - (pt) we agree, in essence, with the rapporteur's assessment and considerations concerning the sixth periodic report on the social and economic situation and development of the regions of the european union. we shall therefore limit ourselves to highlighting only some aspects. firstly, as the commission itself says, the fact that the gdp per capita of some of the poorest regions increased sharply between 1991 and 1996 is largely due to the inclusion of the new german länder in the calculation of the community average, starting precisely from the first year. this is clearly the reason for a significant reduction in the level of gdp per capita in the union. secondly, it is particularly important for us to note that the recovery of certain regions - where it has taken place - has been accompanied by a minimal increase in employment, which therefore requires new development strategies, with a major concern for this area. this contradicts the slow adoption of measures to promote employment which are exclusively reserved for the internal policies of the member states, as the report says. last but not least, we will mention the rapporteur's proposal that in future this type of report should include, among other things, an analysis of the interregional evolution of cohesion in each member state. the multiplicity of situations and the different developments from one region to another within the same state require a thorough assessment of this question in order to allow and guarantee the correction of regional (and other) policies in order to ensure cohesion also at the internal level of each member state.
in writing. - (pt) by claiming to apply the principle of subsidiarity to european competition policy, the commission decentralizes responsibility for decisions and abuses of dominant positions in relation to agreements between undertakings which create market distortions to the benefit of national authorities and courts, although merger and state aid issues remain within the notification regime. the current position aims to create more expeditious and cheaper forms of competition policy enforcement, by applying community law at the level of national courts rather than at the level of the european court of justice, even targeting specialised courts. this partial renationalisation of competition policy may lead to additional costs for the member states. in addition, as the rapporteur points out, public monopolies are often exchanged for private ones in the name of competition policy, through the privatisation of important public sectors and companies, as in portugal, with the negative consequences for the country and workers.
in writing. - (pt) in his comments on the commission's annual report on competition policy, the rapporteur points out that competition policy cannot be separated from social and environmental policy and must contribute to full employment, economic and social cohesion, environmental protection and consumer protection. but when it comes to talking about the essential instruments for achieving these objectives, the rapporteur practically limits himself to criticising the various aspects of state aid and demanding the harmonisation of the rules on the reimbursement of illegal state aid, calling into question the right of member states with weaker and structurally more fragile economies to support economic sectors affected by liberalisation and international competition. the rapporteur also ignores the main causes of distortion of competition rules, namely: the increasing concentration of enterprises and its consequences on the distortion of competition rules; the transformation of public enterprises into private monopolies with serious social implications, in particular for employment and the price of public services; the abuse of a dominant position by various monopoly groups to unacceptable practices in the environmental and social fields, including the dismissal of thousands of workers. that is why we are voting against this report.
- (pt) mr president, ever since plato's republic, the western world has been systematically tempted to replace the government of the people by the government of the wise. our parliament began by inviting the wise men to help it appreciate the actions of the european commission and they took advantage of this to seize its destiny. in this second report, the wise men go further and criticise the political groups in parliament which have been reluctant to dismiss the existing european commission because of the political affiliation of certain members of the commission. the wise think that this problem can be solved by prohibiting the commissioners from belonging to political groups. if we follow the wise, parliament should give up its control over the european commission and entrust it to a committee for the safeguarding of good morals in public life, a permanent and unelected committee, probably made up of another group of wise men. in their second report, the wise men tell us that italy is financed by the cohesion fund, that the erdf and the social fund account for two thirds of the structural funds, that the principle of additionality and complementarity of the structural funds are equivalent, that the agricultural lobby imposes the financing of rural development by the eaggf guarantee section, that the partnership principle applies only to the commission and the member states. this is a lesson in wisdom with 100% ideology and 0% discernment. that is not how we will contribute to reforming the european institutions in full respect of democratic institutions. the van hulten report was a brilliant report at first; i want to pay a deep and sincere tribute to the work of our colleague. unfortunately, his report has been changed several times in the worst possible way and has become a document which is in fact advocating something which we cannot accept.

- (pt) madam president, mr minister and president-in-office of the council, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the rotating presidency is one of the most unique features of the european union. at the impression of a new beginning is, of course, very misleading. continuity of work would not be compatible with a different europe every six months, but the rotating presidency is a reviving mechanism lasting merger of the luxembourg, cardiff and cologne processes, in relations with africa, in the advancement of modernity issues - the information society and digital television - and in the presidency on angola. that is also an unfortunate fact. it is partial, unbalanced, and does not help to resolve a tragic conflict that has been raging for so long. we do not like the actions of unita in recent years either, but that does not mean that we accept the war path, that we ignore the very serious accusations of a dark war economy that punishes the angolan state itself, that we support, - (pt) mr president, mr minister, mr president-in-office of the european council, whom i cordially congratulate, commissioners, ladies and gentlemen, portugal will be holding the presidency during the six-month period, in accordance with the rotating presidency system. this system guarantees fairness in the contribution of each member state to the construction of europe if the presidency is exercised effectively and pursues a wise balance between the affirmation of national perspectives and the perception of the general interest of europe. it is clear that the rotating presidency requires that the main points of the way forward should be the subject of a consensus among the member states and their citizens. otherwise we would have a succession of divergent presidencies without lines of force or led by an invisible hand, which would be unacceptable. i expect a lot from the portuguese presidency. i hope it will help to answer the central question: where is the european union going and which way is it going to go? only then will we be able to move on to institutional reform, the condition or consequence of enlargement which must be achieved while respecting the balance of nations, powers and policies of the european union, which has been pursued with difficulty for decades. building is difficult and slow, but destroying is quick, so extreme caution will be required in dealing with this matter. the european union is in danger of being disintegrated. some people think: it's complex, very ambitious, why portugal? i say to them: if this has to be done, why not portugal? when we've decided on the main case, we'll move on to the other cases. what we think of the european union is incompatible with certain solutions. i will not accept that we talk about enlargement at any cost, as some, perhaps too many, do. i will not accept that, in a redistribution of power, it is entrusted to a board of directors made up of the strongest member states or that only those states have their place and power in the european commission. i will not accept that my language is not an official language. i will not accept the dismantling of the common agricultural policy or its continuing to favour some and ignore others, nor will i accept the abandonment of solidarity and the search for real convergence of levels of development. economic and social cohesion, a principle enshrined in the treaties, must once again be at the forefront of the union's concerns and the basis of all policies. i will not accept the continuation of the present system of financing, just as i will not accept the absence of a common foreign and security policy. we talk a lot about the europe of citizens, about wanting to involve them, to inform them, to mobilise them around strong ideas, to meet their expectations. the european parliament's representativeness is not recognised by the minority status which the european council has given it for the intergovernmental conference. similarly, it is not by deciding the opposite of what the european parliament wanted last month that we will get anywhere. this is what happened with the question of the arms embargo on indonesia. but how can we get the citizens' interest, who often seem so distant? let's try to find a solution to this problem. like my fellow citizens, i see the european union from its western border of the azores, one of the regions defined as 'outermost' in the treaty on european union. what i want to see is equal opportunities, convergence of living conditions, while continuing to live there. for me, enlargement is not simply an addition of territories to meet foreign policy challenges, it should mean sharing, solidarity, the same capacity for personal fulfilment wherever one lives, north, east, south or centre. just as i am interested in the big issues that look at the treaties from a perspective where people matter, i am also interested in the impact of the small decisions of the european union on my region, and therefore important for my fellow citizens. at this stage we would like to know what measures will be taken for the outermost regions. what is the future of the milk production on which our economy depends? we have unparalleled conditions for producing it and we know how to produce it in almost perfect symbiosis with nature. as you can see, i expect a lot from the portuguese presidency. i hope that it will help to open a path which preserves delicate balances, that it will advance, in this context, matters of the greatest importance by giving them their own stamp. i hope that it will involve the citizens and be attentive to their legitimate aspirations. i hope that by serving portugal, it will serve the european union.

the rapporteur. - (pt) very briefly, i would like to say that the principle of relative stability is not in question for the simple reason that there is no transfer of quotas between member states, so that principle is not in question. and secondly, we are talking about a temporary suspension of quotas to oblige the member states to achieve the objectives of the pops. and this is all the more clear with the approval of amendment no 3 by mr ford. i, as rapporteur, will propose that this amendment be voted in favour. secondly, i would like to say that the objective of point 6 of my report is precisely to urge the european union to have an effective sanctions regime, since if we are not able to impose sanctions that oblige countries to respect the objectives of the pops, they are not only useless but, more seriously, they will become an instrument of discrimination between, on the one hand, the member states that apply them and reduce their fleet and, on the other hand, the member states that do not apply them and are not sanctioned. the rapporteur. - (pt) mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the european commission's report on the first year of implementation of the multiannual guidance plan for the fourth generation of fishing fleets, known as pop iv, for the period 1997-2001, shows that in 1997 there was a reduction of 2% in the capacity of the community fleet in terms of gross tonnage and 3% in terms of power. thus, on 1 january 1998, the community fleet was already almost 16% below the final objectives of pop iv in terms of power and 7% in terms of gross tonnage. however, there are serious reservations about this apparently optimistic overall assessment of the effectiveness of pops as a means of matching the size of the community fleet to the availability of fisheries resources. the first reservation arises from the difficulties in comparing the objectives set out in the pop iii and pop iv, due to the change in the criteria for measuring and segmenting fleets. the second reservation we could make is that the respective member states continue to apply their own measurement criteria, which makes it difficult to compare any evaluation. i should also add that in the report the commission itself makes a remark about the unreliability of the data provided to us. the third reservation that could be made to this apparently optimistic assessment of the report is that there is a clear imbalance in the achievement of these objectives between the different member states; the commission found that two member states had not achieved these objectives and one member state had provided information in an unacceptable form. however, other member states, such as portugal, spain, denmark, ireland and the united kingdom, have already reached or even exceeded the final objectives set by the mff iv, as in the case of portugal. it is on this point that i propose essentially two types of measures in my report. the first type of measures consists in the homologation and homogenisation of the criteria for measuring and segmenting fleets, so that a comparative, accurate and continuous assessment of the community fleet in each member state can be carried out. and this is fundamental to the effectiveness of pops. the second type of measures concerns the need for community standards which implement an effective system of penalties. it is for this purpose, and for this purpose alone, that i suggest, by way of example - i stress this - in my report, that quotas be suspended for member states which do not respect the pops in order to oblige them to do so. that is why i thought it appropriate to accept mr ford's amendment no 13, in that it stresses that this suspension is temporary. i would also like to stress that in this case there is no transfer of quotas between member states, there is no violation of the principle of relative state, there is only a temporary suspension of quotas in order to politically oblige countries to respect the pops. this is fundamental, because financial sanctions under the fifg have been shown to be ineffective. if this suspension is not applied, member states will not be encouraged to achieve the targets set. moreover, if some member states comply with the pops and others do not, and if infringing member states are not penalised, this will in fact create serious discrimination in the application of the common fisheries policy between member states and penalize some fishermen more than others. finally, i would add that if these two types of measures mentioned in my report are not put into effect, then not only are the multiannual fisheries guidance programmes completely useless, but, worse still, they will also be a source of discrimination and distortion of competition between member states, between shipowners and between - (pt) mr president, the commission's analysis of the first year of implementation of the fourth generation of multiannual guidance programmes shows that the situation varies from one country to another. some countries did not meet the targets or did not provide reliable data, while others met or even exceeded them, as in the case of portugal. thus, at the beginning of 1998, the community fleet was already below the final objectives of pop iv, both in terms of tonnage (less than 16%) and in terms of power (less than 7%). moreover, while france and the netherlands had not yet reached the objectives of the pop and italy had not provided reliable data, portugal not only exceeded the european average with a reduction of 38% in tonnage and 21% in power, but it was also the country that had reduced the fishing effort of its fleet the most, with all that this implies in terms of impairment of self-esteem, job losses and threat to the survival of nearly 180 major communities. the report also calls for the establishment of a european fisheries policy, which is based on the principle of subsidiarity, and for the establishment of a european fisheries policy, which is based on the principle of subsidiarity. the current situation in portugal is particularly serious and is essentially the result of a common fisheries policy which does not take into account the specificities of each member state, although at national level there is a growing awareness among policy-makers that they want their country to be the best pupil in terms of implementing european standards. it is now clear that a fisheries policy based on the lack of aid for the modernisation and renewal of the fleet, on measures to encourage the destruction of vessels and the cessation of activity, on the one hand, and on the other hand, on harsher working conditions and low yields, combined with strong competition from outside, has contributed to a drastic reduction in national fishing capacity and is causing portuguese fishermen to emigrate to fleets in other countries, precisely those that have not respected the objectives of the pop and have even increased their fishing capacity. the commission has also proposed that the cfp be amended to take account of the specificities of each country, by providing special aid for the renewal and modernisation of the fleets of the various member states that have already achieved the objectives, by taking measures to help the survival of fishing communities, by improving the living and working conditions of fishermen and by paying compensation for periods of fishing closure, such as the sardine closure in portugal, or for any other necessary reductions in fishing.
- (pt) mr president, i should like to congratulate mr cunha in particular on the quality of his report. ladies and gentlemen, the commission report analyses the achievements of several member states and indicates that portugal has largely achieved the objectives set in all segments of its fleet; in addition, there is a significant correspondence between the information contained in the vessel files and the data presented in the portuguese government's audit report. as regards the report for 1997, it was simply necessary to provide information on the five fleet vessels, which is now practically done, since only three vessels are missing. this aspect of achieving the objectives of the pops, which could and should be positive, has had negative repercussions at national level, both in the fisheries sector and in the press and public opinion in general, since achieving the objectives in principle implies a reduction in the capacity and size of the fleet. we cannot penalise countries that meet the targets and this is what we have to explain to fishermen; we therefore suggest that effective sanctions be put in place, such as temporary reduction of quotas for the state which is in breach or fails to meet the deadlines for submitting data. we would therefore like to put things in perspective: when people say that we are destroying more and more ships, this is not true, at least when compared with the existing situation. in portugal, for example, 144 new projects were submitted between 1992 and 1995. from 1996 to 1999, 40 new projects were submitted. however, there is one aspect to which i would like to draw the commission's attention, and that is the following: the adjustment of the fleet must be carried out while at the same time responding to the need to safeguard the working conditions on board and the hygiene and health conditions for the fish. of course, compliance with these conditions may sometimes require an increase in tonnage. finally, i would ask the commission not to start from scratch when it establishes a possible fifth mgp in the future, but to take account of the efforts already made by the member states and the results obtained by them in previous mgp.

- the next item is the report (a5-00003/2000) by mr marinho, on behalf of the committee on legal affairs and the internal market, on the draft council decision amending decision 88/591/ecsc, eec, euratom establishing a court of first instance of the european communities (5713/1999 - c5-0020/1999 - 1999/0803 (cns)) and the draft council decision amending decision 88/591/ecsc, eec, euratom establishing a court of first instance of the european communities (9614/1999 - c5-0167/1999 - 1999/0805 (cns)).
the rapporteur. - (pt) mr president, it is not clear that the future revision of the treaties in the framework of the igc will open the door to a revision of the community's judicial systems. the idea of putting this issue on the agenda discussed today was maintained until the helsinki summit and is still being defended by various member states, by the courts themselves. it is still alive in the european parliament, whose legal affairs committee is preparing an opinion, which i believe is of high quality, on the nature and scope of judicial reform. i have no doubt that parliament's next report on the preparation of the intergovernmental conference will take up this issue with emphasis for three reasons which, in my view, are consensual in nature: because more justice must be done in less time, because the legal basis for an area of freedom, security and justice must be laid and, finally, because our vision of the european area must be more than merchant and economic and give citizens the conviction that they are part of a community of law based on democratic law and courts, respecting their fundamental ethical and legal values and defending their interests, even when the force of national law is not enough to guarantee them full citizenship. in fact, these fundamental reasons, which justify a fundamental reform of the community's entire judicial system, are also the reasons for the possible reform of the court of first instance which we are dealing with today, but on a different legal basis: that of the treaty of amsterdam. two or three reasons justify it in particular, and i will cite them: the imbalance between the increasing volume of cases and the technical and human capacity of the luxembourg courts; the strict requirement of multilingualism in proceedings, which is the corollary of respect for the language and national legal traditions; finally, the fact that this court - the court of first instance - is the only instance to which citizens and businesses can turn in economic disputes concerning decisions of bodies affecting them. the proposal to increase the number of judges to 21 is therefore fully justified, and is ultimately linked to the improvement of the efficiency of the court, which could be set up with two additional chambers, each of which would be composed of three judges. the second project concerns the extension of the jurisdiction of the court of first instance, which until now has been able to hear only actions for annulment brought by private individuals and which is now also able to hear appeals brought by member states. the subject of the present case is the possibility for states to bring actions relating to transport policy, competition rules applicable to undertakings, state aid, trade defence measures, the implementation of funds and other action programmes providing for the granting of community financial support, in particular actions relating to the union budget. by giving member states the possibility of having recourse to the court of first instance, which can already hear cases having the same subject-matter as those brought by private individuals, the court of first instance may have to hear appeals brought by a member state, which will put private individuals and states on an equal footing in the course of the proceedings. some people will say, mr president, that this is a slight reform, but it is a possible reform, based on the standards which allow it in the amsterdam treaty. this is basically the reform which the court of justice of the european communities had envisaged and requested, independently of the igc and on the basis of the treaty in force today. i dare believe, if only because of the broad consensus in committee, that parliament will accept these two reform proposals and give its assent to the court. it is only to be hoped that the council will do the same. finally, my final wish is that the court should make the best possible use of the new powers and competences which our parliament has decided to confer on it.
the rapporteur. - (pt) madam president, despite article 151 of the treaty of amsterdam, culture has been the poor relation of the whole series of programmes and financial, technical and human resources mobilised for the european project. it must be said that the objectives which guided the foundation of the 'european home' in which we live today 50 years ago were more specifically economic and social. it must also be said that throughout these five decades european policies have been subject to many different impulses, in the most varied circumstances and for the most varied reasons. but in the minds of the founding countries of europe, the mold of civilization which impelled them to create the conditions for lasting peace could not be absent. this mold of civilization is made up of a profound cultural diversity. a europe of national identities can live long in peace and prosperity if this cultural diversity remains vigorous, eliminating the petty and aggressive resentments which usually lead to exacerbated nationalisms. for this to happen, it is essential that we know each other and share the values of civic humanism and tolerance, of knowledge, of artistic and cultural creation, which are rooted in the fertile humus of europe and express the highest and deepest values of the human spirit. therefore, in order to make this knowledge and sharing fully possible, they must be based on a conception of culture with the essential dimension of european democracy. there is no doubt that real convergence, economic and social cohesion, the single currency, the fight against unemployment and exclusion, and competition are all linked to the creation of conditions that are designed to provide greater equality, greater development, a better quality of life and better opportunities for european citizens. there is no doubt that the common defence and security policy can strengthen a european identity and affirmation in the world. there is no doubt that human rights policy is aspiring to be a global policy. but if cultural policies do not give a higher and distinct meaning to all this, neither europe nor european democracy will be able to go very far. it is only through culture and only through culture that millions and millions of citizens can recognize themselves as europeans, that they can cultivate, develop and deepen their sense of belonging to europe. but, madam president, that is the momentum that european policy has lacked. we talk about culture, cultural cooperation, emblematic actions, major initiatives, networks of cultural agents and operators, but we carefully avoid referring to a cultural policy of the european institutions, which would in no way conflict with the principle of subsidiarity. we even talk, with a rhetoric that is sometimes reasonably expressive, about the importance of culture for the european project, but we spend ridiculous sums on programmes which should serve continuously and regularly more than 300 million people, giving them access to a heritage that belongs to them and arousing in them an active and interactive appetite for contact with the great values and the great creations that make up this heritage. the european citizens whom we represent here have the right to demand that the institutions of the union adopt a more coherent, more effective and, above all, more european approach. these considerations serve as an introduction to the evaluation by our assembly of the joint project concerning the culture 2000 programme approved by the conciliation committee on 9 december. it will be recalled that culture 2000 creates a single instrument for financing and programming cultural cooperation. the conciliation process has led to satisfactory solutions on virtually all the amendments adopted at second reading, so that it is possible to say that the basic idea of our parliament has been reasonably well implemented, in particular the political priority concerning the creation of conditions for the actions envisaged by culture 2000 to involve as many citizens as possible. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competent authority' to cover the whole of the european union. however, thanks to the personal commitment of commissioner viviane reding in the search for a constructive solution, it was possible to reach a mixed solution to this position. the commission has undertaken to present a report on the operation of the programme before 30 june 2002, in which it will decide whether the financial resources available are sufficient or insufficient and where it will, if necessary, propose a revision of this point. it should be borne in mind that the outcome of this proposal will depend, in the final analysis, on the institutional mechanism which has been found in the meantime and which corresponds to the basic meaning of the word 'conciliation'. i would like to conclude by proposing that the plenary vote on the joint text adopted by the conciliation committee on the culture 2000 programme and i already wish it success.
in writing. - (pt) the purpose of this proposal for a directive is to lay down the conditions for the posting of employees from third countries in the framework of the provision of crossborder services. third-country nationals who are legally resident in a member state do not enjoy the right of free movement within the european union. so far, non-eu workers have been faced with serious obstacles to their free movement within the european union. it is therefore a good thing that the movement of workers within the european union is being facilitated, even if they are nationals of third countries. however, the proposal for a directive only covers their movement to another member state if they are posted on behalf of an employer established in a country where he has his habitual residence, so that the possibility of moving is limited to the period of posting, and only in the member state where he was posted. this means that the fundamental concern of this proposal is not to solve the problem of the movement of these workers but only to create better conditions for the companies providing services. the berger report also introduces, in the name of simplifying procedures, some questionable fundamental changes, notably the creation of a common information system giving access to cards issued by the authorities of all the member states.
in writing. - (pt) i voted in favour of the resolution giving a favourable opinion on the convening of the igc because it enshrines the philosophy of the portuguese presidency, which enjoys the support of a large majority of parliament, of the openness of the igc agenda, beyond the points strictly related to the rebalancing of power between large and small member states, as originally described in the convening of the helsinki council. unfortunately, in the subjects addressed in the resolutions on the subjects of the treaty on european union which will be subject to revision, there is so far no indication of the need to revise article 7, which deals with the suspension of a state in the event of a serious and persistent breach of the 'founding' principles of the union, as set out in article 6. as the current crisis with austria shows, the union has the right to defend itself. the legal mechanisms provided for in the treaties are, however, weak, politically and legally difficult to apply, do not organise the powers of the institutions and do not guarantee judicial treatment of a process of the utmost importance, namely the suspension of a member state. i therefore believe that this point, which in itself warrants a wide-ranging review, should be urgently placed on the igc agenda.
in writing. - (pt) in our view, the central questions which must be addressed at the present time in connection with the convening of an intergovernmental conference to review the treaties go far beyond the controversy raised here concerning the size of its agenda, the possibility or not of formulating proposals for new subjects to be included in it, or the level of parliament's participation. the important questions, in our view, concern not only the possibilities and objectives of the igc but also the topics which will be discussed there. we are not sure whether it will be possible because the objectives are far from being in line with the planned enlargement to which we are constantly referring. this is particularly apparent from the content of the reports, particularly with regard to the points not resolved in amsterdam - which foreshadowed the subsequent formation of unacceptable directorates - but also with regard to those relating to the second and third pillars, which are beginning to take shape, particularly in the direction of an undesirable militarisation of the european union. these are some of the main reasons why we do not support the motion for a resolution under consideration.

in writing. - (pt) we are very concerned about the rise of far-right forces, particularly in austria, germany, france, belgium and italy. it is vital to discuss the root causes and take the necessary steps to overcome them. given that the worsening social inequalities, unemployment, poverty and social exclusion are creating mistrust among citizens and conditions favourable to far-right populism, it is urgent that the european institutions take the necessary measures to prevent the spread of racist and xenophobic ideologies. this requires a profound change in the socioeconomic policies of neoliberal capitalism, giving priority to the creation of quality jobs with rights, adopting policies to strengthen participatory democracy and educating citizens with a particular focus on the values of democracy and solidarity. we condemn and combat the ideas and activities of the extreme right, but we also express our solidarity with the people of austria and support all those who are fighting to strengthen democracy and to combat racism and xenophobia.

- (pt) mr president, commissioner, it is clear today that agriculture is becoming less and less able to retain the rural population, especially the young, on its own. the recent publication of the 6th periodic report on european regions provides evidence of this: the 25 most agricultural regions of the european union are the regions with the highest unemployment rates, in addition to their traditional problems of ageing and desertification. it is in this context that the commission has begun to launch initiatives, little by little and - unfortunately - rather slowly, which in the long run must balance or complete the cap, initiatives whose objective is to achieve a more comprehensive approach to all the endogenous resources of the rural world, by mobilising them in initiatives and investments beneficial to the wealth and employment of the populations concerned, whether farmers or not. this is the case with the recent rural development policy launched with the reform of agenda 2000, and particularly with the leader community initiative, launched by the leader i and ii programmes in 1991 and 1994 respectively, and now continuing with leader+. i would therefore like to emphasise three points: - first, it is essential that the selection of local action groups responsible for the organisation and implementation of projects is not politicised and that this selection should be based solely on the merits of the projects. preference should be given to projects in which non-public entities are in the majority. in some cases, they will have to represent more than 50% of the lag partners; the second question is that in cooperation actions between lags and similar organisations in third countries, the original wording of the commission - paragraph 18 - should be respected and not the restrictive version of the star committee, revealed to this parliament. that is why i have tabled an amendment to that effect, which the committee on agriculture has approved and which i hope will be adopted in plenary; and thirdly, i would like to point out that beyond 2006 leader must cease to be a pilot community initiative and become a component of rural development in the cap, the name of which, in my view, should be changed to 'common agricultural, regional and rural policy'. i would like to congratulate mr procacci on his excellent report.
- (pt) madam president, mr president of the commission, commissioners, ladies and gentlemen, i rise to speak on the subject of the world trade organization, on which there is agreement between the commission's documents and the positions of the socialist group, an identity of views, which is not the case in other sectors, as has already been mentioned. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. what conclusions can we draw from this in principle? that these regulations, these rules adopted by the world trade organization have led to an increase in the volume of trade, and therefore in wealth, but when we look at the indices of the distribution of this wealth, we unfortunately find that the gap between the richest blocs and countries in the world and the poorest and least developed countries is continuing to widen. it is therefore legitimate and necessary to ask what the purpose of the present common rules is. what is the point of the current negotiations and the current functioning of the world trade organization if they contribute to an increase in trade volume on the one hand, but create dysfunctions on the other hand and, above all, widen the gap between rich and poor countries? that is why we have voted in favour of the strategy presented here by the commission for the seattle negotiations and the subsequent ones. and above all, we support this strategic vision, which will require courage and boldness. on the one hand, it will be necessary to insist that social rights, environmental rights and consumer protection be integrated into the short-term negotiations. but above all, we must have a long-term, ambitious and courageous vision, aimed at reforming not only the wto but also the ilo, the united nations and, above all, the financial institutions, notably the imf and the world bank. we must be courageous and not limit ourselves to a partial examination of globalisation, but have the courage to place it in a broader context, so that wealth does not only serve the most developed countries and blocs, but that there is also a harmonious evolution and development in all regions of the world.

in writing. - (pt) the commission's proposal reduces by more than 100 to just 50 the urban areas eligible for the new urban programme for the period 2000-06, and reduces funding by 30%. portugal is severely penalized by this proposal, even though it is the country in the european union with the greatest need for this. the number of projects financed is reduced to two, whereas ten projects are planned for germany, nine for the united kingdom and eight for italy. so far, six projects have been funded in portugal, benefiting the municipalities of porto, gondomar, lisbon, odivelas, oeiras and amadora. it is therefore essential that the commission reviews its position, particularly as regards the award of projects in portugal, in order to create the conditions for the continuation of important work on the economic and social revitalization of urban and peripheral areas, so that sustainable urban development can be guaranteed.

- (pt) mr president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, the framework directive we are considering today at second reading is a kind of constitutional charter for water at the level of the european union. this directive is both an outcome and a starting point. this is a result of the fact that it was prepared by a series of directives, which are more fragmented and dispersed, and it gives the water legislation an overall logic and systemic coherence. a starting point, because it sets up a new reference framework from which future european water legislation will be derived. this framework directive is rightly ambitious and realistic, deliberate and thoughtful, and it succeeds to a large extent. the amendments proposed by the committee, thanks to the excellent work of mrs lienemann, are intended to strengthen the text of the directive and make it more rigorous. the commission's proposal is to make the european union more rigid and to ignore the climatic and geographical differences which are so evident in europe. the rapporteur's proposal deserves my support, but let me express both praise and concern. first, a word of praise: i think the directive is undeniably a step forward in providing for the obligation to manage water bodies in an integrated manner and in allowing water to be transferred between water bodies only when it is absolutely necessary. this is a healthy and educational principle for the iberian peninsula, for example. the first concern is the timetable in the directive, which can be a binding framework, but which also represents, in my view, an exciting challenge. the second concern is self-evident: it concerns the difficulty of determining water costs and pricing, particularly in terms of the integration of environmental costs, which is causing concern, especially among farmers. paying the right price for water can also lead to injustice. this framework directive also contains an important promise: that by the year 2020, european waters will have been restored to a state identical to their natural state, free of pollution and dangerous substances. what do you mean , utopia ? back to the pre-industrial society ? i don't think he is. i call for confidence and optimism. the recent danube disaster shows, like a nightmare, that the long-term policy and strategy of ecological protection of water, however costly, is not a luxury for the rich, but an imperative for civilization.

the commission and the council should know that we will not give up our intention to give life the budget it deserves and which best guarantees the environmental results that such an instrument is intended to achieve. we justify our position on the basis of four very simple arguments. firstly, life is the only direct financial instrument for the promotion of the environment in the european union. there is no other. secondly, life is an instrument that has produced good results, has enabled the development of innovative methods and techniques and has an excellent rate of implementation. thirdly, the life budget has decreased in real terms and has not kept pace with the dynamism and creativity of demand. fourthly, in view of the credit it gives to life, the european parliament has allocated increasing amounts to this programme in its annual budget. the adoption of the proposal advocated by the commission and the council would therefore lead to an unacceptable reversal of this trend. however, our concern is not just about the budgetary issue. climate change and water policy are issues of great concern to european citizens and have received considerable attention from the european parliament. that is why we think it is essential to approve our amendments nos 5 and 6, which aim to establish sustainable management of groundwater and surface water and the reduction of greenhouse gases as life environmental objectives. a final word to congratulate mrs lienemann on her report and to say that we will vote in favour of her amendments on comitology, because, if we prefer, as was clearly stated in the committee on the environment, that the monitoring of life be carried out by an advisory committee, we do not wish to contribute to the adoption of the council's solution, which is decidedly less good.

- (pt) mr president, madam commissioner, this is one of the social areas to which the european union must pay the greatest attention. in fact, macroeconomic policies, the stability pact and the nominal convergence criteria have acted as obstacles to a modernisation of social protection which could contribute to improving the quality of life of the population, in particular through a significant increase in pensions - particularly the minimum pensions - and the fight against social exclusion. this is particularly serious in countries such as portugal, where the minimum pension is just over half the national minimum wage, which is the lowest in the european union. so as long as the commission does not recognise the need to change macroeconomic policies to create more quality jobs and guarantee the rights of those who hold them, which is the fundamental basis for maintaining and modernising a sound public social protection system, we will remain extremely cautious about its position. although the anderson report incorporated positive proposals, including some of those we had put forward, it still contains some of the bases we had criticised in the commission's original proposal and the strategy for modernising social protection continues to be a matter of concern to us.
- (pt) mr president, i think that the present debate is particularly appropriate, not only because of the importance of the subject of development in general, but also because of its timeliness. seattle was a failure and north-south relations were at the forefront of it for several reasons. the meeting in that north american city highlighted the fact that globalisation has profoundly unequal and harmful effects on the poorest countries. at this very moment, and under the obvious influence of this failure, the 10th united nations conference on trade and development is being held in bangkok. however, as has been said, the negotiations which define the future of the lomé convention have been completed. this shows how timely this issue is. we could also mention the next summit between the european union and the oau, which will take place in cairo in april. it must also be said that even a superficial analysis inevitably leads to the conclusion that something, or many things, are wrong with north-south relations, including between the european union and the less developed countries. it is sufficient to note that the category of the so-called least developed countries now includes 48 countries, whereas there were 25 thirty years ago, and these countries represent 13% of humanity, but account for only 0.4% of world exports and 0.6% of world imports. it is also unfortunate that official development aid from the rich countries has fallen by some 23% since 1990. in addition, the recent cuts in the community budget for this sector for the current financial year are part of this trend and are a bad political signal. mr michel camdessus, who is still the head of the international monetary fund and above all suspicion, said in his speech last sunday at the beginning of the work of unctad, i quote, that the international community gives with one hand what it takes from the other. he was, of course, referring to the aid given by the developed countries to the less developed countries. this may seem ironic given the author of this statement, yet he did make it, i heard it with my own ears and it does not seem to me to be far removed from the reality of the present day. this leads me to say that it is essential to rethink and relaunch north-south relations on new models, including the need to rethink the role of the european union in this context, being certain that the promotion of a new, fairer and more equitable world order cannot and must not cease to be a strategic objective of the union, it is therefore essential to reflect on the globalisation that is taking place taking into account the underlying key orientations and dominant interests and, in this context in particular, the important harmful effects that result for the less developed countries. it is a fact that a new agreement has been signed with the acp countries. this is a positive development in itself, especially when one considers the pressure to terminate the agreement and the various member states opposed to its renewal. we shall have the opportunity in due course to analyse in greater depth the exact terms of the agreement. in the meantime, it is certain that the european negotiators have been timid and have fallen short of the proposals we made in good time, even though the new agreement contains new and innovative elements. some important political features of the new partnership agreement have contributed to this. it is probably the result of a complex negotiation in which both sides made substantial concessions, which is true for the european union in particular in terms of good governance and trade. but above all it is the result of a strengthening of the acp group, which has clearly found a new soul after seattle. i would like to stress that a new way of approaching these issues seems to me to be emerging, particularly in the field of trade. we need to have an approach to this. finally, we must go further in policies to support the development of developing countries. at the financial and budgetary level, in terms of debt, support for sensitive sectors such as food security and humanitarian aid or education and health, as well as in the fields of the environment, investment or access to information and new technologies, such as trade, while ensuring full coherence between development policies and other community policies and between them and those implemented by the member states. we cannot simply pursue something which has proved to be inadequate and wrong in the past.
- (pt) mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i think this initiative is a relevant one and a very appropriate one. as many colleagues have already said before me, we often find ourselves in situations where there is a great deal of contradiction between what we are trying to achieve with development and cooperation policy on the one hand and what we are doing away with with other sectoral policies whose interests are opposed in terms of the development of these same countries on the other. we are not just talking about the concrete impact of our common policies on tourism, the environment, agriculture, fisheries and industry. we are talking in general, and this is more serious, about the contradictions between our economic and trade policies on the one hand and our defence policies on the other. finally, there is a set of contradictory situations. in my view, the contradictions are not so serious in terms of community policies; the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat fraud in the community. we must be aware of that. on the one hand we often adopt resolutions on peace processes in other parts of the world, as we have done quite recently in the case of indonesia, a case that raises eyebrows, and then we find, on the other hand, that certain european countries were supplying that country with weapons and war material. these are, in my view, the most serious contradictions in this area, more serious than the contradictions of a certain incompatibility of sectoral policies, which of course exists. it is obvious that there are always contradictions between the different policies, even in a given country. it's not unusual. at the level of the european union, we must also be aware that there will be contradictions between the various common policies, in this case in development policy. we must be aware that these contradictions exist and that they must be minimized, and this objective of minimizing contradictions is a just and wise objective, which is the basis for the presentation of this resolution. i believe that the idea of creating an observatory of the evolution of this coherence between community policies and cooperation and development policy is sufficiently wise and relevant; the commission's proposal is to be accompanied by a report on the implementation of the community's environmental policy. the observatory will be required to draw up an annual report on the evolution and impact of these policies and their combination. i have left for the end a question which i consider to be fundamental and which must be recorded and approved by this parliament. this new objective of minimising the contradictions between development cooperation policy and other policies must be a valid objective not only within the european union but also at the global level, given the increasing globalisation of the economy. if we want fair world trade, we must impose the same principle at the next round of multilateral negotiations in the world trade organisation, because it would be contradictory for the european union to impose on itself an iron discipline, but fair, while the united states and other major powers would then do the opposite and approve of what we are not doing. it is therefore right and fundamental that these principles be established at the next round of the world trade organisation.

in writing. - (pt) after the bse and dioxin scandals, food safety issues are in the spotlight. there are still many uncertainties about gmos in terms of their impact on the environment, human and animal health. the precautionary principle should therefore be applied where there is no scientific certainty. the use of biotechnology must be studied in greater depth and not be already "packaged" for sale on behalf of the profits of certain multinational companies in this sector. the benefits are precisely what are at the origin of the current food security crises. the rapporteur is in this context, by legally framing the question of gmos with animal feed and by recalling that they do not present 'any danger to human health and the environment'. the report also considers that 'genetically modified additives should be clearly defined as such on each label or other accompanying administrative document'. this will allow for greater control and traceability of these elements and will guarantee freedom of choice for the consumer. after the montreal agreement on genetically modified organisms (gmos), i think it is positive that the rapporteur has included this issue in his report; that is why i voted for his report.

in writing. - (pt) we believe that it is positive to seek to reverse the alarming trends in water quality degradation, which governments are increasingly neglecting. we all know that water is a vital asset for humanity and that it is urgent to defend its quality. but it is equally essential to take account of the inseparable nature of the components of water policy, action on this point conditioning all the others. the debate and the proposals have focused on the physical, chemical and ecological quality of water, as if the adverse effects on other dimensions were only incidentally noticed. it is therefore necessary to take account of the diversity of the member states' situations, the specificities of the climate, the hydrological regime and its variations throughout the year, and the social, economic and ecological effects of the application of the standards or even of their absence. it is the concerns surrounding these issues that have led us to diverge from some of the proposals of the committee on the environment, public health and consumer policy, especially those which refer to a rather restrictive timetable and to little attention to the social and agricultural aspects, which are very important for a country like portugal, which is experiencing problems of water scarcity and a lack of socioeconomic development. but we also believe that the commission and council proposals are far behind in many cases, which is why we want a balanced vote on the elements that are the conditions for the proposals that have been put forward.

- (pt) mr president, ladies and gentlemen, it was not until the end of the kosovo conflict that the conclusion was reached that, after several wars in the territory of the former yugoslavia, with their accompanying adverse effects on the stability of the balkan region, it was necessary to implement a comprehensive and lasting strategy to guarantee peace and stability in south-east europe. this strategy, which was embodied in the stability pact adopted on 10 june 1999, considers the region of south-east europe as a single entity, but at the same time recognises the diversity and uneven development of the individual countries that make up it. the european union's contribution to the stability pact, as reflected in the so-called 'stabilisation and association process', concerns the development of the regional approach that the ec developed in 1996 for the five countries of the region, namely bosnia and herzegovina, croatia, the federal republic of yugoslavia, the former yugoslav republic of macedonia and albania, and whose most significant aspect is the establishment of political and economic measures to support the dayton peace agreements, which should thus contribute to the stability of the region. therefore, the establishment of a completely new basis for relations between the european union and the former yugoslav republic of macedonia, and the opening of the country to the prospect of full integration into the structures of the european union through a mechanism provided for in the stabilisation and association process, is an important contribution to the stability of the balkan region. but this integration can only be achieved if the countries are willing to accept a number of conditions. the expectation is that we are sending these countries politically sound signals that they can one day be part of our union, while retaining their sovereignty. without any illusions, the mandate we are giving the commission today can be identified, once the agreement has been reached, as the first major step towards peace and stability in this tortured region, whose peoples have already shown that they can legitimately claim to be part of our area of freedom and development. they have already suffered enough under the soviet yoke and then under the communist bloc. as far as we know, the former yugoslav republic of macedonia is entitled, from an economic and political point of view, to establish new contractual relations with the european union, well beyond the cooperation agreement in force since 1 june 1998, which in practice aims at respecting community legislation, especially in key areas of the internal market. the former yugoslav republic will certainly still have considerable work to do, but judging from the political and economic reforms it has already undertaken under the cooperation agreement, as well as its willingness to accept transitional periods in various fields, it will certainly be able to fulfil the conditions required for a stabilisation and association agreement. based on the thesis that development is a factor of stability in the region, defining the negotiating mandate in the terms proposed to us and highlighting the fact that this is a bilateral agreement with the european union, we share the rapporteur's view when he stresses that this is the first practical application of a long-term integration strategy for south-east europe, and that the question is what kind of structure will be adopted in the long term to guarantee peace and stability in the balkans. in this context, the various references to regional cooperation with neighbouring countries are indeed politically important. this agreement can and must serve as an example for other countries in the region. and that's why it's important. this process must therefore be underpinned by a genuine political will to bring the european union closer to these countries. we are well aware that some countries are in the process of democratization, and that others have yet to assert the democratic character of their respective states; the european union is not only a political force, but also a political force, and it is a political force, and it is a political force, and it is a political force.

- the next item is the report (a5-0020/2000) by mr cederschiöld, on behalf of the committee on civil liberties, justice and home affairs, on the draft council framework decision to strengthen the criminal framework for protection against counterfeiting with a view to the introduction of the euro (5116/99 - c5-0332/99 - 1999/0821 (cns)).
- (pt) mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i should first like to congratulate the rapporteur, mrs charlotte cederschiöld, on the preparation of this report, which is an excellent starting point for strengthening criminal protection against counterfeiting of money in the run-up to the introduction of the euro. the euro will be in circulation from 1 january 2002 and will become one of the world's most important reserve currencies. given its global reach, the euro will be particularly exposed to the risk of counterfeiting and forgery. the draft decision thus lays down a minimum set of standards to harmonise criminal law in this area and to make its application simpler and more effective for each member state. this decision, which provides for protection of the euro even before it is put into circulation, fills a gap in the legislation of some member states. i want to expressly support the rapporteur when she considers the need to extend this protection to national currencies which can still be exchanged after they have been withdrawn from circulation. it will therefore be for each member state to provide for effective, adequate and dissuasive criminal sanctions for acts which are considered to be criminal offences in the draft framework decision. as for the proposal to set a minimum sentence of eight years, i do not think it will solve all the problems in my country, although our penal code, which provides for a maximum prison sentence of five years for the offence of counterfeiting currency, already reflects an openness to the prosecution of counterfeiting. i would also like to invite those member states which have reservations in this respect to take all the necessary steps to reach a decision in the short term, as the author of this report has indicated in his explanatory statement. mr president, this draft framework decision is the first time that a common criminal proposal has been put forward setting out rules to be applied simultaneously in the 15 member states. in this way we are making progress towards the realisation of the eu of freedom, security and justice which was discussed in tampere.

in writing. - (pt) we regret that the first draft of the report presented by the rapporteur was rejected by the commission, as it contained positive aspects, particularly when it referred to the need to increase public investment and job creation in order to achieve a 75% employment level. we therefore voted against the report in its current form as our proposals were rejected, among which i would highlight: - the importance of public investment for sustained economic growth, based on economic recovery, through the promotion of demand and the concentration of public investment in areas with positive externalities for the economy as a whole, such as the creation and maintenance of infrastructure, urban renewal, vocational training, education and health; - the recovery of demand in the community, through greater public investment and wage growth that takes into account growth and productivity, in order to foster greater economic growth, which is essential to achieving the objective of full employment; - the centralisation of support for these latter mechanisms, in order to strengthen the level of competition of smes, by providing various legal, information and support services, such as credit, information and support for associations, and by introducing mechanisms to provide support for smes, in order to improve the level of competitiveness of smes.
in writing. - (pt) we agree that fishing is one of the activities most interested in promoting respect for the marine environment. but there are many other activities that contribute to the degradation of this marine environment: pollution from oil tankers and other means of maritime transport, industrial activity, the pressure of human activity on the coasts and, of course, uncontrolled fishing. it is interesting to note, however, that small-scale coastal fishing shows that it is possible to combine fishing activity with sustainable development that is not harmful to the marine environment. we believe that it is possible to reconcile a fisheries policy based on the conservation of the marine environment and the interests of fishing communities. but this requires taking into account the fact that there is a correlation between the yield from fishing and the increase in fishing effort. measures must therefore be taken under the common fisheries policy and adequate financial resources allocated to ensure that the return depends exclusively on fishing activity, more specifically during periods of paralysis or reduction of fishing activity. this is why the proposals we are putting forward are important.
