- (pt) madam president, ladies and gentlemen, let us go back to the beginning. why is the european union different? throughout the centuries, nations have tried to dominate one another. ideas have varied with the times and interests, but the system has remained the same. power to some, submission to others. we have seen centuries of war, destruction and ruin over and over again. often we had to start all over again. the european project was born out of the last war. what makes europe different? all of it. it's a system based on the mutual freedom of all, rather than the domination of a few. this is a framework agreed between nations, not a framework dictated to nations. this is what it is: a permanent contract between free and sovereign states, equal among equals. it's not about changing majorities, it's about a contract because we're all minorities. this is the secret of the unprecedented peace and progress we have enjoyed: on the one hand, the common culture of human rights, democracy, the rule of law, the market; on the other, the common culture of democracy, the rule of law, the market; on the other hand, the common culture of democracy, the rule of law, the market. on the other hand, strict mutual respect, never to offend the different states. what instruments? the commission's proposal is to be accompanied by a formal rule, unanimity or at least broad convergence, and a substantive rule, of concrete policies for rapprochement and the promotion of economic and social cohesion. at what cost ? patience, a small price to pay for such great results! we must be patient with each other, we must give time to time for europe to move forward; we must not give in to the old temptation to want to impose ourselves on others, to break the contact between equals. it never led to union. to cherish the idea of european unity is to see the reality of the facts, not to force a pseudo-enlightened imagination. we can't tear up the rules of the game that got us here. in my view, the resolution is a dangerous anti-european drift, it completely upsets the balance. for my country, portugal, it would be a disaster, as it would be for other smaller and mediumsized countries. for europe in general, it would be crisis or breakdown. there can be no europe in which a few command the many. there can be no europe that is angry but does not listen. there can be no europe which aims to replace a treaty between states by a constitution imposed on the states. we want a europe of space, not a europe of corsets. a final word on the transnational idea of european parties. i am a christian democrat and my party, the cds-pp, is one of the founders of the epp. a few years ago we were expelled from the epp because we were in favour of a referendum on maastricht. today it is former austrian comrades of the christian democratic union who are in trouble because of the election results in their country. what a terrible idea of democracy! federalism is so blind that it will eventually destroy by its own hands what it says with its own mouth. why would you do that ? because he covets power and forgets partnership. we cannot admit that.
- (pt) madam president, enlargement and the strategic, political and economic consequences for the european union which it entails can no longer be excluded from public debate or considered exclusively from a national point of view. the debate on the presentation of the european parliament's position on the intergovernmental conference is therefore a fundamental debate for understanding this new phase of european construction, which is both risky and common. we are pleased to note that in this contribution the european parliament emphasizes the democratization, efficiency and increased control of the european institutions as the main lines of strength and guiding principles, precisely in the areas where the deficits were most pronounced, where the risks of disintegration were greatest, given the significant increase in the number of member states. most of these proposals are intended to make viable and strengthen common progress and the process of integration in this new complex framework, and therefore they deserve to be welcomed. but other proposals will have to be based on a fairer balance between the member states and more in line with the representation and legitimacy of the respective bodies, i am specifically referring to the european lists for elections to the european parliament, the rule for qualified majority decisions and enhanced cooperation, for example. fortunately, we can improve on these points in the plenary tomorrow. today, when countries which have been subjected to forced and authoritarian integration for years are knocking at our door, attracted by the success of our common experience, we must welcome them into a house that is neat and in good working order, of course, but without any doubt about the participation of all in the search for the best solutions for all and about the primacy of the values of justice, democracy and solidarity. i would like to warmly congratulate the rapporteurs on their work.
- (pt) mr president-in-office of the council, commissioner barnier, i will begin my speech with the same question as that put by my colleague, mr carlos carnero gonzález. i wonder why i, a convinced european, differ in this debate from many other convinced europeans and, in particular, from the rapporteurs. the reason is that the foundation of the european project, in its dual legitimacy between a union of peoples and a union of states, is not reflected in my view in the final outcome of the proposals in this report. that is why, at a time when the european parliament should be able to reach a consensus which could serve as a political reference for the intergovernmental conference, our position is sad. we hope that certain amendments which fundamentally guarantee the balance between small and large states, namely a balance between the dual legitimacy of a union of peoples and a union of states, will be voted on tomorrow. we hope therefore to have every reason to vote tomorrow for the adoption by this house of a small number of amendments - six or seven - which are essential if we are to look at this question not from the point of view of national egoism but from the point of view of defending the coherence and the value of solidarity of the european project. my second point concerns you, mr president-in-office. we were somewhat puzzled to hear minister gama say, in his opening speech, what the current state of play was in the work of the intergovernmental conference. we know the determination of the portuguese presidency and i would like to conclude by encouraging it to continue, to find mechanisms, so that the member states understand how fundamental it is that more questions are decided by qualified majority and that there is greater flexibility in the decisions of the european union. but i would also like to encourage you, mr president, to extend the igc agenda to include issues that are fundamental to us, such as the charter of fundamental rights. we hope that this charter will be included in the treaty, so that it not only makes an important contribution to the future of the european union, but also brings us closer to the citizens at a politically important time like this, which requires a further reaffirmation of a community of values enshrined in my view through this charter of fundamental rights. good luck with that, mr. president.
- (pt) mr president, the dramatic intensity of the situation in the horn of africa and especially in ethiopia is at a level rarely seen in the past, which requires the attention and assistance of the international community, particularly the european union, in order to stem an unprecedented human catastrophe. it is true that something has already been done, i am thinking of the food aid that the european union is providing and which has just been mentioned by commissioner christopher patten. but we can't stop there. we must overcome all the difficulties on the ground in order to get the food and medicine needed by the starving and dying people and children as quickly as possible. to do this, it is essential to urge the governments of the countries in the region, in view of their political and military situation, to allow and ensure the rapid delivery of aid and access for international organizations to the areas where these populations are concentrated, and to ensure that this aid reaches its recipients. it is also essential that international organizations, the world health organization, unicef and fao in particular, act in a coordinated manner and with the speed and efficiency that the situation requires in delivering food and health aid to its destination. but these international organizations must also take measures to stimulate the primary sector, which is crucial for the model economy, in order to guarantee a sustainable solution in the medium term, by sending technicians and specialists to carry out projects in this field. the gravity of the situation and the urgent need to give priority to solidarity with these populations leads me to ask the commission, and especially commissioners christopher patten and poul nielson, to keep us regularly and fully informed of developments in the situation and, above all, of the measures they intend to take or have taken to alleviate the gravity of the situation.
the rapporteur. - (pt) mr president, commissioner viviane reding, ladies and gentlemen, i come from a country whose history over the last ten centuries has been closely linked to the knowledge of foreign languages spoken in the most diverse parts of the world. the portuguese have always had to learn the language of the peoples with whom they have interacted and have, in turn, carried a european language, portuguese, to the four corners of the world, where it is now spoken by two hundred million people. the importance of an initiative such as the european year of languages 2001 is therefore obvious to me, at a time when europe is facing a new dimension in its history and we are all fully aware of the extraordinary richness of the cultural and linguistic diversity of the peoples of europe and we have the extensive conceptual work carried out by the council of europe, and even by this parliament, which leads to the inclusion of the mother tongue as one of the fundamental human rights. it is in this context, where history and civilization, the dynamics of cultural processes and the development of law, the urgency of the present and the necessary vision of the future come together, that the proposal for language learning that is presented to all citizens of the european union takes on its full importance. the programme for the european year of languages, a campaign which involves a bold challenge, is based on the essential nature of the oral and written heritage which has always been produced in all the languages spoken in europe; considers language skills to be an intrinsic element in the construction of european citizenship, as they enable better mutual knowledge, stimulate tolerance and conviviality and are thus an important tool in the fight against racism, xenophobia, anti-semitism and other unacceptable forms of discrimination; finally, it takes account of the economic dimension, since the knowledge of other languages constitutes a set of professional qualifications, facilitates access to scientific knowledge and can be a weapon in the fight against unemployment. the various amendments to the original text tabled by the members of this house did not merely reflect these concerns but a genuine policy in this area which, without infringing the principle of subsidiarity, would guarantee the quality and effectiveness of the campaign to promote the learning of foreign languages and the diversity of the target audience, with emphasis on the most disadvantaged groups. the method used in drawing up the report which i have had the honour of signing and which will be submitted to this parliament for approval tomorrow has consisted in systematically, in a completely informal way and through intensive and varied contacts with the coordinators of the political groups of the committee on culture, youth, education, the media and sport, with representatives of the portuguese presidency and with members of the commission, seeking to define the boundaries between what would be acceptable to the various institutions concerned and what would not be. this method has led to what can be described as a compromise text, since it seems to make any second reading and any conciliation unnecessary, i.e. a solution which allows the procedure to be concluded at first reading and the measures aimed at preparing the european year of languages to be started as early as the second half of this year, so that the programme can enter into force at the beginning of 2001. i think that the final difficulties will be overcome by the statement we all expect from commissioner viviane reding. this does not mean, mr president, madam commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, that we are living in the best of all possible worlds. we consider that the budgetary resources are clearly insufficient, we believe that there are actions ahead of the programme which will have to be calibrated and developed, and we also believe that a whole series of aspects have been neglected, because they are no longer part of a learning campaign but concern the status of european languages which, for historical or political reasons, are less widely spoken or more disadvantaged in certain member states. these aspects urgently require the attention of the community institutions, but they do not in any way call into question the fact that the compromise text has enormous potential for good results, because it corresponds to a real desire and need of the peoples of the union and already covers, directly or indirectly, a significant part of the main principles to be adopted in this field. for all these reasons, and in gratitude to my colleagues, the portuguese presidency and the commission for their cooperation, i have the honour to ask for the adoption of the proposal for a joint decision on the european year of languages, as contained in the compromise text whose outline i have just presented.
- (pt) mr president, it is important that parliament approves the report by mr michel dary, since the european commission's proposal to introduce an exclusively tariff regime for banana imports until 2006 is unacceptable. this single tariff system advocated by the commission would cause the bankruptcy of producers in the outermost regions of the european union, such as madeira and the acp countries. in addition, the failure of the last wto conference has demonstrated that the organisation is not able to meet the expectations and requirements of public opinion and consumers in certain areas such as product quality, environmental protection, social conditions and the protection of workers, as the report also states. if we want to implement a policy that responds to the declarations of support for the acp countries and the development of the outermost regions of the european union, we cannot allow the agriculture of these regions and their development, where banana production and export are of great importance, to be crushed by hordes of dollar bananas from the large plantations that the multinationals in the sector own in latin america. and it is in this light that we support all the proposals contained in this report, to which we also subscribe, in particular by defending a transitional period which cannot be less than ten years and which cannot involve a customs-only regime, with the commission having to assess the impact of the new situation on prices and on the yields of banana producers in these regions so that compensatory aid is corrected, always having as a principle the guarantee of the producers' yields.
- (pt) mr president, i will begin by asking the commission a few questions, particularly to commissioner fischler. why is it that we are advocating the outright liberalisation of banana trade from 2006 onwards, while for meat, milk and cereals, which account for 86% of the eaggf budget, we are invoking, as the commission did in seattle, the specific nature of european agriculture? is this not an unacceptable duality of criteria? does the commission not have a double standard? is the banana the poor relative of agricultural production in the european union? should not the european agricultural model also serve to safeguard the european union's banana production, which would be seriously threatened by the proposed liberalisation? will trade in meat, milk and cereals also be liberalized? will the commission give in to the more radical positions of the united states as it increases substantially its agricultural subsidies? does it not take into account that bananas are the main agricultural product of four outermost regions which are among the ten most disadvantaged regions in the european union? in this proposal for an amendment to the banana cmo regulation, what is the advantage of article 299 (2) of the treaties, which provides for specific treatment for the outermost regions? the multifunctional aspect of european agriculture, which is an essential concept of the european agricultural model in question, must be present in this reform, because in madeira, the region i represent here, the environmental protection function of agriculture is just as important as its economic function. the end of banana cultivation on the island of madeira would not only lead to a considerable reduction in yield for thousands of families, but would also cause a huge environmental disaster. the seattle conference also showed that the new round of world trade organisation negotiations must be a genuine development round. it is therefore justified and entirely legitimate to give priority to bananas produced in the european union and the acp countries in the context of the common organisation of the market in bananas. and it is only the maintenance of import quotas that serves this purpose, which is perfectly compatible with the rules of the world trade organisation.
- (pt) mr president, mr fischler, banana production is of particular importance in certain less developed countries, such as the acp countries, in island regions such as crete, and especially in the outermost regions such as madeira, the canary islands, martinique and guadeloupe. these are not the fully developed regions of central europe with wide alternatives to changing market conditions, where the common agricultural policy invests most of its resources. it is therefore not possible to accept a radical change in the community's trade policy in this area without taking seriously into account the regional impacts this implies and without taking the necessary measures in the field of economic and social cohesion. the lisbon council instructed the commission to present as soon as possible the concrete measures to implement the commission's report, and more specifically article 299 (2) of the treaty on the outermost regions. agriculture is an absolutely vital sector in many of these regions and they expect the commission to act swiftly and decisively to safeguard their development.
in writing. - (pt) the compromise of the portuguese socialists with the european project is one of its permanent objectives and one of its founding principles. today more than ever, the construction of europe is essential not only for peace and progress in europe but also in the rest of the world. the european project is an evolving process which has adapted to reality and responded to the vicissitudes of history. today we are facing one of those unique moments when the european union needs to rethink and adapt its policies and institutions to better meet the challenges of enlargement and the effective and democratic functioning of its institutions. the defence of a progressive political union, pursuing the federalist logic which is in full swing, the strengthening of european citizenship, economic and social cohesion and the cultural diversity of its peoples are essential and inseparable elements of our choices. the institutional implications of enlargement cannot prejudice the fundamental balance - which the treaties successfully envisage - between the dual legitimacy of the union of peoples and the union of states which underlies the european project and which has contributed decisively to shaping its institutions. the deepening of european integration towards political union at world level must satisfactorily preserve the basic balance which has ensured the success of the european project, which brings together states so different in terms of their demographic and territorial characteristics. enlargement can in no way be used as a pretext for introducing institutional changes which, by unnecessarily jeopardising the balances which have been skillfully constructed since 1957, have no direct logical or political relation to that balance. it is in the spirit of fidelity to these principles and convictions that we appreciate and vote in favour of the european parliament's proposals for the intergovernmental conference (igc) to revise the treaties. and it is also out of loyalty to these principles and convictions that we have been obliged to vote against the dimitrakopoulos/leinen report, which in our view attempts to meet the challenges of enlargement with solutions which advocate a significant imbalance between the dual legitimacy of the union of peoples and the unity of states. - as regards the proposed solution concerning respect for the composition of the european parliament, certain principles are not adequately guaranteed, and in our view they are fundamental and consist in the need to eliminate the quantification of a minimum number of members of the european parliament for each member state and the introduction of a principle of degressivity which can correct the negative effects resulting from the application of the strict proportionality rule. - we also think that the points in the report which refer to the proposed composition of the commission are not entirely explicit. the adoption of a criterion which places all member states on an equal footing, whether by assigning one commissioner per member state or by operating from a college of twenty commissioners, does not seem to us to be fully expressed and guaranteed in the proposal for the report. the same could be said of the idea of a simple rotation principle and the definition of the contours of operation, which in our view should be fully established if we opt for the assumption of a commission composed of only twenty commissioners. - as regards the question of the approval of council decisions on the rule of qualified majority, we consider that the proposed rule is not, in fact, the most acceptable. in fact, although we agree with the double majority criterion, it still seems appropriate to us to opt for a solution based on the need for two-thirds of the votes of the member states and a simple majority of the most populous states for a council deliberation to take place, once greater cohesion in the decision-making process is thereby guaranteed. - as regards the principle of rotation of the council presidency, we cannot accept the new rule proposed either, since it consists in a distribution of the presidency according to the various compositions that this institution can assume, so that each member state simultaneously presides over only one of them, there must also be a rotation for a minimum period of two years. in our view, this rule does not guarantee the necessary balance and equality in the representation of each member state in the council. - the question of the election of 10% of the members of the european parliament by means of european lists is not sufficiently clear either. in fact, the wording used in the report to describe such a possibility is, in our opinion, rather vague, because it does not offer any guarantees that can be used to create, as we wish, a genuine european public opinion. the european list is a good idea, especially if people who represent a real reference to european sentiment can be included. we also believe that it merits further reflection, so that the legitimate fears expressed here can be eliminated. - we cannot accept the rule of appointments based on decisions taken by qualified majority. until now, all appointment decisions in the european union have been taken unanimously, and there have never been any absences or crises of appointments. we believe that the rule has the merit of ensuring a greater balance and avoiding possible conflicts between the large and small countries of the european union. but we do not want to confuse the meaning of our vote with that of those who have a selfish and limited vision of the national interest. on the contrary, we want a politically strong european union, capable of sharing sovereignty and of contributing ever more to social progress and the well-being of its citizens. the portuguese socialists will continue to be available, side by side with all the political forces in favour of the enlargement and deepening of our political union, in order to find the best formulas for continuing this political struggle which is ours. this declaration was submitted immediately for regulatory reasons, but certain points remain subject to detailed knowledge of the changes made. in addition, because of the speed with which we voted, we were not always able to understand what we were going to vote on (in particular, there was some confusion between the votes on amendments and on paragraphs), which is why the portuguese socialist members reserve the right to make any corrections to the direction of their vote after analysing the results of the vote.
in writing. - (pt) once again the european parliament is being led to adopt a maximalist position, devoid of any realism in the field of institutions. it opted, as usual, for an unhindered federalisation solution. the same is true of proposals to eliminate almost completely the rule of unanimity, to extend the codecision system, to institutionalise european political parties, to create a european electoral circle, to integrate the weu, to strengthen europol, or, more significantly and generally, to constitutionalise the treaties. these are options from which we have undoubtedly distanced ourselves and from which we continue to distance ourselves. in addition, the new guidelines proposed in this report are inadmissible. the argument that institutional adjustment is necessary to cope with the prospect of enlargement is used to suggest in fact an immeasurable increase in the decision-making power - at different levels - of the large countries and an intolerable reduction in that of the small and medium-sized countries, by suggesting and implicitly encouraging the creation of political directories, which are totally inadmissible, even with the encouragement of 'enhanced cooperation' schemes. of course, we oppose such choices. in the meantime, and at the same time, by approving such a report, the european parliament is indifferent to obvious and truly urgent situations, such as the need to overcome, or at least to mitigate, the profound shortcomings in democracy; to change the monetarist orientations - more specifically the stability pact - which is at the root of current social ills such as unemployment and precarious employment; to change the statute of the european central bank in order to guarantee greater transparency, control and limits on their respective powers; the european council in brussels on 14 and 15 december adopted a resolution on the situation in the former yugoslavia. parliament would be doing itself a great disservice if it were to take these paths. this institution will only distance itself from the peoples it claims to represent if it opts for the solutions proposed in the dimitrakopoulos-leinen report. for all these reasons we shall vote against this report.

in writing. - (pt) the report supports the financial markets action plan to liberalise capital markets proposed by the commission. it is also in line with the lisbon summit, which calls for acceleration of this and other liberalisations. now it is true that this process increases financial instability and encourages the concentration of capital markets and financial institutions, which is why it is sometimes contrary to the commission's position, sometimes to that of the rapporteur. however, concrete measures should be introduced to combat the volatility of capital markets and to control capital movements in the european union, in particular those of a speculative nature, and also to minimise the risks of financial crises; we have tried, through an amendment proposal, to encourage the commission to include in its action plan concrete measures to achieve this objective, more specifically by presenting a report on the institutionalisation of a tax on capital movements, especially of a speculative nature. we regret that our position was not accepted.
in writing. - (pt) we voted against this report because we believe that its content is not so far removed from the commission's proposal to subject social security to the financial markets, which would weaken it or replace public pension schemes with stock market logic, thus clarifying the objectives of the so-called 'modernisation of social protection' decided at the lisbon summit. as we have stated in our proposals, the creation of an internal market for supplementary pension schemes has not provided an adequate response to future demographic problems, as the necessary expansionary economic policies that contribute to the creation of sustainable jobs through the increase of the public contribution base are needed. only a public social security system, which is soundly funded and independent of commercial interests and financial profitability and based on solidarity between generations, is able to guarantee the right to a dignified pension and security in old age for workers who have paid contributions to public pension schemes.
- (pt) mr president, at the request of our colleague, mr arlindo cunha, i am here to state our position on the debate on school milk. the european union has been funding the free distribution of milk in primary and secondary schools for over 20 years. in recent years, this action has meant an average annual expenditure of almost €90 million corresponding to more than 300 million litres of milk, for the benefit of almost 10 million young people, i.e. almost 20% of the eligible school population. there are two main reasons for this action. the first is the social impact of the measure, which ensures a minimum of balanced nutrition for many children and young people who would not otherwise have access to it. it is mainly aimed at young people from the most disadvantaged social strata and poorer areas of rural and urban areas. the second is that, in a market for dairy products which is structurally surplus, this measure also encourages the disposal of the product in a much more useful and constructive way than through distribution, denaturing or export to third countries at much higher costs. it is regrettable that the european union does not provide for more such measures for other products, instead of eliminating them on international markets at subsidised costs which sometimes represent twice their price on these markets, which is why it is difficult to understand the alleged objective of budgetary savings. the united states of america, which has a reputation for having a more individualistic and less solidarity-based outlook, spends almost nine billion dollars a year on the distribution of food to school children, which is 100 times the european union's expenditure in this area. but it so happens that the european union is now proposing to reduce the funding to 100%, proposing 50% co-financing, which is particularly damaging to the less prosperous countries such as portugal. as a portuguese citizen, i would be shocked if such a measure were approved under the auspices of my country's presidency of the european union.

(parliament adopted the legislative resolution) we agree with this report because we believe it is important that millions of european schoolchildren continue to receive milk subsidized by the european union at 95%. as you know, the ec started subsidising milk distribution in schools in 1977. in 1999, the commission felt it was necessary to challenge this programme. however, in the face of the strong reaction, particularly from the european parliament, the commission has put forward a new proposal which maintains the programme while reducing community co-financing to 50% and which aims to have the balance of 50% financed by the member states, in whole or in part. to date, this scheme provides for community financing of 95% of the indicative milk price. in his report on behalf of the committee on agriculture and rural development, the rapporteur proposes to maintain this amount of funding and to reject the proposal for 50% funding, which is very important for us given our opposition to the renationalisation of cap costs.

the agenda calls for the report (a5-0075/2000) by mr sterckx, on behalf of the committee on regional policy, transport and tourism, on the communication from the commission to the council, the european parliament, the economic and social committee and the committee of the regions on the air transport industry in europe: from the single market to global challenges (com (99) 182 - c5-0110/1999 - 1999/2113 (cos)).
in writing. - (pt) the negotiation of these agreements, on the basis of the principles of the overall balance of mutual benefits and parallelism, is in the interest of the various parties involved. portugal is particularly interested in the agreement on the free movement of persons, since more than 100,000 portuguese people work in switzerland, including several thousand seasonal workers. switzerland applies stricter standards to third country nationals and with the entry into force of this agreement there will be a gradual adaptation of the swiss system to the principle of free movement of persons, building on the community acquis. the clause that the seven agreements will enter into force simultaneously and be applied in their entirety is therefore particularly important. the current situation of workers considered as seasonal workers is particularly serious. they are obliged to leave switzerland for three months each year, do not have the possibility of changing jobs or cantons and do not have the right to family reunification. other workers, who are considered as annual workers, also face many restrictions, since they cannot generally change jobs, professions or cantons and their right to family reunification is not recognised. the agreements approved today should gradually resolve these problems. it is to be hoped that, with the entry into force of the joint committee provided for in the agreement on the free movement of persons, progress will also be made on other issues relating to the social protection of immigrants and their families. that is why we are voting in favour.
in writing. - (pt) we can only agree with the report in question when it says that increasing competition has had a negative impact on social conditions and safety, that it has caused an increase in the number of routes on the basis of airspace congestion. we also believe that passenger air transport is important as a public service for peripheral and island regions, as is the need to improve air transport safety. but in the face of this analysis, and in a contradictory way, we do not agree to continue to focus on deepening the liberalization of this sector in all its aspects, as well as on the transfer of sovereignty of airspace. that is why we cannot agree to this report.
- (pt) mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i should first like to congratulate our rapporteur, mrs jeggle, on the work she has done, and to say that this price package is no longer as important as it was in the past, since the medium-term agricultural policy was fortunately presented and defined. and it is precisely for this reason that we have recently put together a reform of agenda 2000; - the next item is the recommendation for second reading (a5-0114/2000) on behalf of the committee on development and cooperation, on the common position of the council with a view to the adoption of a regulation of the european parliament and of the council on development cooperation with south africa (c5-01000/2000-1999/0070 (cod)) (rapporteur: mr martínez martínez).
- (pt) mr president, ladies and gentlemen, parliament must give its opinion on second reading on the regulation which has formed the legal basis for the general framework of cooperation between the european union and south africa. as you will no doubt recall, the council of ministers of the european union approved the general agreement on trade, development and cooperation in september last year on the basis of the kinnock report, and the trade component of the agreement came into force in january this year. one of the key components of the trade agreement relates to wines, and south africa has committed itself, under the agreement reached at the berlin summit in march 1999, to discontinue the use of certain community designations of origin, such as the names 'porto' or 'jerez', which are names used improperly by south africa, within 12 years. after a series of rocamboles, successive postponements and delaying maneuvers on the part of south african traders in accepting a regulatory text for this agreement, the two parties finally agreed that a new regulatory text for the wine agreement in question should be defined by the end of june of the current year and that this agreement should enter into force on 1 september of this year. however, i have been informed that south africans continue to raise problems with the drafting of a text that accurately translates the berlin agreement. and they try to make these wine issues dependent on the world trade organisation's trips file, a file which the european union considers does not sufficiently protect community designations of origin. it also appears to be a concerted position with other wine-producing countries in the new continents, since they were negotiating bilateral wine agreements with the european union and suddenly changed their minds and left the procedure open. in view of the above, i cannot therefore share the rapporteur's view when he asks parliament to approve this framework regulation as a matter of urgency, as a good faith gesture, without establishing any link with the trade part of the general agreement on cooperation, development and trade. and i disagree because so far the european union has shown nothing but tremendous good faith. the commission has also agreed to the principle of subsidiarity, which is the basis for the common position. it's time to say "enough is enough". cooperation presupposes a positive attitude of good faith and responsibility on the part of both cooperating parties. as this has not been the case, i cannot in good conscience support the rapporteur's position.
- (pt) mr president, the proposals in the report under discussion reflect - or should reflect - the desire of parliament to prepare itself to meet the challenges facing the european union. it could not be otherwise: we have been through a difficult period, tensions are constant, contradictions are obvious. it seems that everything and nothing is wanted at once, as is shown by the budgetary translation - or lack thereof - of the priorities of the european union. depending on our ability to exploit the transformative potential of tensions and to reduce contradictions, we will be able to distinguish between the resulting stagnation and decline of the project and the dynamism required for its realization. has the european parliament's powers not been substantially extended with the entry into force of the treaty of amsterdam and the ensuing extension of the codecision procedure to more policy areas, for example? so we need to give members more powers to carry out their mandate, provide more and better information, enable the political groups to provide better answers to the union's problems, and make the european parliament's services more efficient. does enlargement not imply significant changes in the composition and functioning of this institution? so it is necessary to prepare them, to anticipate the challenges and the response to them, not falling into the trap of waiting and seeing, because then we would be lagging behind in relation to events that we did not know how to foresee or condition. this principle applies to the european union in general. we are at a turning point. now that we've gotten to this point, nothing will ever be the same. but the situation will not remain as it is now. once economic and monetary union is achieved, the euro cannot be an end point, but rather a starting point which at a certain point provokes and requires further transformations. the launch of the enlargement process implies profound changes and justifies new ambitions. the need to link the economic and political poles is becoming ever more evident. our hearts are torn between deepening and dilution, solidarity and selfishness, decision and procrastination, coherence and contradiction, convergence and divergence. let us try to engage and to go in new directions by strengthening the influence of the essential principles, among which i distinguish subsidiarity and economic and social cohesion. i congratulate our colleague mr ferber on his commitment to the work he is doing.
- (pt) mr president, this year marks the beginning of the full functions of olaf. in this respect, it should be recalled that this is a decisive step in the protection of the union's financial interests, which was initiated by our parliament, and in particular by the committee on budgetary control and the rapporteur here, our colleague herbert bösch. with a new and strengthened staff structure, a new organisation and independence, all the conditions are now in place for olaf to devote itself to the fight against fraud in an area where objective and verifiable figures show a very serious situation. fraud, together with other irregularities in the traditional own resources reported by the member states, reached a volume of €538 million in 1998, an amount that is unparalleled in other community expenditure, either in absolute or relative terms. the court of auditors' special report no 9 of 1998 on the protection of the european union's financial interests in the field of vat in intra-community trade highlights an even more serious situation. according to the court of auditors, the gap between the revenue actually received and the theoretical amount is €70 billion. there is nothing comparable in these two basic areas of the community's own resources, unlike the main areas of expenditure, over which the commission exercises control and imposes a system of penalties. the commission, in its last two reports on administrative cooperation and on the collection and taxation processes, has identified a situation characterised by the maintenance of a vat collection system which was intended to be applied provisionally but is used definitively, by the increasing complexity of the system and by the widespread insufficiency of human and material resources for control in relation to the volume of transactions. although there is general agreement on the need to overcome the difficulties in the fight against fraud due to the restrictions of national legislation or the lack of a legal basis through community legislation, the proposals have not, to date, been taken up, and the anti-fraud subcommittee in the field of indirect taxation has found itself, in the commission's own words, "not able to make real progress in taking the necessary measures to strengthen the fight against fraud". and what is worse, the commission's reports on vat revenue have been scandalously ignored by the other institutions and have had no follow-up in the various member states. compliance with article 280 of the treaty, more specifically where it requires effective and equivalent protection of the community's financial interests, will remain a dead letter if the legal vacuum in the field of traditional own resources and vat is not overcome. the protection of the community's financial interests, and more especially that of the taxpayer, cannot continue to be ignored in indirect taxation.
in writing. - (pt) following the decisions taken in agenda 2000, and also taking into account the reforms of the common market organisations, the annual fixing of prices or institutional amounts is no longer limited to a limited number of sectors. however, the commission does not always act in the interests of farmers, particularly when prices are falling, and this is something we should oppose. in concrete terms, for 2000/01 the commission proposes the level of the increases for rice, while awaiting the next reform proposal, which should already have been advanced. as regards the monthly increases in cereals, it proposes a two-stage reduction, with a view to an equivalent reduction in the intervention price of 7.5%, which was decided under agenda 2000, which is unacceptable for some member states, and also because the berlin council committed itself to maintaining the monthly increases at their current level. the commission maintains the current price levels for sheepmeat and pigmeat. the level of sugar prices fixed for the last marketing year is maintained, and a reform proposal to abolish quotas is to be presented in the coming months, on the pretext of the compromises reached in the wto. a few positive proposals were put forward by the committee on agriculture, namely the almost exclusive refusal by the sugar and agriculture sectors to allocate €300 million to kosovo, as this funding will increase the negative pressure on farmers' yields, which is unacceptable.
- (pt) mr president, ladies and gentlemen, it is essential to ensure that all workers have good working conditions in order to guarantee their health and safety, which requires an arrangement of working time which respects daily and weekly rest periods, breaks, holidays and other aspects, and therefore the problem of all workers in the sectors which have been excluded from the scope of the 1993 directive, particularly those in transport, maritime activities and doctors in training, must be adequately resolved. it is also true that, despite a positive proposal from parliament to answer these questions, the council has maintained an unacceptable position which substantially reduces the original proposals of the european parliament, namely the extension of the time limit for transposition of the directive into national legislation from two to three years for all sectors except for doctors in training, for whom the time limit for transposition of the directive is increased from four to nine years, possibly to 12 years, and which furthermore calls into question the safety of patients examined by overworked doctors. that is why we are voting against this agreement, because we naturally want a better agreement.
- (pt) mr president, ladies and gentlemen, allow me to congratulate our rapporteur on the good results she has achieved and on the fact that she has placed this report in the context of climate change in general. from the rio conference to kyoto, the european union has always been the political leader of the climate change debate. however, although europe continues to lead the debate from a dialectical point of view, it is beginning to lose the legitimacy required to be able to do so in a credible way. the european union will not get very far in this area by trying to worry the world about this problem when in reality we see that the greenhouse gas bubble, especially carbon dioxide, is on track to increase by 6-8%, rather than being reduced by 8% as agreed in kyoto. it is therefore time to act internally to make our efforts externally credible. and, as far as political action is concerned, the transport sector must be our priority for two main reasons which i will explain to you. firstly, because, under the green paper published by the european commission, this sector will not be part of the european carbon dioxide emissions trading system, since all greenhouse gas reductions will have to be achieved exclusively through national and community policies and measures. secondly, because the transport sector is the one which contributes most to the european union's deviation from the kyoto levels. the recently published study by the european environment agency shows that transport is responsible for almost 30% of the total emissions in the european union, and it is also predicted that the level of carbon dioxide emissions from this sector will increase by 39% by 2001, compared to the level of emissions in 1990. in the face of this extremely worrying situation where road transport is responsible for 85% of total carbon dioxide emissions in the transport sector, and in the face of the urgent need for action, the agreement reached in the conciliation committee on this scheme for monitoring co2 emissions from light passenger vehicles is to be welcomed. this scheme is a fundamental part of the implementation of the kyoto protocol and will allow the voluntary compromise of the automotive industry to reduce carbon dioxide emissions from new passenger cars by 25% to be strictly monitored. i regret that light commercial vehicles have not been integrated into this system. i would like to congratulate the rapporteur once again on the results achieved.
- (pt) mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i should first of all like to congratulate my colleague, mr gilles savary, on the excellent reports he has drawn up on rail transport, a fundamental subject for the european union, with a view to strengthening the internal market, more sustainable development and an indispensable contribution to strengthening economic and social cohesion. i would also like to congratulate him on the fact that this is a very complex subject which he has dealt with with great clarity and depth, and also for the openness of mind and the negotiating capacity he has shown in including many of the proposals for change which have been put forward. the european parliament has long advocated a transport policy which favours rail transport because it is the least polluting, and often the most comfortable for passengers, or the most suitable for long-distance freight transport. in practice, however, we have observed exactly the opposite: the weight of rail transport of people and goods has gradually decreased, as has the number of workers in the respective companies, which is still in the order of one million. this is due to two fundamental factors: firstly, the frontiers between the 15 member states are maintained as regards rail transport, as are the respective national laws. secondly, rail transport is considered to be more expensive than road transport. with regard to this second aspect, some measures have already been taken, namely that in the majority of the countries of the european union, the construction and management of transport infrastructure proper have been separated. if the manufacturers and users of cars and lorries were directly responsible for paying for and maintaining the infrastructure - as is the case with the train - this mode of transport would certainly not have experienced the development it is experiencing. the proposals currently being put forward to establish rules for cross-border interoperability in the european union are clearly the way forward in this area. the interest shown by the portuguese presidency in advancing these matters as quickly as possible gives us hope that, with the contribution of the european parliament, this situation can finally change in favour of rail transport. as regards the high-speed rail system, i would simply stress two aspects which i consider essential: the interconnection and interoperability of high-speed infrastructure, of rolling stock and its ancillary services are essential to promote the mobility of people and goods and to promote an efficient transeuropean high-speed rail system, able to meet the needs of citizens. the other aspect i would like to highlight is the need for convergence of vocational qualifications, training measures and the working conditions of the respective workers. in conclusion, i would like to stress that for us socialists economic and social cohesion is one of the fundamental objectives of the european union and that in the field of rail transport we must help the peripheral regions of northern and southern europe and specific regions for which the cost of rehabilitation is very high, but absolutely necessary, because we have to adopt different track gauges, as in spain and portugal, where the objective of high-speed trains, especially through an atlantic route, is a legitimate ambition of governments and citizens.

- the following oral questions are scheduled for joint discussion: - b5-0221/2000 by mr mantovani and others, on behalf of the group of the european people's party (christian democrats) and european democrats (epp-ed), to the council, on the indebtedness of the poor countries; - b5-0222/2000 by mr mantovani and others, on behalf of the group of the european people's party (christian democrats) and european democrats (epp-ed), to the commission, on the indebtedness of the poor countries; - b5-0224/2000 by mr miranda and others, on behalf of the confederal group of the european united left - nordic green left, to the council, on the debt of the poor countries; - b5-0311/2000 by mr miranda and others, on behalf of the confederal group of the european united left - nordic green left, to the commission, on the debt of the poor countries; - b5-0312/2000 by mr cousteau, on behalf of the group of the european people's party, to the council, on the debt of the third world countries; - b5-0313/2000 by mr rod and others, on behalf of the green group in the european free alliance, to the council, on the cancellation of debts of poor countries; - b5-0314/2000 by mr rod and others, on behalf of the green group/european free alliance, to the commission, on the cancellation of debts of poor countries.
- (pt) mr president, the solution of the problem of the external debt of the least developed countries is undoubtedly an essential element of stability, security and development in these countries, of eradicating poverty and of slowing down the migratory flows which affect and exhaust them. we know the situation. the resources that these countries possess and those that they do not possess are used to pay for the debt service; some of these countries spend 40% of their budget on interest payments and depreciation. despite this, the debt continues to grow exponentially and the gap between them and the industrialized countries continues to widen, while the latter often behave like ordinary unscrupulous lenders. the imposition of inadequate structural adjustment programmes by the world bank and the international monetary fund, and insufficient development cooperation and aid from the industrialised countries, far from the level of 0.7% of gnp recommended by the united nations, are contributing to widening this gap. this situation is also due to the erroneous mercantilist conception that everything would be limited and resolved by the functioning of markets under the auspices of the wto, when it is certain that the globalisation of the economy has greatly contributed to increasing disparities, exacerbating injustices and accentuating delays. the solution to this situation therefore inevitably requires a new international order that puts the development of these countries at the centre of priorities. this order must be based on the idea that everyone, really everyone, including the rich countries, would benefit from this development. this project can only be achieved by cancelling the debt, which cannot be limited to a simple process of intention, such as that advocated by the g7, the scope of which does not exceed 1% of the debt of the third world and which is objectively limited today to the partial reduction of the debt of only three or four countries. the european union and its member states have and must have a say in this. we must abandon the limited and conditional perspectives which often accompany some shameful announcements of debt cancellation, by putting an end to the reductions in aid such as those which are being made and which are proposed for the future, and by moving forward in return with a broad and determined project which contributes decisively to putting an end to such a situation and which makes it possible to envisage the development of such important areas of the world.

- the next item is the report (a5-0131/2000) by mrs plooij-van gorsel, on behalf of the committee on industry, foreign trade, research and energy, on the communication from the commission to the council, the european parliament, the economic and social committee and the committee of the regions 'towards a european research area' (com (2000) 6 - c5-0115/2000 - 2000/2075 (cos)).
in writing. - (pt) in the 2000 economic guidelines, the european commission clearly defends its neoliberal positions, emphasising the stability pact and monetary policies, accelerating the liberalisation of the telecommunications, energy, postal, transport and financial services sectors, increasing the flexibility of work and the said modernisation of social protection systems. this means that despite mentioning the need for active employment policies to combat unemployment and achieve full employment in the medium term, the commission continues to insist on the same revenues which have not led to any concrete gains in employment, given that the current unemployment rate is even higher than at the beginning of the decade, as the commission acknowledges. in the ep report, the rapporteur makes some criticisms of the acceleration of the convergence and stability criteria objectives, insists on the need to increase investment and accepts the application of comprehensive tax measures to combat financial speculation, which is positive. nevertheless, it accepts the acceleration of liberalisation of transport and energy, the increase in labour flexibility and the ongoing process of budgetary consolidation, while clearly displaying its contradictions. in order to improve the content of the report, we have put forward various proposals, aimed in particular at strengthening the importance of small and very small enterprises and their associations, defending employment with rights and the reduction of working time, calling into question the stability pact and the consequences of its application. unfortunately, with the exception of support for smes, we were unable to get these proposals through, which is why we voted against the report.
in writing. - (pt) the pomés ruiz report is a fundamentalist report in defence of the principles of the stability pact, especially with regard to fiscal consolidation to guarantee the single objective of price stability. the analysis of the nominal convergence criteria is blind to the consequences in terms of the risks of deflation of the economy, which penalises public investment, economic growth and the policy of combating unemployment. but the rapporteur goes further, and this is in line with the recommendations of the european commission. he said that the objectives of the stability and convergence programmes should be achieved in advance, taking advantage of the current 'good economic moment'. it therefore proposes an acceleration of fiscal consolidation, structural reforms, privatisation and the 'modernization' of social protection, recommendations already made by the european commission, in particular for portugal, with an impact on the liberalisation of transport and energy, on the flexibility of the labour market, which is unacceptable. that is why we voted against the report.
in writing. - (pt) by relying on the need to identify and prevent the granting of subsidies which distort competition and to increase the transparency of relations between the state and public enterprises, the commission creates bureaucratic conditions and real limitations which can call into question the provision of a quality public service. the commission's philosophical need to ensure competition between the public and private sectors leads it to de facto underestimate the important role of public undertakings in providing a public service, which is unacceptable. the ep rapporteur, who in the initial version emphasised the role of 'services of general economic interest', has now had his report amended, mainly as regards the public service broadcasting. in our view, the directive should not apply to public service broadcasters, given their specific nature, their inseparability from the democratic, social and cultural needs of the various member states and their negative consequences for the fulfilment of the public interest mission. that is the reason for the proposal we have put forward, which has unfortunately been rejected.
in writing. - (pt) we cannot help but criticize the assumptions of this report, particularly with regard to the acceleration of the liberalization of the telecommunications sector, on the assumption that this process will have positive consequences for the users of this service. the experience of portugal is sufficient to show that this is not the case. portugal telecom, a public company with a multibillion-dollar public investment in escudos, was partially privatised, which led to an increase in rates for 'residential users' (the majority of the population) who are now charged 'the most expensive rates in europe'. at the level of workers, there is a reduction of thousands of jobs, an increase in the pace of work and the use in many cases of workers without rights or with low wages. in other words, the application of the logic of maximum profit in a highly profitable sector has created pressures towards a reduction in investment, which affects the quality of the universal service guaranteed to all users. we don't think that's the way to go. we need a modern public telecommunications service that takes account of the advances in technology and science. a public service with affordable costs, under equal conditions, guaranteeing the rights of users and as a factor of balanced development. with improved employment conditions for workers in this sector.
in writing. - (pt) it is important to note that despite all the declarations of intent by the european institutions concerning the new economy, the shift to the digital, knowledge-based economy, driven by the existence of new goods and services, the overall research effort of the european union in relation to gdp has been steadily decreasing for the last ten years. on average, the eu spends only 1.8% of its gnp on research, compared with 2.8% in the usa and 2.9% in japan. according to the european commission's communication, the gap between total public and private research expenditure in the us and the eu is continuously increasing, from eur 12 billion in 1992 to around eur 60 billion in 1998. in terms of employment too, the ratio between researchers and employees in firms is only 2.5 per thousand in europe, compared with 6.7 in the usa and 6 in japan. however, it is known that research and technology contribute 25 to 50% to economic growth and are decisive for competitiveness, employment and the quality of life of european citizens. so how can the heads of state and government of the 15 member states of the european union claim to achieve a new strategic objective in the next decade or to make the european economic area the most dynamic and competitive in the world, based on knowledge and capable of guaranteeing sustainable economic growth, with more and better jobs, with greater social cohesion, without significantly changing research policy, in particular the funds allocated to it? the commission has already taken a number of initiatives to improve the situation of the unemployed, and has also proposed a number of measures to improve the situation of the unemployed.
in writing. - (pt) we voted in favour of this report because it highlights the most important aspects of our analysis of the follow-up to the beijing declaration and programme of action. we also believe that the 2000 review process should not in any way lead to a renegotiation of the commitments made in beijing. the twelve critical areas are important and need to be addressed because sex discrimination is present in all areas and the lack of progress in one area inevitably affects the other areas. we also consider it important to hold a new conference in five years' time to assess the progress made by women in the fields of equality, development and peace. it is essential that the council, the commission and the member states do all they can to combat violence against women, whether in the home, the workplace, society in general or in armed conflict.

- (pt) mr president, ladies and gentlemen, it would have been desirable if the european parliament had made a positive, clear and decisive contribution to the proposal for a council directive, at the instigation of the portuguese presidency. unfortunately, we're not sure he will. in some respects, the proposals correspond to specific improvements to the reference text. i shall not go into details but will personally refer to the group's rights of action, legal aid and other rights which are already provided for in portuguese legislation. but the proposals we're debating could ruin everything. it seems that the rapporteurs and their supporters wanted to go further and in their desire to go further they risk seriously undermining the whole. we are concerned above all about three points: the unresolved question of the reversal of the burden of proof, the question of nationality and the question of religion. the issue of reversing the burden of proof is itself sufficiently delicate. there are, however, areas where this is clearly justified, but it is unacceptable that the rule in dubio pro auctore should be invoked instead of the classic principle in dubio pro reo, especially when it is not said whether this inversion of the rule applies to criminal cases - and we know that we are dealing with cases which can be described as criminal. it would be a violation of the fundamental principles of our legal civilization, of the fundamental guarantees of procedure so ancient, expressed in latin, that is, going back to roman law. secondly, the nationality. we portuguese represent perhaps the greatest mixture of blood and ethnic origins in history. me and my friends have no idea what our ethnicity is. we'd be a real headache for a poor nazi researcher and for the ethnic paintings that were so expensive, a few weeks ago, to mrs. baroness ludford, in another report. we are aware of the problem of racism, which in our view is meaningless, but which must be fought vigorously, especially racial violence. but you mustn't confuse the questions. ethnicity is one thing, nationality another. if there is discrimination between a black and a white, or vice versa, for example if a white portuguese is discriminated against by a black portuguese or a black portuguese by a white portuguese, there is clearly unlawful racism which must be fought. on the other hand, if in the access to certain functions a portuguese, whether asian, black or white, is able to assume these functions in terms which do not apply to a zimbabwean, whether black, white or asian, it is no longer a question of racism, it is a question of citizenship and it can be legitimate. the problem is that in many proposals the concepts are confused, which could have devastating effects, particularly in areas such as the public service or even in unforeseen areas. religion is another key theme. we want to make it clear that for us freedom of religious association is a fundamental civil liberty, which cannot be questioned, or thwarted or endangered, as in some of the proposals under consideration.

- the proposal for a directive on the approximation of the laws of the member states relating to the permissible sound power level of motor vehicles (com (87) 6 final - doc. it was not until 1985 that the problem really began to gain media attention, with the discovery of a hole in the stratospheric ozone layer. after many attempts at consultation, the main countries producing and using chlorofluorocarbons, the famous cfcs, with the exception of the eastern countries, china and india, concluded the montreal protocol in 1987. this protocol was truly a historic protocol because, for the first time, the global scale of the profound climate imbalances that could threaten the survival and health of people and the very principle of life was realized. and it's practically become a paradigm. the recent report of the european environment agency entitled 'environmental signals' strangely sounds the alarm not only because ozone concentrations in the surface layers are increasing but also because the protective ozone layer in the upper atmosphere has been shrinking considerably over europe since the early 1980s, at a rate of 8% per decade. it's a concern. this is happening at a time when chlorine compounds and other gases that affect the ozone layer in the troposphere have been reduced as a result of international agreements that have proved to be of little or no effect. the production and marketing of compounds that attack the upper atmosphere's ozone layer actually declined after 1989. but given the long life cycle of these products, the ozone layer will not be fully restored until 2050, according to the report by the european environment agency. that is why all the actions that the european union could take, all the measures that it could take to protect the ozone layer, are vital and cannot tolerate any polemics such as, for example, global warming and the 'greenhouse effect'. if there were any remaining doubts about the effectiveness of the changes, in the case of ozone, there is no doubt. the protection of ozone is fundamental to the protection of human life, plants and biodiversity. the amendments proposed to the montreal protocol are designed to strengthen the protection of the ozone layer, which is a precious filter for life on earth, and to strengthen the control system for trade in substances which deplete the ozone layer. the rapporteur deserves all our applause and thanks for the speed with which she has worked on this report, because the sooner the better in this matter.

- (pt) mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to begin by congratulating the rapporteur, mr ingo schmitt, on the excellent report he has presented to us and on the suggestions he has made on the rationalization of the commission's proposals. the disappearance of internal borders and the creation of an area of freedom, security and justice require us to make an effort to bring asylum policies closer together, as well as immigration and reinforced controls at external borders. as we have already said, a serious approach to this problem requires us to make a strict distinction between the entry of asylum seekers, in accordance with the geneva convention relating to the status of refugees, the entry of economic and other migrants and the temporary reception of refugees from crisis areas, which are different situations and all require separate treatment. mr president, the asylum applications have not been distributed evenly, as mr pirker has pointed out. there is obviously a disproportion in the distribution of the number of asylum applications registered in each of the member states. this disproportion is negative for the european union as a whole because it encourages inequality between member states. the procedures for granting refugee status and the conditions for examining applications should therefore be harmonised in order to create equivalent conditions for the protection of asylum seekers. a policy of burden sharing must be developed. solidarity must be shared by all. it is essential to adopt common rules on the reception of asylum seekers, the requirements for obtaining refugee status, the process of granting or refusing refugee status, which should in particular avoid applications for asylum being lodged simultaneously in different member states.

- (pt) madam president, mr minister, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to begin by saying that the feira european council represents a new opportunity for us to express ourselves in this parliament on the various political issues which constitute the union's agenda. among these, i would like to highlight four for the moment, some of which were raised by minister jaime gama and others not. i would start with austria: it is well known that we have no sympathy for political extremism, but we cannot fail to condemn the untimely reaction of the 14 member states to the formation of a government following democratic elections, without it having even had the opportunity to take office. this is a decision which has not respected the sovereignty of the austrian people. it was also an ineffective decision: against racism and xenophobia we must fight with intelligence and perseverance and not by means of proclamations which can ultimately generate the most formidable reaction that can be, which goes far beyond the dissemination of an anti-european sentiment, that is, that of encouraging what we want to fight. the portuguese presidency agreed to take on the role of spokesman for the member states last february, in our view by mistake, although the matter has always been considered bilaterally, and obviously in concert with the other member states. so, while we cannot formally demand that the presidency of our country solve this problem, we can demand that each of the governments take responsibility. in this way, it will be possible to know who is still isolating the country and who, recognising this mistake, is opting for the restoration of normal relations with austria, thus avoiding the maintenance of "walls of disagreement", to use the words of secretary of state seixas da costa, between the member states of the union. secondly, i would like to draw attention to the intergovernmental conference. the distinctive feature of the portuguese presidency should be the recognition and defence of political equality between member states as a corollary of maintaining the balance between large and small countries and between them and the european union. the participation of national parliaments in institutional reform and the management of european affairs should even be given priority, so that the citizens of each country can be involved more democratically and more directly in the process of building their europe and in the definition of the community's decision-making methods. as regards enhanced cooperation, the principle of flexibility on which it is based, which we support, should be contrasted with the guarantee that no member state will be excluded from it initially unless it so wishes. finally, as regards the questions not resolved by the treaty of amsterdam, the rule of one commissioner per member state should be established, taking into account the fact that the commission has a monopoly on legislative initiative, the fact that co-decision already reflects a double weighting of votes, one in the council and the other in the european parliament, and that, contrary to a so-called generalization of qualified majority voting, the maintenance of the rule of unanimity should be defended in order to preserve the political balance between member states, regardless of their population or economic weight, because this is a key principle for the success of the peaceful concertation and development of europe that we have known throughout the last half-century. thirdly, i would mention the charter of fundamental rights. its contents are not in question. the point is that the european union does not need a charter of fundamental rights. and it does not need one for the simplest reason in the world: the union has no major problems with fundamental rights. and as we have said before in this house, since there is no problem, the charter is not a solution. and since it is not a solution, the charter is a problem. we therefore hope that the feira summit will make it possible to conclude on this obvious fact: there are no disputes in the definition or application of fundamental rights within the european union which cannot be resolved by the respective member states, within the framework of their constitutions and the convention on human rights, to which all the member states, as well as the european union, adhere, both before and, of course, after enlargement. and finally, i would like to say a word about enlargement. despite the progress announced here, we believe that more than ten years after the fall of the berlin wall, the european union could have shown a more vigorous and friendly political will to open up to the peoples of central and eastern europe, who legitimately aspire to share a common future with us, avoiding disappointment and frustration within them. this will is manifested in three essential aspects: in defending the great european causes of peace, freedom and economic and social development, which must underlie the negotiations with the candidate countries; in promoting the merit of each country as the only condition to be imposed for accession; and in maintaining the economic and social cohesion of the current 15 member states. austria, the intergovernmental conference, the charter of fundamental rights and enlargement: four subjects, four different responses. that, madam president, is what we expect from the feira meeting of heads of state or government under the portuguese presidency.
