the rapporteur, mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the proposal before us marks a revolution in the control systems and provides the technical basis for a revolution in the way the common agricultural policy is managed. the proposed method is based on a modern geographical information system where the cartographic information relating to agricultural holdings is digitised and integrated with the alphanumeric information necessary to characterise the holding and the subsidies received. in terms of control, this method has enormous advantages and greatly reduces the possibility of fraud or any other irregularity. in terms of the management of the cap, it finally creates a management infrastructure that provides the technical basis for changing a policy that has hitherto irrationally subsidised the richest countries, regions and farmers, encouraging surplus production and environmental degradation, into a geographically oriented system towards sustainable agriculture that will benefit the most disadvantaged rural regions and communities. we can thus appreciate the political will and the necessary discernment displayed by the european institutions in implementing a new policy. in my view, virtually all the sectors financed by the cap should be integrated into this system. the recent report of the court of auditors on classical swine fever shows that, even in areas where geographical information seems to be less important, it is decisive and can become an instrument for rationalising the common agricultural policy. it seems to me that there is already a great deal of benefit in immediately integrating into this system three sectors which are not covered by the commission's proposal: oil, flax and hemp, and tobacco. in the case of oil, the aim is to integrate the existing system of specific geographical information into the general system. as for flax and hemp, we know that the commission has already proposed to include them in arable crops, but we do not know when or if the proposal will be approved. as far as tobacco is concerned, this is the most urgent and necessary integration: the fact that tobacco is not currently subsidized per hectare but per quantity produced does not in any way prevent the possibility of integrating it into the system now, given the advantages it offers. the application of a geographical control system will ensure that the subsidised tobacco is actually produced on the farms to be subsidised. it would also be absurd if the very extensive information which the commission requires on each farm in the context of the clearance of accounts were not incorporated into the new system. in this respect, the commission should limit its requirements to what is necessary and reasonable. it must be understood, on the one hand, that the cap has a growing hidden administrative cost which is borne by national and regional administrations. and secondly, that it is not possible to require farmers to spend more time filling in paperwork than they do milking cows or tilling the soil. we do not want to turn farmers back into bureaucrats. but we must ensure that fraud is prevented and that the complexity of the system does not lead to a proliferation of minor formal irregularities and to unfair penalties for farmers. simplifying the legislation for the application of the cap is a top priority. a final word to express what is expected of the commission: it is incomprehensible that a project of this scale and scope does not culminate in the processing, at european level, of all the information collected. it is impossible to understand how a common policy such as agriculture can be treated as a set of fifteen national policies or, worse, as an incoherent stacking of invoices from 80 to 90 national paying authorities. the commission has the obligation to coordinate all the geographical information currently required from the 15 member states. and it goes further: this precious resource must be used to further the policy of economic and social cohesion, environmental protection and also spatial planning.
the rapporteur. - (pt) mr president, ladies and gentlemen, regulation no 302/93, which is amended in this report, created and governs the operation of the european monitoring centre for drugs and drug addiction. since 1993, the agency has made a decisive contribution to improving the knowledge of the drug phenomenon in its various aspects throughout the european union. as the enlargement of the union approached, the commission rightly wanted the observatory to play a role in the candidate countries, as part of a pre-accession strategy. the aim is to prepare the way for these countries to be integrated into the observatory's activities, in particular by creating the conditions for the establishment of structural links with the reitox network, which centralises and statistically processes the data provided by the member states in this field. in concrete terms, the observatory is to be entrusted directly with the implementation of projects financed by the phare programme in the field of drug information systems. it must be acknowledged that the content of this report, which purports to express the opinion of the european parliament, goes beyond the scope of the commission's proposals. the truth is that since 1993, when the observatory was set up, there has never been an opportunity to revise its rules of procedure in the light of an assessment of the use of the instruments at its disposal and the mission entrusted to it. nor has there been any opportunity to make any adjustments. in the meantime, new data have emerged, both from its operational experience and from the 'independent evaluation report' released in march this year, as well as from the evolution of the union itself, its acquis and the challenges it faces. the report before us is based on the principle that parliament cannot ignore these realities now that it has a unique opportunity to take a stand. thus, while the amendments proposed in this report go beyond the strict measure of amendments necessary to adapt the observatory to the requirements of enlargement, these proposals are absolutely essential for the observatory to fulfil its mission satisfactorily, even within the current framework of the union. in this context, the committee on civil liberties, justice and home affairs considers it essential that the observatory be recognised as having a function of evaluating the action of the states, as well as of the union, in their approach to the phenomenon of drugs and drug addiction. this is not to grant a specialised agency powers of political evaluation incompatible with its nature. the aim is to ensure better and more qualified instruments for political decision-making. the political decision is taken elsewhere and by the rightful ones. it is also a question of providing the public with rigorous and objective information for general clarification in such a socially sensitive area. in reality, the observatory should not function as a mere post office box for collecting data describing the national reality of the states, data which do not always arrive in the best conditions and in a timely manner. the states must give their full cooperation to the observatory. it must collect the information and reserve it for appropriate statistical processing, taking advantage of the comparability of the data; the commission will also have to analyse the data obtained, drawing on the experience and technical competence of its experts. in this way, the experience of the different states in the fight against drugs can be mutually beneficial and policy-makers will have a valuable instrument to assist their decisions. in our view, this european agency cannot remain underemployed. the complexity of drug and drug addiction issues calls, more than ever, for the european monitoring centre for drugs and drug addiction and its expert voice to be given a high profile.
- (pt) mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to congratulate the two rapporteurs on their contributions, which represent a new step in our fight against the scourge of drugs. we have no doubt that drugs are an evil that must be fought, both by the repression of trafficking and by an effective effort to prevent, treat and rehabilitate. as the recent evaluation of the european monitoring centre for drugs and drug addiction points out, the centre has made a huge contribution to european drug policy by filling the gaps in information and knowledge. the same report concludes that the observatory has developed an impressive number of activities since its inception, thus adding value to the european fight against drugs. the very existence of the agency has ensured that drug issues remain on the political agenda and has given the european union and its member states greater visibility and credibility in the international drug debate. the work on synthetic drugs has been distinguished by its effectiveness and timeliness. today, drug use is no longer limited to old products, but we are seeing with concern the emergence of new markets for synthetic products, which are easier and cheaper to produce, generate more profits for traffickers and have more harmful consequences for the health of consumers. the observatory shall carry out its activities in the field of information under the conditions laid down in its rules of procedure. the information produced is intended to help the community and the member states to obtain a general picture of the situation with regard to the drugs problem, so that they can take action or initiate action within their respective fields of competence. this underlines the essential role of the observatory as a support instrument for the decision-making process. however, the report pointed to some operational problems and delays in the establishment of structures essential to the observatory's activities. it is therefore understandable that, in the name of the effectiveness of this instrument, we should advocate its enhancement and improvement instead of collaborating in the dispersion of activities that could betray its fundamental objectives. the epp therefore, in accordance with the substance of the two reports we are debating today and which we are going to adopt, will certainly disagree with proposals which in our view will not contribute to the objectives i have mentioned. the enlargement of the european union to include new countries is an imperative of the union. it will make it possible to include states with very diverse political cultures and, in many cases, with young and unconsolidated constitutions and structures. the observatory's work is also indispensable for these states and their citizens as they fight to end the scourge of drugs. the pre-accession strategy for the union includes the possibility for candidate countries to become members of certain agencies - such as the observatory - before becoming full members of the european union. we welcome the accession of norway to the observatory, following its express wish, mainly in view of the close cultural, commercial and social relations which historically link the european union to norway.
the rapporteur. - (pt) i take advantage of the president's indulgence to say, as rapporteur, that the debate we have just had this morning is the first occasion for the european parliament, since the establishment of the european monitoring centre for drugs and drug addiction in 1993, to express its opinion on the substantive question of amending its rules of procedure. it is our view that parliament would have seriously failed in its most basic political responsibilities if it had not given a broad interpretation of its duties and if it had not presented a set of proposals which constitutes a new and profound formulation of the operation of the observatory, because we believe that the drugs observatory can have an indispensable, necessary and useful role in the future in the european union, but not with the present report, on which the commission seems to agree in the main.

therefore not lead to a two-speed europe. they must allow those who wish to make faster progress in the field of sectoral policies, but not in the field of institutions, otherwise they will divide the - (pt) madam president, mr prime minister, the feira council did not bring any major new developments. it is even repetitive in some fundamental policies and has not solved the most problematic ones. so he's disappointing you. as expected, the main issues, from enlargement to the igc and the charter of fundamental rights, were largely deferred to the nice summit. it is certainly for this reason that a last-minute agreement on the timetable for the tax package had to be devised, which is ultimately and essentially limited to postponing the matter to another agreement, which is only expected in a few years, still subject to the dubious agreement of third countries, more precisely the current tax havens. it is therefore not surprising that this virtual agreement has been warmly welcomed by those who have never wanted a greater balance between capital and labour legislation and who have always opposed any joint action against tax havens in the field of tax evasion and tax fraud or relocations. nevertheless, in return, in the name of completing the internal market and continuing the extraordinary summit in lisbon, there has been renewed pressure to accelerate the process of liberalization and deregulation of the labour market. in approving the broad economic policy guidelines for 2000, it was agreed to strengthen and anticipate the constraints arising from the stability pact, which suggests that new problems, particularly in the social field, may arise. the important demonstration of the trade union movement at the feira council is therefore understandable. the large number of participants - the agenda calls for the report (a5-0172/2000) by mrs lienemann, on behalf of the european parliament's delegation to the conciliation committee, on the joint text approved by the conciliation committee for a regulation of the european parliament and of the council on a financial instrument for the environment (life) (c5-0221/2000 - 1998/0336 (cod)).
- (pt) mr president, ladies and gentlemen, at the end of the debate on the second reading of life-iii, which we had here last february, we concluded that this regulation was better than those of life-i and life-ii. better because it is more rigorous, transparent and rational. better because it defined employment promotion as a factor to be taken into account in the selection of candidate projects. better because it defined reducing the overall impact of products, from manufacturing to recycling and disposal, as one of the objectives to be achieved by life-environment. finally, it was better because it defined the development and spatial planning of coastal areas as one of the priorities of life-environment. despite these virtues, there were still four points of disagreement between the european parliament, the commission and the council, which were subject to the conciliation process, namely the budget, the comitology, the foreseeable fourth phase of life and the objectives of life-environment. the work done in the conciliation process has brought further improvements to the life-iii regulation and we can say with some simplicity that, while we lost on the issue of comitology, we were able to make our views known on the essential issues. i therefore congratulate our rapporteur, mrs lienemann, on the work she has done in the first and second readings and on the work she has done in the conciliation. let me emphasize two points which i think are more important. as regards the reference budget for the period 2000-2004, the main question asked, it is management and the reduction of greenhouse gas emissions among the objectives set for life-environment.
(pt) mr president, ladies and gentlemen, it is said that each age, each generation, gives the image it has of its relationship with nature. the concept of the relationship with nature has changed radically in our time. it can no longer be subordinated to profit, selfishness and exploitation. politicians of all stripes must now reflect on this new philosophy of the relationship between man and society and the environment. this is why the european union has been a pioneer in developing legislation on environmental protection, and has also been a key partner in international negotiations and agreements on the global protection of the earth. the transposition of european directives into national legislation has played and continues to play a decisive role in all countries, especially in portugal. the life programme is only a financial instrument for the environmental policies of the european union and its neighbouring countries, the baltic states, the mediterranean countries and the countries of the east. but it is not the only financial instrument whose effects are felt on environmental policies: we know that regional policy and agricultural policy - the latter being largely harmful - have a huge impact on the environment, soil and water in europe. the adoption of life is of undeniable importance. after many twists and turns and a period of paralysis of projects financed under the programme, the portuguese presidency and the european parliament were finally able to agree on a budgetary framework of eur 640 billion for the period 2000-04. life-iii will be operational before the summer. the great merits of this agreement must be shared equally between the portuguese presidency - which has once again demonstrated constructive and effective relations with the european parliament and its capacity for mediation - and the european parliament delegation, in particular mrs lienemann. i welcome the agreement of the portuguese presidency and the european parliament on such a fundamental instrument for the european union's environmental policy as the water framework directive.

- (pt) mr president, after the launch of the euro in january 1999, it seems that the interest of european citizens in the new european currency, far from intensifying, has been diluted, as if the extremely long three-year period during which banknotes and coins did not exist made it a virtual currency. although we have repeatedly stated that since january 1999 the euro has been the official currency of the eleven countries of the european union - and will also be the official currency of greece and possibly denmark from the beginning of next year - and that national currencies are only multiples or fractions of the euro, the truth is that the majority of europeans do not believe that the escudo, the peseta, the franc or the mark are no longer official currencies when they are the ones they have in their hands. where bank statements for water, electricity, telephone and supermarket bills are also denominated in euro, this seems to be a mathematical exercise of minor utility. we are now one and a half years away from the end of national banknotes and coins and it is high time that we became fully aware of the new currency, that we internalized its value, that we became aware of its implications for our lives and for the development of our economies and societies. i believe that information campaigns on the euro should be focused on these two aspects: the first concerns the value, form and use of the euro as a currency; the second concerns the capacity and efficiency of its use in the context of the monetary stability of the european union, the pace of development achieved - the highest in recent decades - the control of inflation and the creation of jobs. but for these campaigns to achieve the desired results, it is essential that they be coordinated. there can be no campaign by the european central bank, another by the commission or parliament and others by the various member states involved. it is essential to coordinate the available means effectively in order to reach the target groups and to ensure that no mistakes have been made, as has already been shown, for example, in documents widely distributed by the commission to portuguese schools. schools are the nerve centres of information and its multiplication effects which must be used as concretely, simply and effectively as possible. the keyword for these campaigns must therefore be the coordination of efforts, participation, human, technical and financial resources. but it seems that so far nobody knows who is doing what, and this situation should be remedied as soon as possible. 2001 should be the year when the euro will play a leading role in relation to national currencies in price setting, company accounting and cross-border use. it is necessary to put an end to the scandal of crossborder costs within the euro area, which does not involve any risk to the banking system. the payment of a percentage for the use of credit, debit or bank transfers is one of the causes of the euro's discredit among european citizens. only action by the commission prohibiting the genuine cartels that have been formed can put an end to this and help to restore confidence. this is what the citizens of europe expect.
in writing. - (pt) it is positive that the conciliation process on the proposal for a regulation on a financial instrument for the third phase of the life programme (2000-2004) has finally been completed, which should allow projects to continue to be financed under the new programme which will start in the second quarter of this year. it is also positive that the amendments to the programme objectives and the compromise on a proposal for a new programme have been approved. therefore, given that life is the only instrument which directly promotes an environmental policy of the european union, and with appreciable results, it is regrettable that the reference budget for life-iii was set at eur 640 million, which is only eur 27 million more than the common position of the commission and the council had foreseen, and therefore far from the eur 850 million proposed by the european parliament at first and second reading.
in writing. - (pt) i voted for the report by mr lehne, which improves the original initiative by extending the scope of the directive on money laundering. the great merit of this directive is that its adoption has led all the member states to classify money laundering as a criminal offence. with its overall preventive effect, it has contributed to the defence of the financial system by dissuading persons potentially interested in money laundering from using the system for that purpose, credit and financial institutions having to take the initiative to communicate to the competent authorities all the elements that could constitute a money laundering index. the aim of this enlargement is to increase efforts to combat money laundering opportunities outside the traditional financial sector. the directive was aimed at various financial sectors such as banking, insurance, etc. and will have to be extended to other activities and professions of a non-financial nature but extremely vulnerable to money laundering. in the case of lawyers and notaries, there should be clear safeguard clauses, in particular with regard to the safeguarding of the right to confidentiality. while recognising the devastating impact of money laundering and other forms of corruption, the right of citizens to access legal advice and assistance should be safeguarded. the list of classified crimes is also to be extended: in addition to the revenues from drug trafficking, activities linked to organised crime should also be included (a uniform definition of the concept of organised crime is essential, not only at european level but also at international level). efforts to agree common definitions, charges and sanctions - in the case of approximation of criminal legislation - should be directed as a matter of priority to sectors of particular importance, such as money laundering. with ever-increasing sophistication, many organised criminal groups are able to exploit legal gaps and differences between the laws of the member states, exploiting the anomalies of the different systems.
in writing. - (pt) it is positive that the fight against money laundering is being intensified, not only in relation to drug trafficking, as was the case in the previous directive, but also in relation to money laundering in connection with organised crime and a wider range of other types of crime (fraud, corruption or other illegal activities which harm the community's financial interests). the commission has also proposed that the directive be amended to include the obligation to report suspicious activities in the case of large transactions involving financial transactions by professionals such as sellers of luxury goods, auctioneers, customs officials and others. the rapporteur of the ep's draft report points to slightly different paths compared to the proposal for a directive, but in essence he supports it. in any case, the great fundamental question of the increasing liberalization of financial markets and capital movements is not called into question, since its practical effects will continue to diminish. that is why i am voting against.
in writing. - (pt) we believe that, in view of the new objectives for the european union, it is essential to carry out a comprehensive review of the financial perspective, with special emphasis on headings 3 (internal policies) and 4 (external actions). we are convinced that the new priorities must match the new financial resources. in this spirit, we reject, first and foremost, the prospect of 'cuts' in cooperation policy, as is apparent from the commission's preliminary draft. the same applies to the proposals on employment and the environment. we also reject the €300 million cut in agriculture. in addition, in the latter area, we consider it essential to create a fund to deal with disasters in the primary sector. we therefore agree with some of the rapporteur's proposals. but the improvements suggested fall far short of what is needed. in addition, certain amendments introduced in the final vote limit and even alter its scope. and we don't follow his lead on defense policy. for these reasons we voted against the report as it stands in its final version.

the rapporteur. - (pt) mr president, i believe that the legislative instrument we are analysing today in the evaluation report sent by the commission has completed its journey in relation to contractual rights and social security systems. although thousands of workers from eu countries have benefited; but it has been overtaken. it is outdated because the ilo convention, which dates from 1952, has just been revised and most countries in the world should substantially improve maternity protection. the union was, of course, expected, and is still expected, to remain socially more advanced in this and other areas. but all is not lost, since the transposition of the directive on work organization has proved to be imperfect, and its implementation even more so. most countries do not respect the three phases of workplace organisation: change of workplace for hazardous work, change of worker and only after sick leave, which reduces the worker's income and career prospects. i would say, for example, that some countries do not even respect the minimum period of recovery for mothers, which experts say is 45 days for a normal birth, at a normal workstation. so there are countries that do not respect these rules under the pretext of guaranteeing an option for women. we believe that women should be guaranteed the option of enjoying a good maternity leave, to be shared with the father, but that leave in respect of the risk to their own health should be assessed and guaranteed. in addition, the risk assessment is not articulated in this directive with the others, especially with the framework directive on risk assessment and information for workers. it is not articulated and it casts an unjust anathema on women's employment, according to which the risks of motherhood apply exclusively to young women of childbearing age. these risks are really underestimated and should not fall on the employer or be an obligation for the employer only when the doctor informs the employer of the condition of the worker. first, because it is too late: the risks of radiation, chemical environments and imperfect positions produce their harmful consequences for the health of the woman and the fetus in the first weeks. secondly, because they do not protect the reproductive health of men and women, while the extent of the problem of infertility is already appreciable today in the union, more than 11% of european households are affected, because of the lifestyles generated by our economic organization and also because it does not offer protection against these dangers: i would take for example the extreme temperatures that cause female and male infertility. it would be good if the union, which now fulfils the conditions, could draw up and apply legislation to this end. as for leave, we know the costs of extending it to economic activity and we know that they are much higher for women's employment. but we also know that health experts recommend that for at least five months, children should be kept indoors, protected from the outside world and, above all, raised in a supportive psychosocial environment that provides the balance needed for safe growth because they need their parents. this leave must be extended and given to both parents in a healthy way. i would also say that the directive must also cover the informal economy and atypical employment contracts. finally, i hope the commission will tell us what it intends to do about this now that it has a broad legal basis, with article 13 instead of the old article 18, which allows it to respond safely to other aspects to which the report refers.

- mr president, with this report from the european parliament, we are faced with a matter of the utmost importance, not only in the context of the common foreign and security policy but also in the context of the deepening of the european union itself. significant progress is already being made in the cfsp, particularly in the area of defence cooperation. a joint military force is being formed. it would be strange - if not dangerous - if the european union did not at the same time take care to improve its common diplomatic instruments. nothing can justify such a lag, even if we take into account the national interest of each member state and the almost exclusively economic character which marked the institutional history of the community between the treaty of rome and the treaty of maastricht. the diplomacy of the early 21st century is marked by major events and processes which make it a much broader activity than it was in the past. the globalisation of the international economy and financial markets is one of these. it is not too much to say that diplomacy is increasingly becoming economically involved and that the defence of economic interests cannot be separated from diplomacy. and, as is also well known, this reality is projected against a background of redefining the protagonists on the scene of international politics. but the objectives which give the market a soul or ideal supplement, namely the struggle for a project capable of reconciling the aims of economic development with those of political and social development, cannot be achieved without a deep cooperation between the member states at all levels and, specifically, at the diplomatic level. what better instrument to promote peace and the reconciliation of interests than diplomacy? the european union must, as this report recommends, equip itself with more and better elements of common diplomacy. it is clear that, among other obstacles, the pillar organization and the absence of a legal personality of the european union make it difficult to achieve this objective and force some acrobatics in the proposals in this report. however, we can contribute, without great economic and organizational costs, to a better use of the diplomatic resources available in the union for the benefit of all. the richness and diversity of the diplomatic experience of the various european countries is also recognised worldwide. it would be wrong to assume that any of the proposals in this report could undermine ongoing processes in the cfsp in general or in the field of diplomacy in particular. the spirit of this report, as we understand it and as it has emerged from the various discussions over the course of months, is to integrate all the valid experiences and processes under way towards a common objective, which is primarily the creation of a genuine common european diplomatic service. in conclusion, i would say that although some critical remarks can be made on certain aspects of the report, i would certainly recommend its approval, hoping that the proposals put forward will be taken up.

- mr president, i shall be extremely brief, since i would like to deal only with a very particular aspect of the european union's external action, a question which i believe is specific but which should be given the greatest attention and which concerns us very much. i am referring to a clear trend towards a growing devaluation, in attention and resources, of cooperation with developing countries. i am of course aware that there are other objectives and that these should of course be given due consideration. but i think that, both because of the expectations that are being created, because of the responsibilities that fall to the european union, and because of the new problems that the developing countries are facing, we think that consideration of these important areas of the world should be very present in our action. and i would particularly like to stress one aspect: in this context we need to have our own and autonomous strategies which affirm the european union in this field. this concern is particularly apparent in the successive budget cuts in the context of cooperation, which in some way illustrate and validate the fears i have just mentioned, which i think must be put to an end once and for all.
mr president, i welcome the commission's proposals on these two fisheries agreements, and i am referring in particular to the agreement with guinea. i would begin by highlighting and supporting the innovations introduced in this agreement by devoting 50% of the total european union support to specific actions aimed at the sustainable development of the sector. and in addition to the novelty of this agreement, i would also like to stress the innovation of an additional premium of eur 370 000 in the event that the republic of guinea implements a set of measures aimed at reducing overall fishing effort. in the case of a country with an annual available production of 250 000 tonnes of fish and a small-scale fleet with a capacity of only 50 000, it is clear that this is an agreement of mutual benefit without any conflict of interest and which also allows exceptional financing to be obtained for the development of guinea's fisheries sector. this shows that fisheries agreements, in addition to their interest for the european union, can be used as development instruments for these countries. and as a portuguese, i cannot but regret that the commission and the council are so late in negotiating the agreement with morocco. i also regret the difficulties that are being raised in other member states having access to fisheries in greenland, whose agreement has been little used and which is due to be renewed shortly. but it is important that the monitoring and control requirements and the programmes aimed at controlling fishing effort should also be imposed on other countries fishing in the waters of these developing countries and not just on the european union states. it is in this spirit that i ask the european union to make an extra effort in the course of the negotiations to ensure that this rule applies to everyone and not just to us.

also have to face the difficulties arising from the implementation of the directive on special measures concerning the movement and residence of citizens of the union, justified on grounds of public policy, public security or public health, in which there should be guidelines not only for the member states, which have so far interpreted the provisions of the directive very differently, but also for citizens in relation to the rights conferred on them. member states may impose restrictions on the right of free movement of community citizens, in particular as regards entry into and exit from their territory, and on the issue or renewal of residence permits where justified on grounds of public policy, public security or public health. but they cannot, in a european union based on the rule of law, on respect for human rights and in particular the european convention on human rights and soon a charter of fundamental rights, abuse these prerogatives. it is in this sense that these exceptions to free movement were interpreted restrictively. finally, mr president, i would like to agree with my colleague, mr hernández mollar, when he expressed his support for the proposal by the would give all the necessary information not only to citizens regarding their rights as citizens but also to the respective administrative services of each member state responsible for applying existing legislation.
in writing. - (pt) the enormous industrial changes of recent years, resulting from increasing globalisation, new technologies and social transformations, have produced not only economic growth and new production sectors, but also an increasing number of mergers, closures and relocations of businesses, which have resulted in the loss of local markets, unemployment, the deterioration of social protection and trade union systems, greater social exclusion and enormous human suffering. it is therefore of the utmost importance to create a european observatory on industrial change in order to stimulate a more active and responsible approach to industrial development, with the participation of the social partners, with a view to avoiding the most painful consequences of industrial change. but it is also urgent that the commission speed up the revision of directive 94/95/ec on the establishment of a european works council and regulation (eec) no 4064/89 in order to ensure greater participation of trade union organisations before decisions are taken on mergers and transfers of undertakings and a greater guarantee of protection and defence of workers' rights.
in writing. - (pt) we welcome the adoption of this report, to which we have contributed with various proposals. we now hope that the commission and the member states will take the necessary steps to make the erasmus programme more accessible to students from economically disadvantaged groups, in particular by optimising the coordination between national grants for higher education and erasmus grants, aiming at promoting social justice in access to the programme and its full use. we cannot continue to accept that the overall participation rate in the programme is painfully close to 50% and that despite this, some 57% of erasmus students are experiencing serious financial problems. as the report states, it is regrettable that the erasmus programme has mainly benefited students from social backgrounds where parents have a high level of education, relative economic capacity and/or are from countries which provide higher public student aid. hence the need for measures to achieve the initial objective of enabling 10% of all students in the community to complete part of their studies in another member state, and not just 1% as was the case in 1997/98, by making it accessible to students from economically disadvantaged groups.
in writing. - (pt) while the report presented to parliament was adopted positively, the same cannot be said of all the amendments tabled in plenary. the principle of free movement of persons, which was enshrined in the treaty of rome fifty years ago, still faces many practical difficulties. as the report states, the application of the directives on the right of residence for students and pensioners (90/364, 90/365, 93/96) is clearly unsatisfactory, as is the case with the directive on migrant workers. workers in 'atypical' jobs, parttime, short-term or other, face difficulties in staying in the host country. the rapporteur also points out that millions of third-country nationals are now legally resident in the european union and are frequently deprived of the rights of free movement and establishment. it is therefore essential that the member states and the commission take the necessary decisions to ensure that the rights of migrant workers are respected and to improve their situation. as regards students and pensioners, it is also essential to reduce bureaucratic procedures and to facilitate the free movement, movement and residence of these citizens of the european union in any member state.
- mr president, the compromise reached on the text of the new framework directive on water policy is generally positive, although it contains specific, controversial and critical aspects which, however, can still be mitigated when the various studies, action plans and draft regulations envisaged in the text now approved are drawn up. it is positive that water management requirements are integrated into a single system, the watershed management, which is based more on geographical and hydrological areas than on administrative and national boundaries, a particularly important issue for portugal, where the main rivers are shared with neighbouring spain. it is also positive to say that water is not a commodity that can be traded like any other, which naturally requires special measures to protect a public good, but always safeguarding the interests of the population, in particular domestic supply and agricultural activity, especially small farms and family farming. the final text does mention that the member states may take account of the social and economic effects as well as the geographical and climatic conditions of the region concerned, which allows for adaptation to the different situations of the member states through derogations provided for, which may, however, prove insufficient for the cases of southern agriculture, in particular for portugal. we shall, however, be attentive to its practical application in the years ahead.

- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, in the traditional conception of education, the student completed his learning at the end of schooling. certainly a small elite continued to create, transmit or acquire new knowledge and expertise. but for the vast majority, leaving school meant the end of formal learning. things have changed radically. schooling has become wider and longer. new sources and ways of acquiring knowledge and training have emerged. the commission has already taken a number of initiatives to promote the use of the euro, and i am sure that the commission will be able to support the commission's proposals. it is rightly said that we have entered the knowledge society. but the great philosopher nietzsche left us with a warning: "it is impossible to found a civilization on knowledge", he said. the warning should be a matter of meditation. although there is no alternative to the development of science, the increase in knowledge and information, the truth is that lifelong learning cannot be reduced to the simple acquisition of useful skills, but must be part of a culture, a set of values which give meaning to man's life in society. lifelong learning has therefore become an imperative which cannot be underestimated by states, educational institutions and citizens. for these reasons, the celebration of the european year of lifelong learning was an important milestone and a symbolic moment in the awareness of this new reality on the part of europeans. the theorizing that preceded and accompanied the implementation of the european year and the movement of interest that it aroused on the part of schools and citizens' associations made an important contribution to this. lifelong learning has definitely entered the language of politicians, educators and entrepreneurs. and, most importantly, in common language. lifelong learning has also become a tool to combat school failure, drop-out and illiteracy. so today is a good cause. the initiative was therefore worthwhile, timely and fruitful. its objectives have been achieved, and what was lacking in money has been made up for by the mobilisation of goodwill and the support received. the boundary between primary education and other educational moments is now crossed. education ... mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to begin by congratulating my colleague, mr hernández mollar, on the excellent report he has presented to us, which the epp supports and which has received the almost unanimous support of the committee on civil liberties and human rights. this is a small step forward, but it reflects the beginning of a strategy of harmonization which we hope is under way in the framework of the third pillar. we have thus started the process of harmonization by setting up a secretariat common to the various supervisory authorities. we hope that in the medium term a regulation on common control standards and a single control body can be adopted. this is very important in order to ensure that the fundamental right of citizens to privacy is respected, while respecting the legal principle of fair and equal treatment of all citizens, with the application and interpretation of the rules according to the same criteria, thus achieving the same level of data protection. in fact, there is no reason to justify the existence of three separate common control bodies. today, in addition to the national supervisory authorities, there are also these three common supervisory authorities, each with its own secretariat. the question is: does this system really provide effective protection? i don't think he did. it is therefore necessary that these bodies should be merged as soon as possible into a single body with legal personality and its own budget, which will guarantee their impartiality and independence from the other institutions, as mr hernández mollar has just rightly pointed out. it is about defending the interests and rights of citizens. it is also imperative to create a single legal framework within the union that provides citizens with sufficient guarantees, preventing the disclosure and misuse of personal data and closing any gaps that arise. the constitutions in force in the various member states recognise the right to privacy and, on the other hand, the union, in its draft charter of fundamental rights, also expressly enshrines, in article 8, the right of citizens to the protection of personal data concerning them. however, the existence of different legislations raises a number of difficulties which could be overcome by the adoption of minimum standards which would make it possible to harmonize them, thus ensuring respect for the fundamental rights of citizens, in particular the right to privacy and the right to freedom of expression and information.
mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the increasing complexity of society and of economic life, supported by ever more powerful technological means, is creating new situations of unprotectedness and threats to the individual rights of citizens which require the establishment of appropriate mechanisms to prevent potential violations of these rights. the current system designed to protect the privacy of individuals by guaranteeing the inalienable right to privacy of all citizens with regard to the automated processing of their personal data is considered to be largely ineffective because there are still, in addition to the data protection supervisory authorities at national level, three common supervisory authorities in the european union, each with its own secretariat. the proposal for a decision of the portuguese council of ministers to counter this multiplicity of bodies at the level of supervision, which creates inefficiency, is to create a single secretariat for the three joint data protection supervisory authorities. this is admittedly a feasible solution in the present circumstances, which would reduce the inconvenience of having three common data protection supervisory authorities in the union. the rapporteur's suggestion that a single data protection supervisory authority be set up, with a single secretariat, with its own legal personality and budget, should also be welcomed. this would create the ideal conditions for pursuing the aims of protecting the privacy and individual freedoms of citizens, without prejudice to the free flow of information, which is a defining feature of modern societies. the rapporteur acknowledges that the creation of this single authority within the european union is not feasible in the short term, and in his report he refers to its realisation in the medium term under the third pillar. in congratulating the rapporteur on his work, i would like to draw attention to the risk that the creation of this single authority will be delayed for many, many years. perhaps it would have been more prudent to indicate in the report a specific and reasonable time limit for the establishment of this authority, which would have given more weight to the proposal of the rapporteur and the european parliament.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i shall not comment on the substantive issues of the proposal for a regulation which have been so thoroughly analysed and discussed in committee and in plenary, a process which culminated in the vote on parliament's opinion last november. but it is worth expressing, in line with the rapporteur, mr pirker, and the commission itself, our misunderstanding that in the new text the council proposes to reserve the power to implement the eurodac regulation, thus avoiding the previously envisaged system whereby such powers competed with the commission. on the one hand, because parliament is, as has already been said, suffering a reduction in its rights, in particular the right to be informed. the commission is generally obliged to report to parliament on the work of committees or on the submission of relevant proposals or measures to the council under article 7 of the decision on comitology. with this new text, that prerogative is obviously being eroded. secondly, because there is a lack of legal consistency in these transfers and, above all, a lack of legal support. article 1 of the comitology decision has not been respected and so article 202 of the treaty is being violated because it says nothing about the reasons which would justify adopting this procedure in this particular case. we are still guided by the general theory that the commission is the guardian of the treaties, equidistant from the member states, autonomous, independent and the defender of the common interest. it is for the commission, rather than the member states, to draw up, overcoming national barriers and particularisms, adequate protection rules for the handling of matters which conflict with fundamental freedoms. the council expressed its distrust of the commission. without wishing to be perverse, perhaps he is looking for a dispute on this matter, opening the door to almost certain appeal to the court of justice of the communities to delay the overall application of the new community rules which are on the way on the right of asylum. sometimes in politics, what looks like is! unfortunately, commissioner, and not through your fault, this is a matter on which you cannot give us a break.
mr president, i should like to begin by congratulating the rapporteur, mr peter skinner, on his efforts to moderate and reconcile the various interests so that parliament can make a positive and consensual contribution to improving this excellent commission proposal. neglect of work organisation, lack of adequate personal and collective safety equipment, poor training and the high number of self-employed are the main causes of high accident rates and deaths in the sector. the risks involved in construction are even greater when compared to other similar sectors. in the second half of the century, activity has grown rapidly, and the use of immigrant workers, subcontractors, is constant. in addition to the above, the problems of communication and organization are aggravated, with the tendency to neglect the collective safety of the construction site and to dilute responsibility. it is never too much to say that all the directives relating to the health and safety of workers and, in particular, the definition of liability contained in directive 89/655/eec are in force, and that the employer and the owner of the workplace are responsible for accidents that occur, but also for their prevention. the safety officer is responsible for assessing the risk and for checking that the safety equipment used by all persons who have access to the worksite, irrespective of their function, obligation or working time, has been checked. risk assessment has been done 'snap-on' in the european union, and this second review is a positive sign that we want to strongly support and encourage. even on an economic analysis, it is worrying to note that in the european union in 1996, 4 229 workers out of every 100 000 were absent for more than three days because of an accident at work. four million seven hundred thousand accidents occurred that year. fatal accidents, although they fell by 13%, which is not unknown to community action between 1994 and 1996, still accounted for 5 549 deaths in 1996. the estimated loss to the european economy in that year was 146 million lost working days. it is particularly relevant to the discussion of the directive i have already mentioned that the construction sector, although not the one with the highest number of accidents, has the highest number of fatal accidents, comparable only to the land transport sector. europe cannot be the most advanced social model in the world and allow people to count for little and the value of their lives to be reduced to a number. men, and especially young people up to the age of 25, are the main victims of occupational accidents, but the probability of death increases with age, being highest for those aged 55-64, highest in smaller enterprises (up to 50 workers) and in the self-employed, where the incidence rate is 73.1 of the total number of occurrences. this confronts us with the need for measures relating to fast-growing occupations, taking into account the qualification of young people, multidisciplinary solutions relating to training and qualification, as well as the quality of smes that exist in certain sectors. since the famous 1907 survey in the united states, which mr president, the commission's proposal to reduce refunds for processed agricultural products and to facilitate the inward processing system involves considerable risks. i will highlight just three: the first is that this proposal is not so much motivated by the restrictions imposed by the wto, but above all by the budgetary constraints that the european union is currently facing, namely the need to reallocate resources to external commitments it has made, such as kosovo. these objectives are not in question. it is necessary to take them on in a transparent way when the major debates on the options for european union are taking place. what is not certain is that the financial perspective for 2007 was adopted just over a year ago and that now, a few months later, we are withdrawing funds that were earmarked for agriculture. the second risk is that the increasingly frequent use of inward processing trade could lead to the extremely dangerous situation of no longer producing many agricultural products internally, as it would be more advantageous to import them raw, process them and re-export them. there would be advantages for only a few groups in society, for the rural world it would be a catastrophe. and in the long term, the european union itself as a whole would be extremely vulnerable because of its dependence on external supplies in such a strategic sector as food. i would therefore be deluded if i thought that trafficking in active improvement was a lasting solution. the third risk has to do with the strategy of the millennium round negotiations. the commission adopted this week a document on export competitiveness which reflects its position in principle on the various types of export support. and his position is very clear: in future, the european union will not be able to accept that only export refunds are reduced under the wto, but that all types of support used by our main competitors on international markets, from export credits to food aid and state trading companies, will have to be reduced. all this has to be 'put on the table' and reduced within the framework of gatt rules. the european union's position on this is quite correct: all types of aid having similar effects should fall within the scope of the wto discipline, not just the eu's system of refunds. and it is in the context of the external pressure that the european union is exerting in the gatt and wto negotiations that i think it was wrong to present this proposal at this stage. in conclusion, i would just like to thank my colleague dominique souchet for the enormous amount of work he has done, which we very much appreciate.
- thank you, madam commissioner diamantopoulou. - i'm not sure i can do that. i have received a motion for a resolution pursuant to rule 40 (5) of the rules of procedure. the debate is closed. the vote will take place at 11 a.m. today.

mr president, mr president-in-office of the council, mr commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, first of all i would like to thank my colleague, mr hubert pirker, for this excellent initiative and to express my support for the positions he has already taken, and i would like to congratulate the french presidency on the statement it has presented to us. i think that most of us recognize the need for greater coordination and closer cooperation between the member states on the problem of fighting crime. this action is a common responsibility given its international dimension, as this type of criminal organisation generally has a transnational action due to the high degree of mobility at its disposal, eventually taking advantage of the limitations of competences, the existing legal gaps and the differences between national administrative and criminal systems, exploiting the anomalies of the various systems. organized crime in the european union has developed significantly over the last decade, with action being required mainly in the areas of computer crime, trafficking in human beings, drugs and arms, terrorism, child pornography, money laundering, corruption and fraud. it is essential that the european union adopts a harmonised definition of the concept of organised crime covering these areas. the fight against the latter thus requires urgent action. this is a fight we must all fight. the european parliament must be involved in defining the strategies adopted and the priorities set.
