mr president, i too would like to congratulate my colleague peter william skinner on his excellent report, which deals with a subject which i really consider of the utmost importance. because if the european union is to become the region with the most dynamic economy in the world, as decided at the lisbon summit, it needs to find financing to support investments, especially in smes, which create jobs and are investing in sectors with a future. the aim is to encourage entrepreneurship in europe, but there are still many difficulties for those who want to be entrepreneurs in order to carry out profitable projects. the difficulties that need to be overcome quickly in the various countries of the european union begin with the administrative aspects, such as the bureaucracy, which is often useless and consumes time, energy and money, and end with its financing. the risk capital firms have a very important role to play in this area and the european union must move much faster if it is to catch up with the united states in particular. the commission proposal seems to us to be moving in the right direction, although i also believe that we must move faster, and i agree with the proposal made to ask the commission to present an action plan by 2000 in the field of venture capital and to adopt an integrated approach bringing together all the community instruments in a coherent way. above all, europe must increasingly focus on the merits of projects rather than on the personal assets of entrepreneurs, as is still the case today. the banking requirement for personal guarantees is very questionable, but it is not acceptable for venture capital companies. it is also necessary to make it easy for entrepreneurs to use risk capital, which implies much more active and effective information on how to use it.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i leave a few words here just to emphasize our great concern about timor. and to urge that the timor issue be very specifically included in the agenda of this next meeting. we all remember the tribute paid here almost a year ago to xanana gusmão, who was awarded the sakharov prize in this parliament. we have a permanent responsibility to xanana gusmão, to what he represents and to the people of timor. the situation has deteriorated recently. the very strong message i would like to convey to the commission and i ask that it be conveyed to the indonesian authorities is that any positive development in relations with indonesia depends on the guarantees that this country can actually give us as regards peace and freedom in timor.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. this extraordinary event, symbolized by the fall of the berlin wall, which separated two blocs but also separated a people, represented in a certain sense a pioneering step in the process of european enlargement towards central and eastern europe. despite the difficulties, contradictions and social and economic disparities which are still apparent between east and west germany, the truth is that a project of collective destiny of peace, freedom and development has been achieved, which would probably not have been possible if the voices of the prudent and those of the adherents of a certain techno-bureaucratic rationality had not been relegated to the background. it was as a result of this reunification that the copenhagen european council decided to integrate into the european union the associated states of central and eastern europe which wished to join. the copenhagen council of 1993 made it clear that the accession of the countries of central and eastern europe to the european union would depend on the fulfilment of the obligations inherent in their membership of the union. in other words, these states should fulfil a set of economic and political conditions considered necessary and necessary for integration. and on 30 march 1998 it was finally possible to start a negotiation process with the first group of countries, which included hungary, which is the subject of the progress report currently being debated in the european parliament. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i think i can say that this report, after being duly debated and enriched in the committee on foreign affairs, where it was unanimously approved, describes and analyses with sufficient precision the current stage of hungary's accession process, in the twofold perspective in which it approaches the question. on the one hand, it is an instrument for parliament's assessment of the periodic report drawn up by the commission in 1999 on progress made by the country. and on the other hand, as an expression and result of the view that the rapporteur has been building on the current situation in hungary. in this speech i shall try to highlight and systematize the six aspects of the development of the hungarian situation in the run-up to accession to the european union which deserve special mention. firstly, as regards compliance with the political criteria defined in copenhagen, the situation in hungary remains generally satisfactory, with a consolidation of democracy which reinforces the positive trends already noted. the problems are not therefore in the area of the exercise or defence of civil and political rights, but in the perspective of the most effective fight against some less positive aspects. in this context, the question of the integration of the roma into hungarian society was of great importance in the debate in the committee on foreign affairs. in our view, efforts to abolish any discrimination against the romanichel community should be focused on a series of positive measures, some of which have already been launched by the hungarian government through a medium-term action plan supported by the phare programme, mainly in the field of education and in specific programmes to support employment and housing. these policies must lead to the free, non-coercive assimilation of individuals belonging to the community. this is the sense we wanted to convey in this part of the report, and we cannot therefore fully agree with the text of recital c, which is the result of an amendment adopted in committee which stresses the persistence of a situation of segregation in the education of children and of serious discrimination in various sectors of society, the economy and the public sector which does not correspond to the perception of the current reality, which i have had the opportunity to observe. secondly, the economic situation is worthy of note, with growth picking up and hungary becoming the economic leader in the region. public finances were consolidated and the balance of payments improved markedly, while consumption improved as a result of wage increases, a fall in unemployment and a lower rate of inflation. thirdly, with regard to agriculture, whose productivity is still below that of the european union, it is important to ensure that the agricultural market is fully open to the capital needed for the privatisation of land, the modernisation of farms and marketing structures and the improvement of incomes. however, the prohibition on the acquisition of agricultural land by non-hungarian nationals is an obstacle to the fixing of a fair price for the same land. fourthly, there is the relevant question of the fight against organized crime, especially of russian origin. the high crime rate and the wide repercussions of this type of crime are perhaps the most serious domestic issue in hungary, despite the fact that the hungarian parliament has already voted on a set of texts on money laundering, the increase of penalties for drug trafficking and prostitution, the confiscation of funds and a witness protection programme for whistleblowers. the commission has also taken the view that the accession of hungary to the european union is a step in the right direction. finally, the environment and crossborder pollution problems arising from hungary's geographical situation are highlighted. the pollution of the danube and tisza rivers by cyanide discharges from romania is a classic example of this situation. i should like to leave a final word on the concerns expressed by some colleagues in the committee on foreign affairs about nuclear power stations of soviet origin. it is a matter which deserves serious consideration, but in a general framework covering not only hungary but also the other candidate countries which have this outdated and unsafe technology. i am therefore pleased to see that this subject has been taken up in mr brok's general report on enlargement. i would like to ask the house to hold an enlightening debate and to vote in a way which, by its expression and its content, contributes to the meeting between the old democracies of the west and the new democracies of the east, which is the ultimate objective of the process of enlargement of the european union.
- mr president, today's debate will be remembered as one of the most significant testimonies of the european parliament's work on the enlargement process of the union. i would like to thank the authors of the reports in particular, but also mr mocovici and mr verheugen for their speeches. they have succeeded in demonstrating realism and pragmatism in their analysis without losing sight of the ideal objectives which give the european project its true meaning. despite the obvious differences of opinion, we see an important consensus on fundamental issues. and among these i would distinguish the following: the full integration of each new country into the european union means a full commitment, combining economic convergence with adherence to a political project. this project has several features which must be complied with by the candidate countries and the present member states, otherwise enlargement will be a dilution. among the characteristics which must define the identity of the european project are: cohesion and solidarity between the member states, democracy and an ethic of responsibility, the social dimension and equal opportunities. finally, mr president, the fact that this debate is taking place at a time when germany is celebrating the tenth anniversary of its reunification should make us reflect. we defeated the ghosts of the past. and if the 20th century marked the extreme level of disunity in europe, with two terrible wars, the 21st century will mark, if we wish, the union of the peoples of europe. the european union is a union around a common project which is definitely a project for peace between europeans and europe for the world.
- mr president, i should like to begin by saying that this commission proposal is very welcome. it is also a longstanding desire of the european parliament, which already in 1997 and 1999 called for a review of the current legislation on the marketing of compound feedingstuffs. we all agree that there is a need for mandatory labelling which clearly indicates the different components of compound feedingstuffs and their nature. we shall of course not complicate matters and require unnecessary detail on the labels. but we shall require all that is indispensable, such as an explicit and open-ended declaration with a precise indication of the percentages of each component and its intrinsic nature. these are fundamental measures, as we all know, to give consumers confidence. indeed, there is a distrust that is particularly relevant in intensive production processes, not only in meat production but also in fish farming. and it is possible, through clear, transparent and responsible labelling, to give consumers that confidence. this subject was discussed during the portuguese presidency of the european union and the conclusion is that the council's position was unfortunately rather timid in this respect, rather uncourageous, and indicated that, after all, the ministers had not learned much from the past. it is essential that the commission and the council strive to ensure that these same rules apply to all our global partners, especially in the world trade organisation.
the rapporteur. - mr president, the reason for this amendment is that recital (c) stresses the persistence of a situation of segregation in the education of children and of serious discrimination in the various sectors of hungarian civil society, from the economy to the public sector, which does not correspond in its gravity to the perception of the current reality that i have had the opportunity to observe as rapporteur and during my visits. the wording i am now proposing is not intended to conceal or deny the existence of discrimination against the roma community, which unfortunately still exists, but it emphasizes the need to adopt a set of positive measures aimed at the free and non-coercive assimilation of the romani people into hungarian society. the recital would then be left with this text, which i read: ' (c) whereas the situation of the romani people continues to be neglected; whereas the discrimination against the roma must be taken seriously by the hungarian government by adopting a set of positive measures which can lead to the free assimilation of the roma; whereas a solution can be found by setting up a minorities office and by the adoption of legislation in the medium term which will promote the integration of the roma into hungarian society;' i should just like to make it clear to the house that this last part is the transcript of the recital as it was expressed in the report and approved in committee.
in writing. - (de) i voted in favour of the report as a whole on enlargement to the east for the following reasons: 2. i wanted to send a positive signal to the applicant countries, given that they have made enormous progress in the context of the envisaged enlargement. despite the problems that undoubtedly exist, including those with regard to the present member states, i believe that the opportunities offered by this enlargement process are overwhelming. as a member of the eu-slovenia joint parliamentary committee, i would like to emphasize that i consider slovenia's level of achievement in the field of the acquis communautaire to be exemplary, and this is also reflected in the report. as regards the requirements for retrocession and the situation of the court, i take a somewhat different view from the rapporteur, since my experience as an east german, ten years after the unification of the two german states, shows that these difficulties persist in the land of brandenburg as well. this difficult process objectively requires up to twenty years. i cannot agree with the amendments tabled here for the following reasons:• it has already been established on several occasions that slovenia fulfils the copenhagen criteria; • the question of shutting down the nuclear power plant is not part of the acquis; • the consequences of the second world war can be seen in a completely different way, but we cannot expect them to be brought into line with the political conceptions explicitly set out 50 years later in the copenhagen criteria.
in writing. - (pt) the importance and scale of the problems raised by enlargement are not compatible with the double language used in the european union. it is absolutely unacceptable to have opted for ease and to have created false expectations in the candidate countries, while at the same time and in order to delay things, an impassable wall of difficulties and conditions was erected, or obstacles were invoked that were known from the outset, to the point of proposing studies on the costs of non-enlargement. we have always defended the logic of truth, however crude it may be. that is why, although we have no objection in principle to enlargement, we have stated from the outset - from the beginning of the process - that the final position would depend on the conditions under which enlargement is achieved. we have said this because we have always known that there would be difficulties on all levels. it goes without saying, and it is inevitable, that these difficulties can only be overcome by strong political will, new financial means and, above all, a careful analysis of their impact on the institutional, economic, financial and social levels, both for the present member states and for the candidate countries. we repeat, for example, that it is unacceptable that enlargement should be carried out at the expense of the less developed member states - as the absence of a revision of the financial perspective implies - or by means of institutional change, as some have advocated.
in writing. - (pt) food safety requires that increasing attention be paid to animal feedingstuffs, given the close relationship between animal feedingstuffs and human feedingstuffs. hence the importance of the amendments to directive 1999/29/ec to cover all products in whatever form which may be used in animal feed, both those intended for the production of raw materials for food and those intended for the feeding of domestic animals, which are not intended for food. it is also positive to prohibit the dilution of batches of products with a content of undesirable substances exceeding the maximum limits laid down for non-harmful products, in order to circumvent the maximum limits laid down. the setting of maximum limits must take account of various aspects, such as the continuous emergence of new chemicals and the need for periodic reviews based on accurate scientific analysis and proven experience; or, in some cases, the need to compensate economic operators, especially farmers, in special situations where they are not responsible for the contamination of products used in animal feed.
in writing. - (pt) it is important to make full and detailed declaration of the raw materials of feed compounds mandatory in order to guarantee their safety, through clear and transparent labelling enabling farmers to make their decision on the purchase of feed. as you know, since 1997, following the bse crisis, the european parliament has been calling for legislation on this subject, a request which was reiterated after the scandal in belgium last year with the presence of dioxin in animal feed. it is also important to establish an exhaustive list of authorised raw materials and for feed manufacturers to prove the composition of feed on request by means of internal company documents.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, as is well known, there is still a lot of discrimination in the european union, particularly in employment and occupation, despite the fact that it is prohibited by article 13 of the treaty on european union. it is therefore important that the two legislative initiatives now under discussion be advanced, albeit improved in some respects and increased in the amount of money provided for the financing of the programme, reflecting the political importance of this fight against discrimination. it is not enough that equality is guaranteed by law. we all know that the causes of discrimination must be eliminated from the outset - and the fight against discrimination is equally very important - whether by information, education and training to promote equality and combat prejudice, by changing mindsets, whether through various forms of positive action, particularly favouring discriminated groups, or by resorting to the competent authorities whenever employers persist in discrimination. as regards the burden of proof, it is important that it is the employer's responsibility to prove that there has been no breach of the principle of equality, whereas the employee will only have to present the case of discrimination to which he has been subject.
- mr president, i would like to stress what my colleague mr hernández mollar has just said. this fight against discrimination is a fight that involves all of us and cannot be the banner of any single bench in this parliament. article 13 of the treaty of amsterdam created a challenge to the european union's agenda which came to give the community specific powers to initiate a varied set of anti-discrimination actions so that the same level of protection is ensured in respect of all disadvantaged groups. i would like to congratulate the commission on the important step it has taken in starting this article 13 process with its package of anti-discrimination proposals. experience has also shown that the promotion of legislative measures to combat discrimination is essential but not sufficient. these measures must be accompanied by prevention and behaviour and attitude changes, as is clear from the results of the relatively recent eurobarometer survey, which found that one in three europeans, around 33%, considered themselves to be very or somewhat racist. it is essential to teach and promote respect for multicultural diversity in europe. i would also like to stress the importance of involving the applicant countries as much as possible in these community measures to combat discrimination.
- mr president, the proposal for a council decision establishing a community action programme to combat discrimination is the beginning of the article 13 procedure. this is a major task whose positive impact will sooner or later be reflected directly or indirectly in the reality of the member states in the form of specific programmes and measures, or even legislative changes which the evolution of societies has called for and to which political power has often been unable to give satisfactory answers. our common civilization affirms its greatness in respecting the rules of the open, tolerant and liberal society, in its inclusive and multicultural dynamics. however, the generous idea of granting every citizen the greatest possible freedom, provided it is compatible with an equal measure of freedom for others, still today runs into obstacles - some in fact, others in law. when faced with the dogmatism, prejudice and ignorance which still today cast so many of our fellow citizens into a status of sub-citizenship, europe finds itself with the best of itself: the tradition of enlightenment and individual and collective emancipation. the family, for example, remains a foundation and a pillar of society. but how many of the member states of the union still accept in their legislation static and dogmatic conceptions of the family, discriminating against de facto unions and, within them, in a particularly shocking and anachronistic way, those made up of homosexual couples? the family must now finally be understood as a community of affections, rather than the civil effect of a contract blessed by religion. the fight against discrimination we are talking about today is above all about affirming an inclusive citizenship, which is more demanding than the exclusive citizenship of the past, historically linked to the origins of democracy and the republic.

- mr president, commissioner, minister, ladies and gentlemen, as the commission's reports acknowledge, there are still major unresolved issues in the field of equal opportunities for men and women. in virtually all areas of activity, deep inequalities remain. despite the diversity of situations in the member states, women are generally less present than men on the labour market, and are the most affected by unemployment, including long-term unemployment, precarious, part-time, atypical, insecure and poorly paid work. the average wage of women remains on average about 28% lower than that of men, which contributes to women being the majority of the poor population and hinders their participation in social and political life. however, since quality employment with rights is a fundamental basis for ensuring equality between men and women and guaranteeing their autonomy on an equal footing with men, the causes of this situation must be analysed and the necessary measures taken to overcome it, particularly in employment, training, education and family and child support infrastructure policies, giving priority to this issue in practice and not just in declarations of intent. women are still in the minority in decisionmaking, both at political, professional and trade union level. and if this situation is partly explained by the difficulties women face in the labour market and in the family, it is also true that political parties must assume their democratic responsibility to ensure equal opportunities for women in political life and in the decision-making process, in order to put an end to the scandalous situation which still persists at the beginning of the new millennium.

in writing. - (pt) although the european union recognises the freedom of movement, provision of services and establishment of the citizens of the member states, there is still a great gap between the texts and the reality of life, for example in relation to inequality in the reimbursement of health care, the risk of double taxation, the random nature of social benefits, the non-recognition of diplomas and the complexity of legal texts, which make it very difficult for a layman to know what his rights actually are. as the rapporteur says, the commission seems to realise that if no concrete measures are adopted, certain fundamental freedoms enshrined in the treaty, such as the right of residence and the right of free movement, risk being translated into practice by mere statements of principles. the commission must take ambitious measures to guarantee freedom of residence and establishment, including, among other things, the right to work, to study and to have one's qualifications recognised in other member states.
- mr president, the need for this resolution on what happened in peru meant two things. the frustration of having to return to monitoring democracy and human rights in latin america, which had been a central concern of this parliament in the 1980s and which had abated in the 1990s with the emergence of democratic governments in most of its countries, was evident. frustration also because, in addition to peru, other attempts at aggressive populism are generating dictatorships that justify all our fears and re-impose latin america as the reference point for our democratic surveillance. let 's be very frank about this . the resolution expresses the feeling of the fragility of europe's influence in this subcontinent. with a clear conscience about the democratic developments of the 1990s, europe has done little for these countries, apart from giving advice on models of economic integration. the opening up of our markets was symbolic - with the exception of meat from argentina, because of bse, as we know - the technological exchange was scarce, cultural and political relations were only verbal, vocational training and value-added investments were not accelerated. europe has not taken advantage of the emerging democracies to promote development and social justice in latin america, to free these countries from their almost exclusive dependence on the accumulation of capital based on drug trafficking. peru and fujimori, mr president, are not yet a catastrophe: they are the disappointment and, for now, a warning. to avoid this, it is absolutely necessary that europe should also make policy there, on the american subcontinent.
- mr president, commissioner, minister, ladies and gentlemen, the citizens of the european union expect more than just vague promises which are then forgotten and rarely translated into binding instruments and decisions which actually improve the quality of life and work. the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for adoption. the council must not leave pending a number of concrete proposals which have been postponed year after year, concerning essential decisions in terms of strengthening the rights of pregnant and breastfeeding workers, the collective and individual rights of workers, including the right to information, consultation and participation, particularly in the framework of the statute of the european company and the european works council, to ensure the rights of workers and prevent the redundancies which the successive mergers and relocations of multinationals are causing in various countries in europe. as our rapporteur, mrs anne van lancker, has pointed out, although the social dialogue is important, it must be able to produce something concrete. if this is not the case, the commission should propose legislative instruments and the council should adopt the necessary measures, including amending macroeconomic policies and the stability pact to give credibility to the fight against poverty and social exclusion, as well as to the defence of quality employment with decent wages and reforms that give people dignity.
- mr president, i would like to say briefly that i think the duhamel report is a really excellent document, which makes very substantial progress. i would like it to go further and speak directly of a constitution and not just of a framework treaty, a kind of single treaty, a treaty of treaties, including, however, the declaration of fundamental rights. in any case, i admit that there were no political conditions to go that far and therefore i wholeheartedly support the duhamel report now under discussion. there is, however, one point i would like to stress: i do not think it is right to demand or to go towards requiring a mandatory referendum in all the member states just to approve the proposed amendments. we in portugal, for example, did not hold a referendum to approve our accession to the european community, we did not hold a referendum to approve the maastricht treaty, all this was approved in parliament in terms of effective representative democracy. i do not see that we have to force all the member states, regardless of their pro-referendum or anti-referendum traditions, to hold a referendum. this is the only reservation i'm making. in any case, i warmly congratulate the rapporteur, olivier duhamel, on the excellent work he has done.
in writing. - (pt) it is good to confirm that full employment is the priority objective pursued by the european union's economic, social and employment policy strategy, but we must not stop at vague declarations and introduce real changes in macroeconomic and social policies to move effectively towards full quality employment in practice. it is also good to confirm the need to discourage early school leaving by providing appropriate support for young people with learning difficulties and by raising compulsory education or training to 18 years of age, but the essential measures to ensure access to quality education and training for all young people must be taken, with particular attention to families with low economic resources and the most vulnerable segments of the population, which also requires adequate budgets, which are still lacking. it is important to recall the need to reduce by 50% the gender gap in employment, unemployment, wages and sectoral segregation in the member states over the next five years, but it is essential that practice shows a real improvement in quality jobs, wages and career advancement, which is not yet the case.
in writing. - (pt) this report is part of the european commission's policy of giving the utmost priority to competition policy, even when the fundamental rights of workers are called into question or privatisations are carried out in its name, by transforming de facto public enterprises, which are accused of being of a monopolistic nature, into genuine private monopolies and, often, by calling into question public services essential to improving the quality of life of the population, as happened in portugal. the report is completely devoid of social considerations; the author goes so far as to say that 'when it comes to whether a behaviour is restrictive of competition, the focus should be on the harm caused to competition and not to economic actors; the case should be based on an economic analysis". it also defends the concentration of enterprises, including large ones, under the pretext of international competitiveness, but ignores the social consequences of this situation. it was therefore inevitable that we should vote against this report.
- mr president, it is fair to begin by distinguishing the work of the various rapporteurs. and having followed more closely the way in which the rapporteur, mrs jutta haug, has been handling the matters under her responsibility, i want to testify to the wisdom and balance she has always shown. rather than carrying out the technical analysis of the budget or reading the amounts provided for in the respective revenue and expenditure columns, parliament should be concerned with the quality of the expenditure actually and proposed, which implies, in particular, the evaluation of the criteria for allocating funds and also the evaluation of their impact. on the basis of this understanding, the epp-ed group distinguishes the following parameters:- the level of budgetary implementation;- the degree of implementation of the policies defined and the results obtained;- the coherence between the legislative process and the budgetary options;- the progress of the reform of the commission. the commission has already taken a number of measures to improve the situation. firstly, by referring to the low levels of budget implementation, in particular expenditure classified as 'non-compulsory', a special requirement is introduced in requests for explanations to the commission. it is accepted that, as long as the explanations requested are not given or are not satisfactory, it will not be possible to use the funds under consideration. but more than that, without the actual use of the budgeted funds, how can we talk about reinforcements, which in some categories would be so necessary? secondly, in view of the systematic increase in the balance to be settled - the gni - resulting from the growing gap between commitments made, commitments obtained and payments made, particularly in category 4 'external actions', greater speed, transparency and efficiency in administration must be required. the needs are obvious, the commitments are many, the contribution of external cooperation to the consolidation of the european union is clear, the implementation of various programmes is very low. the meda programme, which is devoted to the mediterranean basin, a geographical area of major strategic importance, is a good example of a lack of capacity for implementation. we must make a recovery in the meda and ensure other effectiveness in serbia, for example, while at the same time effectively implementing the european union's traditional policies in this area. in order to overcome the existing bottlenecks, a general reserve is established in category 4 - a performance reserve - which will be used in proportion to the degree of implementation of the various headings. the commission is also expected to revise the financial regulation to set a clear deadline, for example two years, as the maximum period between the authorization of expenditure and its payment, otherwise the amounts involved will be withdrawn from the budget, as is already the case in other categories. the third point to be noted is that, in order to achieve greater coordination between the european institutions, the formal and substantive link between the commission's legislative programme and the budgetary procedure is encouraged, as is the prior information to parliament of decisions of other institutions which have a budgetary impact. fourthly, in the light of the reform of the commission's procedures and staff policy, we must distinguish between new recruitment and the desirable - in our view - creation of a system of permanent and compulsory pre-retirement. fifthly, by giving priority to initiative and responsibility, the creation of a programme for enterprises and entrepreneurs is being promoted. in keeping with this strategic approach to the budgets for this year and the next, the european parliament fully assumes its place in the budgetary procedure. parliament is truly representing its electors, an essential element of democracy, when it refuses to be a mere formal endorser of the proposals of others.
- mr president, it is clear that the present financial perspective does not correspond to the priorities and commitments that have been made. and the heart of parliament's strategy should be to force the council to provide new means for the new objectives, so as to avoid pursuing them at the expense of previous priorities. and the european parliament has the authority to demand a revision of the financial perspective, especially in the case of a council budget proposal which has brazenly brought us the lowest budget in the last decade in relative terms. failure to do so will inevitably lead to 'financial engineering', redistributions and cuts in both general expenditure, social and environmental expenditure and cooperation, and it is certain that the appropriations for the balkans will nevertheless remain insufficient. this is not to mention the horizontal reservation, which will result in unacceptable constraints on cooperation policy, the cohesion fund, or even in the case of timor, where there are firm commitments from the european union's point of view in international terms. we obviously value some aspects, in particular the fact that the 2000 amounts for development policies have been recovered. but that is not enough to change our overall negative assessment of the proposed budget as insufficient, and hence the demand for a revision of the financial perspective.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, in welcoming the excellent work of mrs paciotti, i would like to stress the need for us to move quickly on this matter, which was in fact the aim of completing this dossier at first reading. in fact, in the modern society in which we live, huge volumes of personal data are collected, stored and processed on a daily basis. for this reason, it is legitimate for european citizens to be concerned about whether or not their privacy is being protected and to hope that a balanced solution will be found that safeguards and guarantees their rights and freedoms. none of the two existing directives in the european union aimed at ensuring protection against the misuse of personal data applies to the community institutions. it is therefore essential and urgent to translate the amsterdam agreement into practice by adopting a regulation conferring legally protected rights on individuals, laying down the obligations of data controllers within the community institutions and bodies in relation to the processing of personal data and finally establishing an independent supervisory body responsible for monitoring the processing of personal data by the community institutions and bodies. the creation of this supervisory body, the european data protection authority, should be pursued, with the expectation that in the medium term it will be given new powers which should include those currently exercised by the other supervisory authorities, and the creation of a single secretariat to ensure uniform treatment of cases subject to control.

- doc. a5-0285/2000 by mrs sudre, on behalf of the committee on regional policy, transport and tourism, on the report from the commission on measures to implement article 299 (2): the outermost regions of the european union (com (2000) 147 - c5-0247/00 - 2000/ 2135 (cos)).
- mr president, commissioner, with this report the european parliament is contributing to the relaunch of a policy for the outermost regions, as proclaimed in article 299 (2) of the treaty. the commission's recent action in this field has been met with some hesitation and some contradiction. the articles of the treaty are all to be respected, all must be guaranteed effectiveness, and article 299 (2) cannot be an exception. there is therefore reason to distinguish this position of the european parliament and to praise the balance, competence and experience demonstrated by the rapporteur, margie sudre, and the contribution of all those who collaborated with her, in particular the other seven members from the outermost regions. i'm going to try to distinguish the essential. as well as promoting the equity that underpins policy modulation, the derogation regimes and the specific measures, treating what is the same in the same way and what is different in a different way, what is important to me is that in my region, as in the other outermost regions, people live with dignity, which presupposes the exercise of productive activities that ensure an increasing degree of self-sufficiency. since the economy of the outermost regions is characterised in article 299 (2) of the treaty as being dependent on a single product or a small number of products, possible diversification should be encouraged while at the same time creating better conditions for producing what can and knows how to be produced. what we must not do is to contribute to the elimination of our few comparative advantages. in the azores, we produce mainly milk. i can only hope that, in accordance with article 299 (2) of the treaty, our conditions will improve so that we can produce what we can and know how to produce.
- mr president, this parliament - and in particular its committee on regional policy - has been the great driving force behind the adoption of specific policies and programmes for the outermost regions. the unanimous approval by the members of the committee on regional policy of the report by our colleague margie sudre represents the fidelity of the european parliament to one of its most important commitments. i would therefore like to congratulate the rapporteur and the committee on regional policy on the excellent work they have done. the entry into force of the treaty of amsterdam clarified the legal basis and broadened the scope of these specific policies. one of the areas where the impact of this approximation has become clearer has been in the agricultural policy. through partial derogations from the principles of the cap and the implementation of specific programmes for the development of agriculture, the desertification of archipelagos such as the azores has been brought to a halt and the rural island world has seen its living conditions improved. the experience of almost a decade and the new challenges posed by the evolution of the common agricultural policy suggest a review of these programmes in order to maintain the capacity of these regions to survive in the new competitive environment. the treaty expressly states that economic dependence on a small number of products is one of the constraints on the european institutions to adopt specific measures for the implementation of common policies. the dependence of agriculture and the entire economy of the azores on milk production is reaching unparalleled levels throughout the union. that is why we in the azores all hope that the commission, in the light of the treaty and in the light of parliament's position, will quickly propose the necessary adjustments to the cap in its application to the azores.
- mr president, commissioner, if it had not been for the inclusion of article 299 (2) in the treaty of amsterdam, we would not be here today debating this excellent report by mrs margie sudre, whom i would like to congratulate on her excellent work. this is a very important rule. the outermost regions have, with this article, obtained the recognition by the european union of a genuine right to be different, a right to specific and special treatment in accordance with their unique reality, which is totally different from that of the rest of the territory of the european union. it is now a question of legal and constitutional recognition, and no longer, as was the case before amsterdam, of recognition by means of declarations annexed to the treaties, which have a purely political value. it should be noted that this right to be different is nothing more than an emanation of the principle of equality, which also requires that what is different be treated differently. i reject retrograde and exaggerated interpretations of the principle of equality which lead to the equal treatment of what is unequal. article 299 (2) is thus a key instrument to: first, help promote equal opportunities for european citizens living in the outermost regions; second, promote the good and full integration of the outermost regions into the european union and the dynamics of the large internal market; and third, enable the outermost regions to respond in the best possible way to the challenges posed by globalisation and the enlargement of the european union, to which they are particularly vulnerable. that is the only reason for article 299 (2), not to guarantee any privileged status. mr president, mr commissioner, the qualitative leap represented by article 299 (2) must be matched by a qualitative leap in the different treatment which the european union must give to the outermost regions. and that you have to go beyond posei. this qualitative progress must be reflected in the definition and implementation of a comprehensive and coherent strategy by the european union, as proposed, and very well, in the margie sudre report. the outermost regions and the citizens living there look forward to the action of the european institutions in implementation of article 299 (2).
- thank you, commissioner barnier. - thank you very much. the debate is closed. the vote will take place tomorrow at 12 noon. the next item is the report (doc. (the sitting was suspended at 12 noon)
- mr president, we are all aware - and this has just been mentioned by our colleagues emma bonino and luisa morgantini - of the scale of the tragedy resulting from the use of anti-personnel mines and the seriousness of the problem given the large number of mines which are still being placed and activated on the ground. some even say 70 to 100 million. the ottawa convention is therefore both important and opportune, since it seeks to develop concerted action by the international community to put an end to this tragedy, which particularly affects the least developed countries. even if there are still difficulties because some important states - and i have already mentioned them - have not yet signed. and in this connection we cannot but regret the fact that one member state, finland, has not yet signed it and another, greece, has not yet ratified it, as our colleague luisa morgantini has just mentioned. and here the first question arises: the need for the european union, and in particular the commission, to do everything possible to get the non-signatory countries to sign the ottawa convention. it is desirable that the commission should tell us what it intends to do in this area, particularly with regard to the applicant countries. but obviously its authority in this area will be greater if the council and the member states state explicitly that the european union will under no circumstances use or assist in the use of anti-personnel mines in its external action. in the meantime, it is essential to ensure maximum coherence and coordination between the actions of the european union and those of the member states. the commission can and must play an invaluable role in this area too. and in that respect too, you must tell us what you intend to do in the future. we also believe that community action in this field should be given adequate budgetary support in order to be more effective. the commission proposal for a regulation seems to us to be too vague. we are clearly in favour of creating a single budget line for mine action, with the exception of research and food aid, and we think it would be useful to create a central unit for the coordination of all the measures in this field at the same time, as our colleague emma bonino has said. as regards the measures to be taken, it is essential to ensure that the existing mines, the huge quantity of mines in storage, are destroyed and that they are not placed on the black market. how equally indispensable it is to end their production. other mine clearance or victim assistance will be of little use if we continue to produce and sell mines. it is in the field of prevention that action must be taken first. secondly, more investment is needed in research, particularly in the detection of landmines. and finally, it is absolutely essential to strengthen mine clearance operations by ensuring the effectiveness of the measures taken and giving priority to countries which respect the convention, just as it is essential to ensure measures for the rehabilitation of victims. we would also like the commission to explain in more detail what it intends to do in these areas, taking into account past experience and the important role that the european union has assumed in this area. the field of action is of course very wide and requires means and great political will. the challenge is enormous, but the scale of this drama requires enormous effort and commitment to put an end to the situation.
mr president, years ago, when the international campaign to clear mines began, it was said that mines were the atomic bomb of the poor countries. despite the passage of time, this apparent exaggeration can still serve, unfortunately, to illustrate a reality. there is no development without safe lines of communication, without proper land to be cultivated and without places where children can play freely. the european union has therefore joined, and even taken a leading role in, the international campaign launched by the united nations, as has already been mentioned by previous speakers. the merit of the proposal before us, which is the subject of mrs emma bonino's report, is now clear. we support this report and all the initiatives to revive the european union's role in this area. we stress that this requires a more coherent european union effort involving finland and greece, that the european union coordinates all its international pressure capacity on those countries which have not yet acceded to the ottawa convention, and that the european effort becomes more effective, including through increased funding, taking into account the need for safe demining, the systematic destruction of existing stocks and the further development of advanced technologies for detection and demining. the commission's proposal is to make the european parliament responsible for the implementation of the budget. and finally, we hope that the future tasks of the european joint military force will include the training of technicians, particularly those with a local interest in the removal of mines.
mr president, i abstained from voting on the resolution on the outcome of the informal european council in biarritz for two reasons: firstly, because i had the opportunity to vote nominally, separately, on paragraphs 2 and 5, which allowed my outright opposition to both to be expressed. i am not in favour of incorporating the charter of fundamental rights into the treaty, since that would be the first step towards constitutionalising it, a federalising process which i do not support, nor am i in favour of the indiscriminate generalization of qualified majority voting, a measure which, when dissociated from the others under discussion, could conflict with relevant national interests and is even contrary to the request expressed in paragraph 7 of the resolution. it was precisely this paragraph 7 which allowed me to abstain. with this vote i do not intend to support any initiative which would put an end to the atmosphere of battle between large and small countries in which the debate on institutional reform has been taking place, a climate which the french presidency has unfortunately been one of the main instigators of. as stated in paragraph 7, it is absolutely necessary to ensure that solutions are valid and acceptable to all member states, regardless of their size or economic weight. we are europeans, and we want to remain so, but always with due regard for the founding principle of a union of free and sovereign states.
in writing. - (pt) i voted for this resolution because parliament does not favour the 'imperialist' theses of the europe of the large populated countries over the affirmation of the other countries. the resolution wisely limits itself to a responsible vote in the search for a balance of common sense, drawing on the history of the community. those who would like to see the parliament "echo" the (revisionist) claims of the most populous countries are at their own expense.
in writing. - (pt) the portuguese socialist members voted for the duhamel report on the constitutionalization of the treaties, essentially because they agree with the need for the european union to simplify and organise the basic texts governing it, which are now almost illegible because of the complexity introduced by the successive accumulation of treaties. it is essential that the european union moves towards a constitution which incorporates the charter of fundamental rights. the existence of a full democracy in the european union would benefit from the adoption of a constitution which is clear, simple, readable and represents a reference for all citizens. the portuguese socialist meps in the european parliament, however, do not agree that this 'constitution' should necessarily be the subject of a referendum. this does not mean that we are against the use of the referendum, nor that the political deepening of the european union should not give rise to a popular consultation of the portuguese. this choice must be free and independent, it must not be imposed, which could run counter to the constitutional practice of each country. that is why portuguese socialist members voted against the proposal of their own political group, which favours a uniform referendum model for all the member states of the european union. in order to achieve the objective of a european constitution - which we want - we must avoid imposing rigid schemes and methodologies which would inevitably lead to the failure of the desired objective. in such a delicate matter, boldness and prudence must go hand in hand.
in writing. - (pt) without denying the laudable intention to strengthen, clarify and deepen the european union and to simplify the texts in order to bring them closer to the citizens, i did not vote for this resolution. it is essentially a "pandora's box" because the text does not clearly state what the european constitution is for or why it is really necessary or urgent. by its very nature, a constitution, as it is sometimes contradictorily defended in europe, can either serve to advance the 'civilization' of the union, based on democracy and citizenship, or serve as an instrument for 'clarifying' competences, a circle of chalk which formally and definitively confines the union and its organs to very particular and specific competences. the constitutionalisation of the treaties can mean either the development of the union and the defence of its political competences or, on the contrary, it can be nothing more than a sacred formalisation of the principle of subsidiarity. it is strange that many members, including prime minister blair, are defending the european constitution. despite the clarity of mário soares' speech, which interpreted this report in its profound european sense, without making concessions to the ease of the very risky referendum and not relying on any tradition in many member states, this is indeed the clear interpretation of a convinced european and the accomplished story. unfortunately, the text does not follow the design and will of the first european of the portuguese. my distrust of the text and the political impossibility of voting on it is based on this divergence, for which the rapporteur is solely responsible because of its lack of clarity and precision. i would have voted against it, if it had not been a very radical way of expressing an opinion on a text of such importance.
in writing. - (pt) the european parliament and the ppe group in particular have always contributed to the affirmation of the concept of ultraperiphery. this was the case with the gútierrez díaz report, the fernández martín report, which argued for the inclusion of this concept in the treaty, and now the sudre report, which is relaunching the european policy for the outermost regions. in the face of the hesitations and contradictions of the commission, which have resulted in the disappearance of regis, the delay in the revision of the posei and the reduction of their respective amounts, the sudre report seems very timely. the european parliament calls for measures to be taken in various fields, from agriculture to fisheries, from the environment to transport and energy, and from the information society to the information society, with the necessary budgetary support. at the same time, the productive sectors are listed, which means that, in the case of the azores, the milk and sugar disputes must be settled. i quote paragraph 7 of the sudre report which states that 'it is essential that specific adaptations of the common policies be developed when the outermost regions are heavily dependent on a limited number of economic activities or even on a single economic activity, in accordance with article 299 (2) of the treaty'. we are now awaiting the initiatives of the commission and of the national and regional authorities.
in writing. - (pt) this report seems to us to be most timely in view of the need to give an objective and rapid translation to article 299 (2) of the treaty on european union, and we subscribe, in the main, to its guidelines for the sustainable development of the outermost regions. the particular characteristics of these regions, which are generally due to their insularity and remoteness from the european continent, but also to their chronic dependence on a limited number of products or even on a single product, require specific measures to overcome these constraints. we therefore welcome the fact that the report has adopted guidelines which are largely in line with the proposals we have put before the committee of this parliament. but we also stress that the report does not pay enough attention to the specific social situations affecting these regions or some of them, which also require special measures. this could have been resolved by adopting the amendment which we have tabled to that effect.
in writing. - (pt) the serious crises and scandals affecting various countries of the european union in relation to food products, due to the pursuit of maximum profit in agri-food activities, in disregard of the interests of consumers, constitute a serious problem for society and food security. all the measures proposed to change the situation and defend consumers deserve support, including the creation of a european safety authority for scientific risk analysis, working closely with the existing national agencies in this field and the main european consumer organisations. but we have to go further. account should be taken of the type of agricultural and agro-industrial practices, problems of importing foodstuffs, the composition of feed ingredients, maximum levels of dioxins and pesticide residues, food components and others, giving priority to food safety. support for regional crops and products, family farming and rural development is also essential.

- the next item is the report (a5-0296/2000) by mr van hecke, on behalf of the committee on foreign affairs, human rights, common security and defence policy, on the communication from the commission to the council and the european parliament: cooperation with acp states involved in armed conflicts (com (99) 240 - c5-0115/1999 - 1999/2118 (cos)).
mr president, mr van hecke's report raises a fundamental question, namely the extent to which international aid to countries in conflict is a factor in that conflict and the extent to which such aid favours corrupt and belligerent governments. i would like to mention two examples of the harmful role of unconditional international aid in relation to one of the poorest and most unfortunate areas in the world. i am referring to a country which has been in constant war since the 1960s and to a country which has a precarious peace, but which international aid can perversely damage, namely angola and mozambique. in angola, the government and unita are waging one of the most brutal civil wars in africa, and unfortunately, it is clear that international aid, in particular to the government in luanda, has played a role in perpetuating that conflict. unfortunately, i regret that my own government, the portuguese government, is involved in this military effort. in the case of mozambique, where there is a precarious peace achieved with great difficulty, the use of international aid by the ruling party to favour certain provinces over others on the basis of electoral results may in itself be a major factor in the crisis of the hard-won peace some years ago and in the resumption of civil conflict in mozambique. and this is a matter of major concern to the european union and the donor countries: whether in certain countries such as mozambique international aid is being used as an instrument of power to legitimize a government that is on the edge of democracy and, from this point of view, to help bring an end to a very precarious peace. these are the issues raised in the report and they should be given considerable thought. signals. outwardly, it has regenerated the european union's leadership capacity. inside, he did the pedagogy of the compliment. let us start by looking at the green paper on emissions trading. this green paper launched the debate on the architecture of a future emissions trading system in the european union. the commission has defined a hard core of assumptions for the start of the system: the system should be based on a learning by doing approach, should start in 2005 and should, in a first phase, be limited to carbon dioxide and large point sources. in our view, and this is reflected in the report, the implementation of such a system in the european union as from 2005 has undeniable advantages. let me highlight three: firstly, this system will provide practical learning and economic preparation for sectors, companies and member states to start the international emissions trading system in 2008. secondly, this system will help to make it easier to correct the situation of non-compliance which is currently found in most member states. thirdly, the system will make it possible to reduce substantially, by billions of euros a year, the costs of implementing the commitments made by the community in kyoto. despite the good nature of this initiative and the technical quality of the green paper, for which we congratulate the commission, the approach to some points has been somewhat limited. the function of this report was therefore to broaden and deepen the debate initiated by the green paper. that's our job. let me highlight six points from this reflection i have made. firstly, the green paper should have set quantified greenhouse gas reduction targets to be met by the emissions trading system, that is, the green paper should have said what percentage of the greenhouse gas reduction would be attributed to emissions trading. secondly, we believe that the green paper should not have avoided analysing whether the emissions trading system would give nuclear energy competitive advantages. thirdly, we believe it is essential that in future the system should cover some sectors which the green paper has already excluded, namely transport, which is the sector where emissions are growing at the fastest rate. fourthly, the commission should not have set direct emissions trading between companies as the only possible option in the debate on the scope of the system. there are other options, and we hope that the commission will soon consider all the alternatives. fifthly, we consider it essential to involve the countries applying for membership of the european union in the emissions trading system as soon as possible. we therefore call on the commission to present in a future communication a plan for how to involve these countries in the emissions trading system. sixth and lastly, we believe that, as regards the method and allocation of emission allowances, auctioning can more easily ensure the desired environmental outcome, non-distortion of competition and simplicity of the system than grandfathering. however, this is not the time to exclude any method, and we therefore invite the commission to continue this reflection and to extend it to a third option: benchmarking. let us now look at the communication on the european programme on climate change. given the commitment and quality of the green paper on emissions trading, the communication on policies and measures, the communication 'towards a european agenda for climate change' is a disappointment. it is a disappointment because it results in a subordination of the role of policies and measures, that is, of domestic and community political action to market mechanisms. it is also disappointing because this communication results in an imbalance of treatment between the different economic sectors; in particular, it was hoped that those sectors which were not included in the emissions trading system would see an ambitious approach adopted with well-defined commitments in the other communication, the communication on policies and measures. we therefore call on the commission to complete the european programme on climate change, iccp, as a matter of urgency and to come forward six months after cop6 with a set of specific policy and action proposals defining, by economic sector and by policy and measure, quantitative greenhouse gas reduction commitments. let me say very briefly a few words about the next united nations conference, cop 6, which will take place in the hague in november. the difficulty of cop 6 is not only due to the complexity of the kyoto leftovers, but also to the political situation in the united states. it is worth noting that, regardless of the outcome of the us elections, it will be bill clinton and al gore who will negotiate the kyoto protocol in the hague. so we are faced with extraordinarily worrying scenarios. if bush wins, any agreements we made in the hague with the clinton administration can be absolutely ignored by the new administration. if al gore wins, there is nothing to prevent it from remaining very difficult to get the two-thirds needed to ratify the protocol in the senate. i therefore believe that the european union should not abandon its negotiating agenda. the european union has a good negotiating agenda and should not give it up. if we abandon it in order to seek an agreement with the united states, we risk a loose loose result: we have made the wording of the protocol worse and, on top of that, it may not be ratified by the united states. that is why we must give signals of openness to russia, canada, japan, norway and the countries of central and eastern europe. i would like to conclude, mr president, by thanking all the members, in particular the rapporteur and the shadow rapporteur, for having enabled us to reach a broad consensus on the strategy to halt climate change.
- madam president, with the proposal to amend the common organization of the market in fruit and vegetables, the commission is aggravating the problems of the present organization of the market and the injustices of the cap and is creating greater difficulties for farmers who produce fruit and vegetables. the measures aimed at abolishing the minimum price, as in the case of tomatoes for industry, the lowering of the ceilings for aid to the operational funds from 4.5% to 3% of the value of the production marketed by each producer organisation, the precautionary reduction of 9.1% of the amount of aid for the first marketing year following the reform of the common organization of the market and a reduction in the quantity which may be withdrawn in citrus fruit are particularly serious. a sector of such economic, social and strategic importance, particularly for portugal, with a great potential for creating jobs and supporting the rural development of large areas in the southern countries, deserves special attention, which the commission does not do. the amendments tabled by the rapporteur and our colleague, mr jové peres, deserve our support in that they seek to correct the unacceptable positions of the commission, in particular in its proposals against increasing aid to farmers, raising the thresholds and re-establishing the limits of aid to producer organizations.
- madam president, commissioner, the commission proposal on the fruit and vegetable policy is very incomplete and insufficient. i would like to stress four points in this connection. firstly, the guarantee thresholds for the various types of fruit and vegetables should be raised in order to bring them closer to the actual production of the european union and to respond better to the level of demand. it is in this context that i particularly stress the need to raise the guarantee threshold for tomatoes for industry in portugal, which should at least approach the value set at the berlin summit, namely 953 000 tonnes. secondly, i would point out that the level of aid per tonne proposed by the commission is quite inadequate and that an increase is necessary, as the rapporteur proposes. thirdly, i would say that the level of operational funds is also something of a profoundly inadequate one. moreover, as the rapporteur proposes, without an improvement in the level of funding for the funds it is not possible to improve the functioning of producer groups. finally, i would like to say that the commission has missed a great opportunity to make the cap a little more equitable: as the commissioner knows, a sector which accounts for 16% of agricultural production receives only 4% of the budget, and in fact, it does not even reach 4%. and at this stage, the fruit and vegetable producers in the european union are essentially living off the market income, what they sell on the market, taking on the market risks, the market uncertainties, in contrast to their colleagues in other countries who produce other products, such as cereals, oilseeds, beef, milk, etc., who have the income guaranteed by the cap without taking any risks. commissioner, we must put an end to this schizophrenia in the cap as quickly as possible.
