in writing. - (pt) i voted in favour of this report because it corresponds to the expectations of the portuguese horticultural organisations, which are calling for a drastic change in the cmo for fruit and horticultural products, which is considered to be deeply detrimental to national interests. the loss of 7% of the tomato quota this year and the risk of losing 10% of the remaining quota again next year make it imperative to revise this com before the end of the year. this report makes proposals on a number of points which are in line with the interests of european production in general and portuguese production in particular. these are: the rigidity of the current system for processed tomato products; the level of guaranteed quantities for processed tomato, pear and citrus products; the complexity of the operational funds system; and the management of export refunds. it is now absolutely necessary that the portuguese government does not give in and defends uncompromisingly the specific national interests on each of these points. it would be absolutely grotesque and humiliating if the position of the european parliament were to be more firm and more adapted to portuguese interests than that of our own government.
- mr president, commissioner, the european union has had fisheries agreements with morocco since 1992. the current one expired at the end of november last year. under this agreement, some 500 european union vessels, 50 of which are portuguese, have been granted access to moroccan waters for an annual fee of some eur 125 million. as we expected, the difficulties of negotiation with the moroccan authorities have been very great. on the moroccan side there is a feeling of the great value of its fisheries resources, and it is constantly harassed by major international fishing powers, such as japan, south korea or russia, to fish even more in its waters. it should also be noted that the fisheries agreement with morocco is part of a wider framework of cooperation and partnership through which the european union also provides morocco with important trade concessions and important support for its development. i would like to emphasize four points in my speech. firstly, it should be stressed that the fisheries agreement with morocco is by far the most important of the european union, accounting for more than 30% of all catches by the community fleet in third country waters. the 30 000 direct and indirect jobs dependent on this agreement may not seem large on the scale of the european union, but the fact that they are concentrated in small fishing communities, mainly on the spanish and portuguese coasts, without tradition and possibility of economic conversion, would make the elimination of these jobs dramatic. secondly, i must point out with great bitterness the lack of openness on the part of the moroccan authorities involved in the negotiations. a position i don't understand for two reasons: the first is that they have in the meantime been passing on private licences to a number of shipowners in other countries, which is a contradiction to the argument that the difficulties in negotiating the agreement with the european union are related to the need to preserve fisheries resources. the second is that the moroccan rigidity in the face of the attitude of opening our markets to moroccan products and the aid provided for in the framework of the general cooperation agreement with morocco is also not understood. another point i would like to make is that the temporary aid paid to fishermen and shipowners to compensate for this year's stoppage will end at the end of the month. this is an untenable situation, since everyone prefers work to aid. the commission and the council of ministers of the european union must therefore assume their responsibilities, not only by extending aid until the situation is clarified, but also by reviewing the general basis for cooperation with morocco should the situation continue to deteriorate. finally, the commission, as the body responsible for the conduct of the negotiations, must give parliament the necessary information about what is going on, which is incomprehensible to us. the council of fisheries ministers has been equally incomprehensible in its inaction.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, europe's blue, as it is metaphorically called, is in crisis. the seas are being abused, fish stocks are becoming scarce, and unemployed fishermen are finding it difficult to accept a cruel fate that threatens the survival of a traditional activity and a certain way of life. last wednesday we discussed in this house the alarming drop in the irish sea in cod catches, this emblematic speciality - by the way - of portuguese cuisine. today we discussed a fisheries agreement with morocco. we are on the verge of another blow to the european union's fishing activities. i am talking about the european union and not just two of its parts, as some members have suggested: european solidarity cannot be a vain word or have only one meaning, and it is no longer the case when it comes to problems more concentrated in portugal and spain ... unfortunately, the commission was unable to obtain new arrangements in the negotiations with morocco which were held before the fisheries agreement expired on 30 november 1999. almost a year later, it seems we're at ground zero. the information available is scarce and unclear, although the negotiations have reportedly proceeded with various complicated vicissitudes. the commissioner assured us today, however, that the negotiations would resume next week and that progress was being made. i said it vaguely. we have been told that there is a possibility of reaching a balanced partnership with morocco. i mean, right? circumstantial words? the scepticism and the perplexity are great and legitimate. so does the european union not have enough weight and influence to convince its moroccan partner of the mutual advantages it has in concluding the new agreement? what's going on, anyway? does the moroccan state have an unconstructive attitude and prefer to negotiate with companies in isolation rather than with the european union, with which it has longstanding and deep interests in various fields? has the european union played its full diplomatic and economic role? i can't believe you! the european union has, in my view, downplayed the problem. what if the deal goes south? what will the council and the commission do? it cannot be a question of 'washing our hands' since they are the institutions with the competence and responsibility to conduct these negotiations. finally, in the unlikely event of a negotiating failure, the union has a political and moral obligation to support fishermen and fishermen's owners with adequate aid to ensure their livelihoods. that's the minimum. but it's not enough. the union must draw up and prepare a programme for the conversion of the activities of the agents concerned, so that they can have a future without drama. to shift this problem to the structural funds and the member states would be to disqualify the union in the eyes not only of those affected but also of the citizens.
the fight against financial crime - and especially money laundering - is being tackled in several areas of today's plenary session, which reflects the political importance of the legal mechanisms which the union is seeking to provide for the creation of an area of freedom, security and justice. following the creation of the single market and the corresponding liberalisation of capital and financial services, the union is committed to combating money laundering, a complex crime which presupposes another major crime from which illicit gains have resulted which are intended to be recycled in normal and regular economic life. the final destination of international crime, a consequence of the globalisation of the economy and the progressive liberalisation of trade, is at the heart of organised crime, as the tampere conclusions say. the european union is also committed to the fight against drugs, and the fight against drugs is a matter of the european union's own responsibility. the only way to combat this effectively is through international cooperation, which in europe takes the form of judicial cooperation between all the member states in dealing with international crime. the report therefore stresses the measures proposed by the french presidency, which, together with the portuguese presidency, has taken steps to approximate legislation, in line with the tampere council and in implementation of the treaty of amsterdam, without forgetting the good work the commission has done in this field with the dedicated efforts and lucidity of commissioner antónio vitorino. there is therefore coherence between the three initiatives which are the subject of my report, mrs karamanou's report and mrs roure's report today, when the framework decision obliges the states to comply with a result, when it extends the competence of europol to money laundering in terms of investigation or when mutual legal assistance is improved by convention. we are therefore on the right track, madam president, towards creating a broad and effective legal framework to at least cover this type of crime. my report clearly supports the core of the reasoning behind the framework decision, proposals which are fundamentally aimed at making it possible for member states to confiscate the proceeds of major crimes, in the same way as they are obliged to sanction and criminalize money laundering crimes without the flexibility offered by the reservations that other legal instruments before them gave and which made it easy for criminals to play with the law and control economies. we also support the binding nature of the framework decision, which obliges the member states to adapt their laws to the common result, while at the same time subjecting the member states to the judicial review of the court of justice. however, the report also includes critical remarks on the french proposal. why only part of the previous joint action, with these measures divided between weak and strong rules, given their different nature, crystallizing the fragility of the joint action, and not accepting the full transposition of the whole into the framework decision as explained in the amendments i proposed in paragraphs 9, 12, 13 and 14? nor do we think that the pursuit and confiscation of property should be restricted to cases where the main crimes are classified as serious. that would leave open a huge, totally unjustifiable judicial space of offenses without any kind of prosecution. hence amendments nos 4, 5, 6, 7 and 8. finally, while reiterating its appreciation for the french and portuguese initiatives in the context of the wider real possibilities which amsterdam nevertheless offers - and, let us be frank, the commission has been very good at taking advantage of them - the different legal instruments, directives, joint actions, framework decisions, conventions and international conventions are still so different in origin and effectiveness that it is urgent to make judicial, criminal and civil law community law, creating a judicial area in the service of freedom and security. it is a pity that this is not a priority for the time being in the reform of the treaties, given that the present igc is in the balance of member states' weights and measures... but we believe that the citizens will soon be able to impose this new dimension of europe, they will be able to impose a revision of the treaties which is in their real interests and that this revision will indeed be made in the name of freedom, in the name of security and in the name of justice.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the development of the economy and the phenomenon of globalisation have been accompanied in recent years by an increase in international crime. according to the united nations, the annual volume of money laundering recovered will amount to one trillion dollars. we must continue to fight money laundering in the european union, aware that, especially after the creation of the single market and the liberalisation of capital markets and financial services, effective police and judicial cooperation between the member states is needed, as effective as possible. we are discussing three reports today which recognise that money laundering is at the heart of organised crime and must therefore be eradicated wherever it occurs. the marinho report therefore proposes that concrete measures be taken to detect, freeze, seize and confiscate the proceeds of crime, and that joint police and judicial resources be mobilised to ensure that criminals or the proceeds of crime cannot hide in the european union. i agree with the main points of the report, but i have doubts about the rapporteur's proposal to extend this framework decision to lesser crimes. the aim of this framework decision is to combat the major crimes which are at the root of money laundering, not minor crimes with lower criminal penalties, which, if covered by this framework decision, may undermine its objective. the karamanou report aims to extend europol's powers to money laundering, by strengthening police cooperation and giving europol more effective tools. this is a portuguese initiative which has already been praised by several previous speakers. finally, the roure report seeks to remove a large number of legal obstacles to mutual legal assistance, while at the same time providing for the adoption of practical measures to strengthen it. i would like to see the line taken by this house in adopting the lehne report on money laundering regarding the professional secrecy of lawyers maintained in this report. as we have already said, professional secrecy is an indispensable element in the administration of justice, and any violation of this right may lead to a violation of the civil liberties of citizens.
- mr president, i should like to begin by informing the house that i am a lawyer by profession, although my membership of the portuguese bar association has been suspended at my request. i shall therefore speak only from this specific perspective, because i think that the amendment to article 3 under discussion in the roure report involves, even in the wording of the compromise, an unacceptable attack on the professional secrecy of lawyers. professional secrecy is the presupposition, the corollary and the consequence of a relationship of trust established between a lawyer and his client. this is regardless of the existence of a forensic warrant, as it arises from the mere fact that the client consults him or even takes him as a simple confidant. the newly approved charter of fundamental rights states in article 47 that everyone has the right to be given advice, to be defended and to be represented in court, and in article 48 that the rights of the defence are guaranteed to all defendants. in this context, professional secrecy is an inalienable duty of the lawyer. where would we be if, in this context, the lawyer were not bound to such secrecy or if he had to become a whistleblower instead of a defence lawyer? the lawyer does not have to bear the burden of the ineffectiveness or impotence of the authorities charged with investigating money laundering or any other crime. the commission has not yet adopted a decision on the matter. the court has already held that the wording of article 2 of the directive is not to be interpreted as meaning that the member states are required to take all necessary measures to ensure that the information provided by the applicant is not disclosed to third parties. as a financial advisor, there's no reason to assume that the lawyer, as such, isn't practicing law. i would simply like to reiterate, mr president, in conclusion, the position of the bar of my country on this matter, which is extremely clear: the court of justice has held that, since professional secrecy is the cornerstone and the presupposition of the profession of lawyer, a lawyer who is accused of having committed or being an accomplice to a criminal offence is liable to prosecution as any other citizen, but no lawyer may be required to declare or report facts which he has knowledge of in the course of his profession. the lawyer is forbidden everything that is not guided by the strict observance of legality. the bar association has the final and decisive authority in matters of professional secrecy, and it is up to its body to authorise - exceptionally - or not to authorise the disclosure of facts at the request of the judicial authority. it is for all these reasons, mr president, that i will vote against the amendment of article 3, even in the compromise wording of the legislative proposal.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to congratulate mrs gebhardt on her excellent report, which is in line with one of the decisions taken at the tampere council. it is fair to criticise the fact that, as regards police cooperation, judicial cooperation has come late, especially in view of the enormous proportions that organised and crossborder crime has been acquiring and which require rapid, joint and concerted action. the creation of eurojust is part of the european mechanism to combat organised crime, which aims to strengthen and ensure the balanced development of police and judicial cooperation in the european union. the proposal on the table today to set up a temporary judicial cooperation unit should therefore be regarded as a first step in the process of setting up eurojust. in its action it should strengthen good coordination and close cooperation between the national authorities competent for investigations and prosecutions where two or more member states are involved and where the case requires joint action, always in compliance with the national legislation of each member state. the experience gained from the operation of this temporary unit should serve as a basis for the decision to set up eurojust. and in the context of the desired strengthening of europol's powers, this parliament has been drawing attention to the need for democratic control. eurojust should also work on this point by strengthening its judicial control. commissioner vitorino concluded the previous debate by drawing attention to the ongoing initiative of a measure aimed at the com munitization of europol: we look forward to the presentation of this proposal and then to our opinion on it.
- mr president, mr president-in-office of the council, mr commissioner, i would first of all like to join in the congratulations which have been very rightly addressed to all the participants in the convention, in particular to mr méndez de vigo and my comrade pervenche berès, and let me also point out with great pride the active and committed participation of mr antónio vitorino. i would like to emphasize three points which my colleagues have already raised this morning. firstly, the merits of the operation of this convention. it is a merit to be retained and encouraged for future work, including future revisions of the treaties of the european union. secondly, to insist on the binding nature of this charter. the european citizens would not understand that this europe is faster in the market and in the currency, and slower in the actual realization of their citizenship rights and their basic rights. thirdly, to insist, as has already been done here, that in the nice conclusions, and in particular in the revision of the treaty, there should be this reference to the charter of fundamental rights in article 6 (2). the signing of this charter and its proclamation are not enough. these rights need to be translated effectively. we therefore call once again and insist that the presidency should be able to play a pedagogical role with its peers in the council. finally, two proposals: that the signature and ratification by the parliaments of the applicant countries of this charter of fundamental rights should form part of the community acquis; and that the nice conclusions should establish a precise timetable for the future presidencies to move from this charter to the treaty, and i would suggest that it should coincide in time with the ratification by all the parliaments of the new revision of the treaties to come out of nice.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the explanatory statement to mr morillon's report states that the decision on turkey's accession is too important for the future of the union and of turkey itself to be taken in comfortable silence or in the secrecy of the offices. we fully agree with this statement, but what we find is that the decision of the helsinki european council to grant turkey the status of candidate country was not taken after the necessary public and parliamentary debate which is also called for in the explanatory statement. the turkish candidacy has always divided european opinion, and we are surprised that there is still a consistent debate on the reasons for changing the traditional reserved position with which this candidacy was viewed. let us first consider the geographical problem. is it not true that only a small part of turkey is considered to be part of the european continent? in writing. - (pt) i abstained from voting on the charter of fundamental rights because i felt it was essential to know what the fate of the charter would be at the nice european council before adopting or rejecting it. will its provisions in future be merely a proclamation or will they become binding on the community institutions and, therefore, on the citizens of europe? we know the position of the majority of this parliament. for supporters of european federalism, this catalogue of rights is the embryo of the future european constitution, which the national constitutions of the member states will have to accept. we understand their position when they try to give another political meaning to a text on fundamental rights which is not only vague and general but also of questionable utility for an eu which has no serious shortcomings either in terms of rules or in terms of the defence and enforcement of these rights. fortunately, the dominant tendency of national governments seems to be towards the common sense approach, i.e. a charter with a purely political and non-binding scope. the great political debate on the charter will therefore take place first between national governments and then, possibly, when this parliament is faced with its decision. as i said at the beginning of my explanation of vote, i can only abstain for the time being.
- mr president, mr president of the court of auditors, madam commissioner, during the last two years the court of auditors has published six special reports on the common agricultural policy. its special report on the subsidy of skimmed milk disposal shows an absence of control measures and a level of milk falsification and fraud in its use that is unparalleled in any other agricultural sector. in its report on the disposal of butter, the court of auditors noted that 89% of the budget is spent on subsidies to the food industry in processes of dubious transparency and of dubious effectiveness in terms of impact on farmers' incomes. in either case, it is a small number of countries and a small number of economic operators who are the direct beneficiaries of these policies. in its most important work on the common agricultural policy, the special report on arable crops, the court concluded that from 1993 to 1997 the community budget paid more than €13 billion more than was reasonable. the arable crops account for 43% of the cap budget but only 10.7% of the final product. in 1995, almost 40% of payments went to the top 3% of beneficiaries, while the bottom 57% received only 4.5% of payments. the court therefore concluded, quite correctly, that support for farmers should not be based on prices, production or area, but on support for the net income of farmers or work units. these are documents of excellent quality which are a 'stepping stone' in the common agricultural policy which continues to favour the largest farmers over the smaller ones, the countries and regions of central europe over the peripheral countries and regions. it is a pity that the court of auditors' annual report, which is supposed to consider the most relevant aspects of the work carried out during the year by the special reports, has not retained these analyses and has decided to devote itself to other subjects for which it has not made specific analyses.

- doc. a5-0309/2000 by mrs gonzález álvarez, on behalf of the committee on the environment, public health and consumer protection, on the proposal for a european parliament and council directive on general product safety (com (2000) 139 - c5-0224/2000 - 2000/0073 (cod)).
- mr president, i congratulate the rapporteur on her work, which addresses the fundamental concerns of consumer associations, which must be accepted as valid partners in a constructive dialogue. we, the socialists in the committee on industry, support the commission's initiative to make clear the procedures for informing and collecting products declared dangerous, extending this responsibility to distributors and service providers to the extent of their intervention, producers and distributors who must unequivocally assume the consequences of the foreseeable risks of their products, from the installation of equipment to its maintenance, being responsible for informing the authorities and consumers and just compensation for damage. the collection of products at risk cannot be done as a last resort, but should be started as soon as reasonable doubt in the light of recent scientific knowledge warrants it. community standardization should be based on adequate scientific knowledge and, to this end, cooperation between member states should be encouraged in research into the safety of products in general and of groups at risk in particular: children, the elderly, the new youth cultures, new lifestyles and people with pathologies. reliability in product safety and after­sales service are an asset for the european label at a particularly difficult and crucial time. all measures are welcome to increase confidence and to ensure that the european product is produced and distributed with due regard for workers' health, social rights, the environment, fair competition and high safety standards. what does not meet this standard must be properly removed and disposed of, because what we do not want for ourselves cannot be exported to less favoured countries either. i hope that this directive will help to meet the growing anxieties and fears of europeans about consumer safety.

in writing. - (pt) the commission has presented, albeit belatedly, a programme on the community strategy for equality between men and women (2001-05), which is very different from previous programmes, in particular the fourth action programme, which expires at the end of this year. the proposed change in methodology is intended to increase the access and the possibility for non-governmental organisations to actively participate and contribute to the project. to make this possible, effective participation must be encouraged not only at community level but also in the member states. as the rapporteur says, it is important for the commission to consider that democracy requires equal participation and representation of all citizens - men and women - in the economy, decision-making and social, cultural and civic life. but it must be borne in mind that no work will produce lasting effects in terms of equality between men and women if at the same time women's rights are not strengthened, particularly in everyday practice, in all areas of activity.
in writing. - (pt) the pmo in the pigmeat sector needs to be reformed urgently to deal with the prolonged crisis affecting eu producers. but the commission's proposal for the creation of a voluntary solidarity fund financed solely by producers is contrary to the principle of financial solidarity in the cap and will not solve any of the basic problems of this sector. the garot report attempts to improve certain aspects of the commission's proposal by trying to reach a compromise with the council, particularly on the financial contribution from the fund. but as everyone knows, even with a certain openness on the part of the french presidency, the commission rejects these proposals and there does not seem to be a majority in the council to approve them. it is therefore essential to continue to fight for the compulsory establishment of the fund with a contribution from the cap and a modulation of its payments, so as to cover more producers, paying particular attention to small and medium-sized farmers. as regards the main issue of community participation in the fund, it should be added that the garot report proposal improves the situation in relation to the commission's position, but we still do not agree with the linking of this participation to a principle of cap co-financing, which was rejected at the time of agenda 2000.
the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work is a good example of the need to ensure that the directive is applied in a uniform manner. this is a real attack on fundamental human rights, since millions of people are denied access to basic social rights, such as housing, health, education, training and culture, quality employment and fair wages. although there are great differences between the various member states, the situation in portugal is certainly the most serious, with some 24% of the population living in poverty, mainly because of low wages, precarious and poorly paid employment and pensions and pensions so low that they do not allow a decent minimum standard of living for workers and their families or the elderly. this unacceptable situation of poverty and social exclusion, which the lisbon european council of 23 and 24 march recognised as requiring urgent action, requires a fundamental change in macroeconomic policies at community and national level, giving priority to the monetary policies of the stability pact in order not only to combat the current poverty situation but also to prevent further worsening in the future, including in the area of new technologies, with particular emphasis on women, children and young people, ensuring access to income levels and decent living and working conditions for all. however, despite all the commitments the council made just recently at the lisbon summit, and the emphasis it placed on the link between economic and social progress, the proposals put forward are little more than a slap in the face to the council in relation to its numerous promises to fight for the eradication of poverty. the commission's proposal for a community programme to encourage cooperation between member states in the fight against social exclusion is little more than a study programme. it is therefore essential to strengthen several aspects of the programme, in particular the innovations proposed in the application of the open method of coordination, whether it be the creation of the european-level think tank made up of community partners, representatives of ngos with experience in this field, and representatives of the european parliament, the council and the economic and social committees and the regions, the networking of the different recognised observatories or similar bodies in the member states, or a special attention to the problems of poverty and social exclusion at local, regional and national level, with the involvement of the most affected or threatened population groups in the programme. equally important are the proposals for an increase in funding from eur 70 million to eur 100 million, which stress the need for the commission to ensure the consistency of its policies, particularly in the economic, monetary, fiscal, competition, consumer protection, fisheries and agricultural fields, with the objective of eliminating poverty and social exclusion which is achieved at national level, and for this purpose an assessment of its policies should be made of their possible effects on poverty and social exclusion. i also hope that the proposals to recognise that qualifications and integration into working life are of particular importance will be adopted, and that it is therefore important to take account of the increasing phenomenon of social exclusion and poverty despite the fact that people are gainfully employed. it is essential to ensure quality employment with decent rights and wages and to create conditions for access to quality public services. it is essential that the council fulfils the promises it has made, not only with regard to the programme but also with regard to setting appropriate objectives and targets to be agreed by the end of this year, so as not to disappoint the expectations it has created with the conclusions of the lisbon summit in relation to its commitment to combating poverty, unemployment and social exclusion. finally, i would like to thank the excellent cooperation of my colleagues from the committee on employment and social affairs, the rapporteurs from other committees, the economic and social committee and the committee of the regions, the social ngos and the technicians who worked with me at the commission, the french presidency and parliament. in this work, and given that we are dealing with a matter for co-decision, it was possible to reach a consensus which aims to simplify the final report and facilitate its rapid adoption by the council, so the proposals we are putting to the vote tomorrow are the result of the consensus reached in committee, including the new proposals for replacement and those rejecting several amendments with a certain repetitiveness. i thank you for your understanding of this process so that we can help the european union to have a programme in this area again, despite its limitations and shortcomings. but it will be another tool to keep us more alert in the defence of greater social justice.
- madam president, i am deeply convinced that there is a lack of political, moral and pedagogical authority throughout the union to be able to speak about food quality and food safety. it is not by chance that the president of the council or the commissioner refrained from mentioning the main cause of all these problems in the food chain, which is, of course, the common agricultural policy itself. parliament votes half the budget to encourage production. today, the modern farmer is not what he produces, but what he is able to produce with quality and safety. but you don't help them! because you only help those who produce in quantity and therefore spend a large part of the budget to encourage these problems! these and others to come! but you don't want to accept the principle of ending animal feeding with the remains of other animals... we have already come here to talk about the united kingdom, portugal, and now we are here to talk about france. and i'm waiting here for the commissioner to come back with the next case, because we'll be back in a year with another country, with new cases. the fundamental point is that the president of the council and the commissioner must be convinced that a policy guided by the principles of the post-war period is not possible today, is not acceptable. you must stop all aid to production and start aiding quality, food security and the conservation of resources. and without that, we won't have a food chain that gives consumers guarantees. we shall continue to cheat farmers and consumers here permanently.
- the next item is the continuation of the debate on the report (a5-0307/2000) by mrs figueiredo, on behalf of the committee on employment and social affairs, on the fight against social exclusion.
- mr president, eurostat is very clear in pointing out that some 18% of the population of the european union lives on less than 60% of the national average income. this represents some 75 million european citizens. overall, the national poverty rate has varied over the last ten years from 10­11% for denmark and the netherlands to a worrying 24% for portugal. in addition, more than 10 million people are dependent on social assistance schemes, a situation which is worsening in most member states. this report is very important because it aims to improve the living conditions of the most disadvantaged, and is another step towards strengthening social cohesion. i would therefore like to congratulate the rapporteur. it is an ambitious community action programme which, according to the commission proposal, will have a budget of eur 70 million and should run for a period of five years. this appropriation should be increased in the light of the procedures and the realism required in this area. dialogue with non-governmental organisations and other social partners should be stepped up with a view to rigorously identifying the sources of poverty and social exclusion and identifying the most efficient and effective ways of combating them. however, it is necessary to warn against the inadequacy of the mechanisms for financial control of non-governmental organizations, which require better supervision to ensure the judicious and rigorous use of public funds. this programme must be aimed at the victims of poverty and social exclusion and we must avoid that it becomes in practice only a funding line for bodies which aim to investigate the problems of poverty and social exclusion.
- mr president, i would like to congratulate the rapporteur on her excellent contribution to identifying the causes and extent of poverty, a term which is appropriate to a social reality whose cruelty cannot be diluted in a concept which is both comprehensive and abstract, such as social exclusion. i support the commission in its initiative to create a programme which meets the objectives of the new social agenda of the treaty and gives substance to the lisbon decisions. and i hope you will accept the very consensual proposals in this report. i call on the council to set quantified targets, to plan specific and cross-cutting actions against exclusion based on information-rich indicators so that enhanced cooperation can be translated into concrete and effective action, more in its social results than in meeting electoral needs. i support the need to know better the causes of poverty, i support preventive actions against exclusion as a result of mergers, industrial changes, relocations, rural desertification, urban hyperconcentration, with innovative and concrete responses involving the social partners, local and national administrations and joint action by citizens.

- doc. a5-0320/2000 by mrs rothe, on behalf of the committee on industry, foreign trade, research and energy, on the proposal for a european parliament and council directive on the promotion of electricity produced from renewable energy sources in the internal electricity market (com (2000) 279 - c5-0281/2000 - 2000/0116 (cod)).
- mr president, commissioner, 90% of the victims of war and 4/5 of the refugees worldwide are non-combatant populations, mainly women and children. and yet, when it comes to women's participation in decision-making on the prevention and resolution of armed conflicts or peace initiatives, we find that their presence is very low. we cannot allow this to continue, because women's rights, priorities and interests are often ignored in peace negotiations. and because the few examples of women in decision-making positions in these matters have proved to be extremely effective. i will give you two examples which illustrate my point: mary roberts, the head of un humanitarian aid, has fulfilled her duties with great courage and determination, going to the places of danger at the times of danger, denouncing unspeakable situations, forcing countries and international bodies not to hide behind complicit silence and to take the required measures. portugal too, where diplomacy was only opened to women after the 'april 1974 revolution', has recently demonstrated with the case of east timor and our ambassador to indonesia, ana gomes, how the role of a woman and her sensitivity to the problems and suffering of a people, who have faced alone a 25-year struggle for freedom, can be felt throughout the world and, in particular, here in the european parliament. it is time to say 'enough' about the absence of women in solving these kinds of problems. i am not saying that we should not be concerned about the situation in the middle east, but that we should be concerned about the situation in the middle east, and that we should not be concerned about the situation in the middle east. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. these are some of the reasons why we are going to vote in favour of this very important report.

in writing. - (pt) the increase in the number of cases of the creutzfeldt-jakob disease variant in england and the situation in france have brought the question of mad cow disease (bse) to the forefront of the agenda. this case has always been a case of lack of transparency and of very serious concealment of information, at the highest level in some member states of the european union. the truth is that food safety is at stake in the case of mad cow, dioxin and even gmos. it is clear that the pursuit of maximum profit is a threat to human and animal health. consumer distrust is growing and agricultural markets are contracting. without a change in the common agricultural policy, the problem of food security will tend to worsen. the cap has always favoured the intensification of production or the gradual 'verticalisation' of production and the concentration of land, to serve the interests of the agro-food industry and the large multinational food companies. the basic problem will not be solved by a european food authority and by one-off, positive but belated legislative measures such as the ban on meat meal in all animal feedingstuffs. we must go further and change the common agricultural policy.

the rapporteur. - mr president, we are here today to debate the first of two reports on the amendment of the statute and the general conditions for the performance of the ombudsman's duties. and i apologize to the house for being tempted, in this late afternoon of the end of the parliamentary session, to take as a source of inspiration for the debate on this report some of the homesickness. if we look carefully, just as in relations with children, so in the processes of birth and growth of institutions which develop within others there is a time when it is necessary to show by deeds the recognition of their autonomy. and indeed, the time has come to recognise that the institution of the european ombudsman has grown sufficiently and has already shown sufficient proof of maturity to have its own budget. if, during the first stage, it seemed right and normal to make the ombudsman's mandate and action independent of the european parliament's budget, and to make him dependent on the european parliament's budget, this no longer makes sense. what we are proposing here is exactly the recognition of this separation: the council regulation of december 1999 which approved the creation of a specific section for the ombudsman in the general budget of the communities. a fundamental step was then taken, the ombudsman being considered an autonomous institution for the application of the financial regulation. this step was nothing more than the recognition that the growing visibility of an institution born and created in the name of the need to bring citizens closer to the life of the community institutions also requires financial autonomy in order to be able to be held accountable for the budgetary forecasts it makes and which it deems appropriate for the material and human resources necessary to carry out its noble functions. we therefore need to adapt the statute to the financial regulation by proposing the deletion of two articles - article 12 and article 16 of parliament's 1994 decision - which, as everyone knows, approved the statute and the general conditions for the performance of the ombudsman's duties. these rules no longer reflect the budgetary reality after the last amendment to the community's financial regulation. it is therefore necessary to verify that this does not correspond to reality and to approve its abolition. and since we will soon have to deal here in this parliament with substantial changes to the statute of the european ombudsman, which are still under discussion in the committee on constitutional affairs and whose report has also been entrusted to me and concerns the extension of the ombudsman's investigative powers, we reserve for that time the qualitative and complete revision of the text of the statute. before i close, mr president, i should like to take this opportunity to congratulate mr jacob söderman, the rapporteur, on his work in combating maladministration and, therefore, also on bringing european citizens closer to the life of the community institutions.
- mr president, mr commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, first of all i would like to congratulate the rapporteur, mr posselt, on the excellent work he has done in this initiative on the provisional establishment of the european police college; the commission has been working on this issue for some time. we are faced with a very useful instrument to optimise not only police cooperation between member states but also to improve cooperation with the applicant countries and with norway and iceland. this police cooperation is essential if the great objective of providing european citizens with a high level of security within the european area of freedom, security and justice is to be achieved. the intention of the portuguese presidency in presenting this initiative was, following the tampere conclusions and in order to meet the deadlines set out in the evaluation panel, to start a first phase of the european police academy by creating a network of the national institutes already existing in each of the member states in order to improve the training of senior police officials. we are thus beginning to train the next generation of police officers to be able to work and operate in the european context, that is, to prepare them for the application of community law and for joint and community action. this will be a kind of virtual academy, which should aim at deepening mutual knowledge of the national police systems and structures of the member states, reinforcing knowledge of international instruments and optimising cooperation and coordination between the academy and the other european multinational police training institutes. we have no doubt that it will be easier to reach consensus in this first phase. the transition to the second phase, i.e. the institutional and physical implementation of the future european police college, will certainly raise some problems. we know that there are still a number of member states which support the establishment of the academy only as a permanent network of national institutes, but we assume that the majority will accept this proposal as a temporary stage which will allow the establishment of a solid institution within a maximum of three years. this is also the proposal supported by the commission and so vigorously defended by commissioner vitorino. this is the position also advocated by the rapporteur and shared by the european people's party. the rapporteur goes even further by proposing that the deadline be shortened to two years; the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to begin by congratulating our colleague mary banotti, not only on her excellent report but also on the efforts she has made over the last few years to defend and guarantee the fundamental rights of children, in particular as the european parliament's mediator on the issues of international child abduction, a task in which she has invested her recognised qualities and sensitivity. the french initiative deserves a favourable reception. it is part of the progressive development of judicial cooperation in civil matters and its community dimension. the aim of the proposal is to facilitate the exercise of the right of access and residence of children of separated parents, in order to prevent the child from becoming a hostage to the conflict between his parents residing in different member states. in these cases, unfortunately, there are often situations that correspond to the cycle of love, hate and revenge of one or both parents. hence the need for speed to protect the child's welfare and sometimes his own safety. in a europe where citizens can move freely, regardless of their country of origin, it is easy to understand how easily family ties have been formed between people of different nationalities or residing in different countries. it is also understandable that litigation, such as divorce and parental responsibility, can lead to problems with the exercise of rights of access when the child is living in another country, as well as cases of abduction, in particular when the child is not returned by the parent who has the right of access within the set time limit. this regulation establishes the principle of mutual recognition of decisions for the purpose of exercising the right of access without intermediate steps and limits the possibilities of obtaining a stay of execution of such decisions. this regulation thus responds to the need for direct application of uniform rules on such important issues as the regulation of parental responsibility and the exercise of rights of access. we are thus strengthening our sense of community and european citizenship. however, it is regrettable that the scope of this regulation is limited and does not cover the de facto family, where there are children born out of wedlock, who do not see their rights recognised and applied in the community framework. now, this is an important reality in our states. according to the latest statistics from eurostat, 26% of all births in the union in 1998 were out of wedlock and these figures are continuing to rise. it is therefore essential to fill this gap for reasons of fairness, realism in the face of sociological developments and equal rights and obligations for all citizens of the european union. finally, i would like to say that i support the amendment proposed by the rapporteur to article 1, which should specify that the period foreseen for the right of access should not be less than one day. we take particular account of the fact that we sometimes encounter situations where the parent comes from opposite ends of the union, travelling thousands of kilometres, so it makes no sense to do this for an interview limited to one hour. this is ridiculous! maintaining relations with both parents makes an indispensable contribution to the child's education and personality formation.
- mr president, mr commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, what is happening to sport in the european union, and in particular to professional sport and, within it, to football, is the consequence of a right without politics, a right which no one has really been able to choose. this is, strictly speaking, an attack on democracy, however much we avoid seeing things in this light. the general difficulties we have encountered and which have come to the fore in the debate, particularly since the bosman judgment, even if they have nothing to do, directly or indirectly, with the content and nature of this case, are the result of the frequent and sometimes sharp contradiction between, on the one hand, specific forms of sports organisation and their entrenched practices and, on the other, certain rules of the treaties, particularly in the framework of the competition rules or other principles of the internal market, with emphasis on the freedom of movement of workers and the rules of non-discrimination on grounds of nationality. many of these rules are among the oldest in the treaties since the treaty of rome, regardless of the changes they have undergone. we all know that at the time of its original definition, and at the time of its subsequent amendments, no one, absolutely no one, neither its drafters nor the decision-makers, ever thought that a current legal framework would come to take on this true imperial dimension over the world of sport. we were dealing with economics, just economics in the strict sense. not the whole of society, because in all sectors of society it is possible to detect economies of scale. that's what i mean when i say a right without politics. a right defined without participation, this absurdity of a right that no one really wanted so, but that everyone is irremissibly imposed. that is why it is imperative that the specific nature of sport be recognised in the treaties. without this, no problem will be solved satisfactorily, without this we will continue to watch helplessly as the european union and money seem to be working hand in hand to destroy sport, to distort the very nature of sporting competition, to sabotage the social roots of clubs and the national networks of their traditional organisation. without this, sport will continue to be cyclically run over with inexplicable legal violence by the sometimes corrosive or demolishing effects of rules which were not designed for this and which truly no one has ever been able to choose, or vote with such a scope. it is not a question of laying down fundamental rules in the treaties or of laying down essential principles of community law. it is certainly a question of being able to strike the right balance, in a politically free and properly reflective manner, between the specific needs of a sector which, while having a cultural value and enormous social relevance, also has economic aspects. the aim is to protect the sporting authenticity of sport and its social function against the exclusive domination of the laws of the market. i would stress that the aim is to protect sport, not to abandon it. why should we allow the essential value of training to be abused within the rich network of sports clubs and associations? why can't or don't we want to allow a minimum of national identity to be safeguarded in the professional club teams? in sport, even in professional sport, the competition that really matters is the sporting competition itself, not the mere unrestrained economic competition of its agents. in european sport, moreover, there is not and there does not have to be a single market. there are fifteen different national markets, corresponding to the competitive framework of each country, especially if we look at the specific case of the united kingdom, which, together with those of other countries which are also european but outside the european union, form the basis of long-established european competition. now, things are happening exactly the other way round. the growing inequality in wealth makes law and it is increasingly taking all the cups; the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a good example of the need to ensure that the safety of workers is not compromised by the use of asbestos. i would like to end by recalling one of the conclusions of the resolution we voted in september following the mennea report: 'the european parliament calls on the intergovernmental conference to include an explicit reference to sport in article 151 of the treaty, so that the european union, in its action, recognises the cultural, economic and social phenomenon which sport represents.' maybe we're just in time. i would like to ask you, mr president, to make sure that the next igc is not another missed opportunity in sport. i would like to ask the french presidency in particular not to deny in nice the words and assurances which mr jacques chirac gave us here in july.
- madam president, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to begin by congratulating myself, as chairman of the european parliament delegation to cop vi, on the excellent relationship which the commission and parliament have developed during this week. if there is one area where there has been no disagreement, it has been the relationship between the delegation of the european parliament and the commission. and i cannot but regret that some of the 15 ministers have been more concerned with running to the press conferences than with creating a real spirit of unity to ensure a global policy on climate change. madam president, it was known that this cop vi was of very special importance: the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. we have failed to reach agreement and have failed to act. if we are to be very clear and we have to take stock, we can say that of the three parties only one has tried to reach an agreement, has tried to have a global policy on climate change: the european union. the united states - but let us also be clear, the whole umbrella group - has been making a series of excuses for not reaching any kind of agreement at cop vi in the hague. from now until may, from now until bonn, the european union must act in two directions: on the one hand, in the diplomatic field, thinking that the scenario of ratification by the united states is the most unlikely scenario, so there is diplomatic action to be developed with the other members of the umbrella group. secondly, we must act internally to ensure credibility. only this credibility will enable the european union to lead the discourse and action at cop vi 'b' in bonn.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, in his statement of 9 may 1950 robert schuman said: 'europe will not be achieved by a coup, nor by a joint construction, but by concrete achievements which first create a de facto solidarity.' on the eve of the nice summit, i think it is appropriate to question the present state of this spirit of 'de facto solidarity', without which the construction of europe is obviously neither genuine nor authentic. as regards the conclusion of the igc, what will be at stake in nice is an agreement which, by correcting the shortfall in the functioning of the european institutions, does not affect the balance between member states and, of course, between them and the union. the very fact that this question has been raised is in itself a clear demonstration of the failure of a policy of strengthening the bonds of mutual trust between the countries of the union. we are not the only ones to say this, the french presidency, through minister pierre moscovici, has also recently acknowledged in an interview that the positions taken this year, especially by president jacques chirac and minister joschka fischer, on the future organization of the union have had the direct effect of intensifying a climate of distrust between small and large states which will inevitably be reflected in the results of the nice european council. i am talking about the idea of creating a vanguard of countries centred around the paris-berlin axis, which would culminate in a re-founding of europe or in the dangerous confusion between a heterogeneous europe, which is healthy, and a europe at two or more speeds, which is dangerous. the text of the compromise resolution which we are about to vote on reflects this unease to some extent, because its generic nature is evident. but it is also a text in which its authors, even knowing that they risk a political defeat and anticipate it, reveal a vision focused above all on the powers and competences of the european parliament itself, demanding more and more means of parliamentary intervention without devoting a single word to the essential role of the national parliaments. the reality is quite different, however. we all know that the nice summit is intended to complete the work of an intergovernmental conference, which is accountable not to the european parliament but to its national parliaments, and which categorically affirms the sovereign nature of this process of revising the treaties. we hope, mr president, that the nice summit will be a success in the sense we have just mentioned, that its conclusions will express a joint formulation of the various national wills which are found and harmonized in the permanent search for cohesion and development, and that in fact we will take a few more steps towards the 'de facto solidarity' of which robert schuman spoke.
mr president, i should like to concentrate my remarks on three essential points. the president-in-office of the council raised the question of enhanced cooperation and said that a consensus was being reached on this matter. i would like to ask both president hubert védrine and president romano prodi what role is envisaged or advocated for the commission in the area of enhanced cooperation in the second and third pillars? the second question concerns the overall agreement which my comrade and president of the socialist group, mr enrique barón crespo, has already discussed here, and which fortunately forms part of the resolution which this european parliament will certainly adopt tomorrow. today, in the council as in parliament, we do not have a state-splitting of power proportional to the size and number of citizens in each of the states, but a balance based on the principle of dual legitimacy between the union of states and the union of citizens. but, according to different readings, what is being prepared at the moment is a change in this balance and the strengthening of the representation of the large countries both in the council and in parliament. and that's not fair or acceptable. i would therefore like to stress once again that the method adopted for decisions in the council is the double majority method (not only is it more noticeable but it is more fair), and that in the european parliament the current method can be maintained and that linear reduction applied when new members enter. it seems to me that this final picture is more balanced, more fair and takes account of a historical reality of which the builders of europe cannot fail to be aware: the problem is that there are states and there is a relationship between the states and the citizens of each of these countries, while there is no stronger relationship between all citizens and this europe itself. finally, the question of the charter of fundamental rights: the president-in-office has been sincere and i appreciate that sincerity. and i said that there are countries, there are governments that even admit not to proclaim the charter if it is introduced into the treaties. it is unacceptable that it should be said in this way. what will a european citizen think when he hears that governments are willing to write certain things down, but no longer agree that these things have value and legal force? what signal do we send to the applicant countries when we tell them that in order to join the single market they have to submit to what we impose on them, but we no longer require them to ratify the charter of fundamental rights? in other words, we are sending a signal that this europe treats consumers better than the citizens of this european union. and so we once again urge and appeal to the presidency to make every effort to ensure that at least one reference to the treaties is made in nice, because that is the best way of honouring the work of the european parliamentarians, the commission and even the representatives of the governments who were at the convention for the drawing up of the charter.

classification societies depends on their size or even the number of inspectors on their staff, and prefer to be guided by the consequences of their certification, so that the civil liability regime of classification societies for smaller countries. the performance of classification societies shall be assessed by common criteria: number of accidents, incidents or detentions of inspected and in writing. - (pt) the debate and the resolution adopted by the european parliament raise serious concerns about the decisions to be taken in nice, particularly as regards possible new restrictions on the sovereignty of small countries such as portugal, the strengthening of federalism and the militaristic approach to security and defence policy, and the weak and clearly insufficient measures in the social field. as regards the intergovernmental conference and the subsequent reform of the european institutions, it is particularly serious to move towards strengthening the power of a small number of large countries at the expense of the decision-making capacity of the large majority of small and medium-sized countries, which could happen with the expansion of the qualified majority, 'enhanced cooperation' in a 'hard core' design, the application of enhanced cooperation to areas such as security and defence policy, the prospect of moving towards the elaboration of a european constitution based on the charter of fundamental rights, with restrictive content and many aspects lagging behind identical documents, whether at international or national level, as in the case of portugal. as regards the social field, the proposals are very timid and do not really give substance to the commitments made at the lisbon summit, particularly as regards the need to strengthen economic and social cohesion. the council must therefore revise its positions and move forward in order to counter the wave of mergers and concentrations of companies in different sectors, to interrupt the ongoing liberalization processes, to change macroeconomic policies with the aim of fighting for quality employment with rights and the fight against poverty and social exclusion.
in writing. - (pt) parliament has just voted a resolution on the nice summit which has the following features: 1 - it does not deal in depth and clearly with any of the subjects which gave rise to the main differences between the member states during the preparatory work of the igc, leaving all the great relics of amsterdam without any prospects. parliament thus refuses to exercise influence, it lowers its voice and remains silent in the name of partisan, national and governmental loyalty, thus forgetting its obligations towards the citizens. 2 - it nevertheless provides an advantage, not so residual as it may seem, to the less populated states, which can always take advantage of parliament's silence in the face of the options of the states which demand a strengthening of their institutional power in all the organs of the community. in this respect, the large states are "on an equal footing" with the others, not involving parliament in defending their views, using it against us in the future institutional architecture. 3 - it shows that the combined efforts of the delegations from the smaller countries have served to give the others a political advantage in the debates next week in nice. without winning, this resolution has allowed for a result that does not place us in the camp of the defeated. sometimes silence is a victory... 4 - that is why silence, which is not innocent, can justify a vote in favour.
in writing. - (pt) this report is particularly serious and its adoption is a support by the european parliament for the reinforcement of the militarisation of the european union, in line with the objective of creating by 2003 a rapid reaction force of 50 000 to 60 000 men, mobilisable within 60 days and able to remain on the ground for a year, with adequate air and sea support, emphasising the development of the european union's military means and capabilities and the efforts of the member states to fill certain existing gaps in this area, as they have just done in the field of strategic air transport, cruise missiles and reconnaissance and navigation satellites. it is also a serious matter to want a state which does not wish to participate in a mission to be obliged to pay a financial contribution which will be distributed among the states participating in that war. the satisfaction with the framework agreement signed in farnborough by six countries of the european union is to be deplored, since it will encourage the restructuring of europe's defence industries by insisting on a space policy which covers the civil and military aspects of the use of space and which includes the national and multinational means available to it in the military field. finally, as the report says, nice could lead to the creation of the definitive bodies of the csdp - the military committee and the military staff - with powers and decision-making powers to make even more rapid progress in the implementation of the common security and defence policy. that is why we must vote against it.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the report we are considering today is the result of an initiative by the portuguese presidency and is part of a package of measures designed to promote the creation of an area of freedom, security and justice in order to provide a useful response to the concerns of the daily lives of european citizens. often, in approaching justice issues, we spend too much time on the criminal frameworks, the prison system, the crime-fighting forces, the procedural codes and even the demands of judicial officers. perhaps we have spent too little time with the victims, who are often the weakest part of this universe. the initiative covers three key areas: the right of the victim to information, access to justice and the right to compensation for damage. the main purpose of this proposal for a framework decision is to promote the protection and enforcement of the rights of victims in proceedings, in particular by simplifying access to justice, to courts and to legal information, and to promote and protect their rights to compensation for loss and damage. it is essential that there be a legal instrument to approximate the various national laws with regard to the status of the victim. there are certainly a number of areas which need to be improved and even corrected. but it is important that we vote in favour of this framework decision, which is quite balanced. we cannot seriously talk about a people's europe if we do not take into account the rights of victims. and in these, as mrs smet has already pointed out, special attention should be paid to certain categories, such as minors, foreigners, victims of sexual assault and victims of terrorist acts. at the same time, special training should be provided for the professionals who will be in charge of direct contact with victims. the necessary measures must also be taken to enable victims to resume their normal lives, not only from an economic point of view, but also to enable them to receive legal, psychological, medical or social assistance if they need it. finally, i would like to congratulate the rapporteur, mrs cerdeira morterero, on her work and the improvements she has made in the committee on civil liberties, and i also welcome the improvements made by the reports by mrs smet and mr lehne. the committee on civil liberties and internal affairs has been able to work together, which i would like to stress and thank. i would have wished that such a consensus could have been reached in plenary. the few amendments we have tabled are mainly concerned with questions of legal rigor and our understanding that victim support services do not have to be exclusively or preferably public: often, in services that are supposed to be humanized, the experiences with social solidarity institutions and other non-governmental organizations are the best. the ppe generally welcomes the report by mrs cerdeira morterero, which we voted in favour of in committee.
in writing. - (pt) victims are not always properly protected in criminal proceedings. various council of europe instruments have addressed this issue since the early 1980s, but it was not until 1998 that it was discussed at the level of the council of the european union. since then, there has been, on the one hand, the commission's proposal in the run-up to the tampere european council in 1999, which indicated a number of measures in the field of prevention, assistance and assistance to victims, defending the need to develop minimum standards especially with regard to the protection of victims, their access to justice and their right to compensation for damage suffered, including for legal costs, and, on the other hand, the portuguese presidency's proposal for a framework decision on the status of victims in criminal proceedings. the latter proposal is the subject of this report and deserves our approval, particularly as regards simplifying access to rights, courts and legal information, and promoting and protecting the rights of victims to compensation for damage and harm suffered through reparation mechanisms.
- mr president, we have already made a rigorous assessment of what happened in nice, but it is worth remembering that nice started with a very narrow agenda. the council asked this european parliament not to broaden this agenda because it wanted to focus exclusively on the remains of amsterdam. and the council said that it wanted to do this to give the european union functionality in its institutions, so that it could work better at 27 than it does today at 15. what has been the result of this exercise and this work? it was precisely that what was most fundamental and most important in bringing functionality, decision-making, flexibility and operability into the functioning of the institutions was not dealt with. the council concentrated most on the question of power, i.e. there was too much calculation and too little politics in nice. the images that emerged from nice were those of a europe in which countries tried to treat each other, implying that the europe of the future is a struggle between large and small countries. i wonder if these are not the arguments that best serve the interests of the eurosceptics and those who are the enemies of europe... i wonder if this image of nice is not an appeal to the consciences of the europeans who are convinced, of those who do not want only a single market, of those who do not want only a single currency, in the face of what i consider to be this european 'no time'. in my view, nice was the second part of amsterdam and we are now in a phase, an interval, between amsterdam and an igc which will take place in 2004, but which it is not known when it will end. and it is the duty of the europeanists at this moment to counter the image that has emerged from nice and to say that solidarity has meaning for us, to say that it is possible to build a europe where large and small countries can live together; in other words, there are no countries to be dismissed in this european project and national egos have no meaning in this europe. the appeal i would like to make to the house and the commission at this time is therefore: it is necessary that someone should do the teaching of the european project. the pedagogy of the european project is not the sum of national interests, it is a project of solidarity, as president romano prodi had the opportunity to say, and it is looking to the future that the institutions must join hands with at this moment in order to make enlargement a reality, so that citizens look at europe as we want them to look at it and so that politics returns to the construction of the european project and not just to the defence of power and, above all, the power of the big countries.
