- mr president, my first words are to welcome the opportunity of this initiative and the diligence with which our rapporteur, eluned morgan, has carried it out. the purpose of the discharge procedure is to analyse existing processes and procedures and identify possible shortcomings in order to enable institutions to reform. to achieve this, i would like to highlight four points which i consider to be fundamental in the work of the budgetary control authority. firstly, the balance in the consideration of all the budgetary lines: it is unacceptable that the court of auditors' analysis of own resources should expressly exclude all revenue from undeclared economic activities, in contrast to the diligence with which the various administrative aspects, sometimes without substantive content, of various forms of expenditure are dealt with. secondly, transparency in the reference to cases and events: the court of auditors, when it refers to specific cases, must identify the institutions involved, taking responsibility for the statements it makes. third, the rigour of the analysis: it is not understandable that a large number of high-quality special reports of the court of auditors should be omitted from the annual report in favour of other subjects dealt with without any prior analysis and on the basis of questionable criteria of political expediency. fourthly, the accuracy of the use of statistics: statistics can be a valuable tool for analysis, but it can also be a dangerous tool for distorting reality. using statistics to compare irregularities detected by different institutions as a result of more or less rigorous checks can more easily hide than reveal what is going on. high rates of irregularity detected can be both a good indicator of the zeal shown by the supervisory authorities and a signal of the perversion of the regulatory system.
the commission has also proposed that the council should adopt a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos. - madam president, the opinion of the committee of which i have the honour of being rapporteur, adopted by an overwhelming majority, introduces limitations to the rapid liberalization of the postal services, opting for a gradual and controlled opening to competition, without market distortions unfavourable to the customers and users of the services and to the national universal service undertakings. in this context, we advocate that the opening-up should take account of article 16 of the treaty, which elevates the traditional postal services to the category of services of general interest, given their place in the union's common values and their role in promoting social and territorial cohesion, and should therefore operate on the basis of principles and conditions which enable them to fulfil their missions. we naturally point out the weaknesses of the proposal for a directive, demanding that the economic and social impact of liberalization be known and that the economic efficiency and quality of service of the companies be assessed so that parliament can decide with certainty and clarity. without prejudice to the modernisation of all post offices and the opening up to competition, member states must continue to preserve their rural network of over-the-counter services, which bring the peripheries closer to the decision-making centres and are therefore essential for the social cohesion of the smallest urban or rural communities. the committee on legal affairs was more demanding on the price limit for services reserved for the universal service provider, but it is the same as its proposal on the 150 gram weight limit and the common rejection of the 50 laid down in the proposal for a directive. the difference with the report on the substance is simply a matter of detail, which is understood in the context of a parliamentary negotiation, so well conducted, moreover, by mr ferber. it also ensures that crossborder mail remains a service reserved for post offices and, if vat is applied to postal services, that the tax is applied at the reduced rate so as not to raise the price of community stamps. my committee has clearly rejected the concept of special services, which i understand was intended to strip the universal service of any profitable activities, leaving it with the services which cost more than the price, and has introduced the so-called new services, defined - mr president, first of all i would like to emphasize the european parliament's efforts to find a balanced solution to this complex web of interests surrounding the banana com. it is proof of this effort that we are discussing for the second time a report which makes excellent proposals to make the protection of eu and acp banana production compatible with wto rules. it is a pity that the commission and the council are persisting in a solution which could seriously compromise this production, especially after 2006. i represent in this parliament madeira, a region where bananas are the main agricultural product. the same is true in other regions of the european union, such as the canary islands and the french overseas departments. all these territories are part of the outermost regions. these regions are not only the most disadvantaged but also the only ones in the european union where bananas are produced. this situation must necessarily be taken into account when the new rules for the banana import regime are drawn up. the european agricultural model, based on the multifunctionality of agriculture, must also serve to safeguard the european union's banana production. in my region, among the many functions of agriculture, the one that is becoming increasingly important is environmental protection. in other words, the end of bananas in madeira would be at least as serious from an economic point of view (serious loss of income for thousands of farmers) as from an environmental point of view (destruction of the subtropical landscape and the rural world, worsening erosion). mr president, without this important environmental aspect, madeira will have no chance of sustaining its 'golden goose', which is tourism. why then liberalize it in 2006 when the quota system is perfectly compatible with wto rules and is the best guarantee for maintaining community banana production and that of the acp countries? mr president, as is well known, this common organization of the market in bananas is of particular importance for the acp producing countries and for some of the outermost regions, such as madeira, both in terms of production and employment and in terms of the environment, in the maintenance of a productive green area, a fundamental issue for regional development itself. the solution to the problem of the future of the common organization of the market by means of a uniform import regime would entail a serious risk of a fall in the price of bananas on the community market, which would require an increase in the compensatory aid granted to producers in the outermost regions and would entail a revision of the financial perspectives themselves approved under agenda 2000 for the financing of the cap, so it is good that the commission continues not to insist on its proposals, otherwise banana production would end, with serious social, economic and environmental consequences, as our rapporteur, mr dary, points out. the transitional period should therefore be about 15 years and never less than 10 years, and compensatory aid should include guaranteeing the incomes of community producers and the disposal of the production of the outermost regions, so that aid should also be given to those entities marketing the bananas produced there. we shall vote in favour of the proposals and this report, and we hope that the commission will reconsider its position.

in writing. - (pt) it is important to avoid interrupting and to continue the extension of existing programmes in the field of public health, which are about to expire, because their objectives are complementary to national policies for the protection of human health. in this report the rapporteur is in favour of extending the six programmes until 31 december 2002, although he rightly criticises the commission's legislative programming, which is the source of this problem, since four of them are due to expire at the end of this year and two at the end of 2001. these are important programmes aimed at improving public health, combating cancer, preventing aids, combating drug addiction, sanitary control and pollution-related diseases. in the meantime, as the report says, it is essential to extend the financial allocation every year and to take the necessary measures to ensure a smooth transition, pending the preparation of the new programme.
the commission has already made a number of proposals in the past, and i am sure that the commission will be able to take them up in the future. it is an important instrument in the construction of the internal market and also a substantial source of revenue for the community budget. in 1992, an agreement was reached, which was then regarded as a minimum harmonisation, whereby the normal vat rate could not be lower than 15%, and member states were free to apply one or more minimum rates of not less than 5%, although derogations were provided for in the meantime. but this first phase was to last only until the end of 1996, when the definitive system was to be introduced. the attempts made in the meantime have only led to the fixing of the minimum rate of vat at 15%, although the commission has also proposed an upper limit of 25%, which was not accepted, and to the extension of the system until the end of 2000. by the end of this year, the commission proposes to follow two paths in this field: the first is contained in the report by my colleague mr garcía-margallo y marfil on the coordination of vat administrative systems. the second is contained in this report, which seeks to postpone any decision on the harmonisation of vat rates until 2006. if i agree with the proposals for the coordination of administrative systems for the collection of vat, i no longer agree, nor did the committee on economic and monetary affairs agree, with the proposal to postpone the harmonization process for five years. has the commission given up on this matter during its entire term of office? does the commission not consider, as we have, that the existence of the euro as a single currency, for the time being, in twelve countries of the european union will bring about price transparency which will highlight the distortion introduced into the internal market by the existence of such disparate values between the rates applied in the various countries of the union? does the commission, and in particular commissioner frederik bolkestein, consider that this is a case of tax competition which your proposal seeks to maintain or even encourage? that is not our view, and that is why we voted to reduce this deadline to the end of 2003, so that, with two years of the euro's actual existence, new solutions can be put forward. i would also like to suggest, in line with the positions adopted by parliament, that the references to the council's having to vote unanimously on this matter be withdrawn. if the treaties continue to require it, so be it. but we do not think that the commission and parliament are obliged to define at this stage that this matter must be voted on unanimously by the council by the end of 2005. this proposal is not in line with the principles laid down by the commission and parliament for the igc, which has now come to an end. it is even less justified to decide now that this will be the case until the end of 2005, when it is already known that there will be a further revision of the treaties in the meantime. we therefore propose that this requirement of unanimity should also be abolished. let us say, finally, that we do not like to see the commission, at the beginning of its mandate, giving up completely on vat harmonization and that parliament, understanding the need to extend the period of validity of the current legislation, is waiting for the commission to present a new, complete and substantive draft on this subject in the middle of its mandate and with the euro already in circulation.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, there are many citizens of the european union for whom the quality of life is nothing more than a mirage. they are left with hours lost in city traffic and no time to spend with their children; they work without conditions and have no green spaces and leisure to rest; they waste days in hospital queues and struggle with the bureaucracy of the administrative services of the state. in the cities, we run and lose sight of well-being. environmental noise must be introduced into this sorry picture. it is present all day long, making life even more tiring, and it is present at night, disturbing deserved rest. unfortunately, more and more european citizens are affected by environmental noise. but despite this, the european union has been slow to implement a common noise policy. it took five years to come up with a proposal for harmonised european noise indicators. it is unacceptable that we have to wait another five years to define noise limits to be applied throughout the european union. i therefore agree with the rapporteur, mr de roo, when he proposes that we should waste no more time and turn this directive on noise assessment and management into a binding framework directive. the noise which most disturbs the sleep of citizens is that caused by night air traffic. in the absence of common standards in the european union, we have seen situations of a clear distortion of competition between airports and a lack of fairness between citizens of the different member states. it is therefore essential to set binding limits on aircraft noise in the vicinity of airports. however, i do not think amendment no 36 is the most sensible way of doing this. in any case, i congratulate the rapporteur on the work he has done.
- mr president, the precautionary principle has become one of the citizens' greatest allies in the defence of public health and the environment. however, there is still a long way to go in perfecting this principle. on the one hand, it remains typically european; across the atlantic, this instrument is dubbed obscurantist and even medieval. the european union itself uses it in a vague and even irrational way. it is not the case that the commission is not aware of the problems of the european union. that is why i consider this commission proposal, enriched by mrs patrie's report, to be so important. by clearly stating when and how the precautionary principle should be applied, it makes its use at european level clearer and more credible, and it mandates european negotiators at the next round of the world trade organisation to inscribe the precautionary principle as basic and universal. but let me denounce what i believe is a glaring example of the lack of coherence in the use of the precautionary principle in the european union: i'm talking about the mobile phones. it is incomprehensible that the european union should remain insensitive to the dozens of independent scientific studies which point to the possible risk of mobile phones to human health. we can indeed ask ourselves whether this stagnation is not due to the fact that most mobile phone manufacturers are european companies. just yesterday, in lisbon, an american, professor george carlo, who has been entrusted by the food and drug administration for the last seven years with a study on the effects of radiation on human health, revealed that there is a risk that the use of mobile phones could potentiate the development of brain tumors, alzheimer's disease and skin lesions. in these circumstances, i call on the commission to apply the precautionary principle to mobile phones, in particular by warning of the dangers of their use by children.
in writing. (pt) the european parliament has just now approved, by means of the second reading of the budget, the full reinstatement in the budget line for the cohesion fund of the eur 360 million it had set aside at its first reading. the cohesion fund is, for portuguese socialists, a cornerstone of the economic and social policy launched by the single european act and considerably strengthened by the maastricht treaty. since its creation, the cohesion fund has been a notable example of significant impact in two areas of community interest - environment and trans-european networks - of the effective implementation of the community budget as well as of the highest execution rates across the community budget. as our colleague mário soares has said, when the current prodi committee took office, the most negative aspect of its programme was the abolition of the services directorate of this fund and its exclusion from the name and the organization chart of the former directorate-general xvi. this abolition, which began to take place in the middle of last year, as we had anticipated, led to a dramatic break in the rate of approval of projects and execution of payments, with the result that the annual execution of commitments of this fund, at the end of june 2000, was below 1%. in proposing to the european parliament a cut of eur 360 million in payments for 2001, the commission showed worrying signs of a lack of interest, motivation and will to fully implement the financial package adopted by the berlin european council. the portuguese socialists decided to vote against the budget at first reading in the event of a cut of eur 360 million from the draft budget and agreed to vote against the budget only when this position was amended to place this amount in the reserve. in the light of the combined efforts of the delegations of various governments in the council, in particular the portuguese government, and of members from various parliamentary groups, where it is fair to distinguish the portuguese socialists, it was decided in the tripartite dialogue between the commission, the council and parliament to re-register the total amounts provided for in the council's first reading for the cohesion fund payments, and the european parliament has now endorsed this position. we welcome this and we are committed to monitoring the implementation of the cohesion fund. in order to make up for the delay in the implementation of the cohesion fund, which started this programming period, it will require a multiplier of effort and attention on the part of the beneficiary member states and, above all, the highest priority from the commission, in particular by increasing the human and financial resources available for monitoring it.
in writing. - (pt) the commission's proposal on postal services reveals a desire for unlimited liberalization, which is detrimental to a vital public service, the companies that provide it and the workers who are part of it. that is why we strongly and clearly oppose all of his statements. we do not think that the commission's proposal is a good one, but we do not think that it is a good one. we appreciate its true value and therefore do not reject it. however, since the ultimate objective of these regulations is not called into question, we cannot endorse them either.
in writing. - (pt) the liberalization of postal services proposed by the commission is neither adequate nor consistent, which is why i voted in favour of the report and all the amendments tabled which express the same orientation. the initial rejection of this proposal reflects a general feeling of distrust and is a strong signal from countries like portugal against the tendencies towards liberalisation in the nordic countries and from the commission, which has tried in vain, as we have seen, to impose a view which most member states do not accept. it is clear that the exclusively competitive perspective does not satisfy the interests of the people. let's not forget, i fought for this, the social function of public service. postal correspondence is still the preferred means of keeping in touch with family members in portugal. moreover, it continues to play an important economic and social role, both as a means of payment for public services such as water, electricity, telephone, etc., and as a means of payment for pensions, invalidity and survivors' pensions. a transition to full market opening cannot be ensured without provision for the continuation of the provision of these services. in addition, the drastic change in the weight and price limits for postal items proposed by the commission would, if adopted, have extremely serious effects on the portuguese postal service and would involve a significant loss of market and a very negative social impact. the vote on this report in plenary in december was extremely important. this is a codecision process which not only highlights the importance of the role of the european parliament but also strengthens its position at the next telecommunications council meeting, which will take place on 22 december this year. it is therefore a strong signal from the european parliament to the commission that it has reservations about the full liberalisation of the postal market.
in writing. - (pt) the nice summit will unfortunately go down in history as a further step in the militarization of the european union and as a major attack on the sovereignty of small and medium-sized countries such as portugal; the commission has also been asked to provide a report on the implementation of the new rules. the big countries, especially germany, france and the united kingdom, see their powers strengthened since they can, in practice, prevent any decision by constituting a blocking minority. this situation is aggravated by the extension of qualified majority voting, with 14 countries able, under certain conditions, to take decisions on behalf of the 27 or 28, since the right of veto on policies of major importance to portugal, such as those of the structural funds or trade policy agreements, is in practice eliminated. portugal also loses meps, despite having passed the 700 mark for a european union of 27 member states; the reduction in the number of members of parliament is particularly affecting certain countries, such as ours. finally, i would like to refer to social policy, which france was counting on when it presented its presidency, but which in the end remains a handful of very few things, despite the declarations of intent and the titles of documents with very vague content and clearly insufficient in relation to the existing problems and the enormous discontent, of which the demonstration of the workers in nice will have been an important expression as well.
in writing. - (pt) we are aware that the nice european council was perhaps the longest and most difficult summit in the history of the european union. the european council in brussels in december last year was a major event in the history of the european union. we must therefore regard its conclusions as the result of a compromise from which no one has emerged as either a winner or a loser. in our view, the fifteen have been able to agree on certain institutional changes which are essential if the union is to function with a considerably larger number of members, such as the commitment that each member state will retain the right to appoint a commissioner and the adoption of better rules of access to the enhanced cooperation mechanism. the maintenance of the unanimity rule for decisions on the structural funds, on the one hand, and taxation, on the other, are positive aspects of this summit, as these are decisive areas for countries like portugal, which still need the solidarity of the european union and the use of the tax system to stimulate the inflow of investment and promote savings. we cannot ignore, however, that the solutions adopted for the complex decision-making system in the council and the weighting of votes widen the gap between the more or less populated countries, which is less positive for us in view of our conception of a europe of sovereign nations. the resolution voted by our parliament does not reflect our orientations and concerns, since it even calls for the current method of intergovernmental conferences to be abandoned in the future. for our part, as long as europe is what it is, questions of constitutional significance or of fundamental interest to the states, such as those which were discussed in nice, cannot be settled in any other way. they must be unanimous. that is why i voted against the resolution.
in writing. - (pt) the highlight of the debate on nice is the image of a treaty which is ultimately without father or mother, since it is the son of no one. the french presidency has been confused by apologies, rather than clearly accepting the igc's findings and their reasons; the political groups, which correspond to the governments of the member states, have multiplied their criticisms. it is obvious that this is a political falsehood: what has happened is the truth of europe and not the artificial projection of a majority in this house, which has been diverted from the feelings of the european peoples. this is the same falsehood that accompanied the previous debates and explains this "disaster". the 'disaster' is the result of the unrealism of the process of european integration. i refuse to recognize a higher legitimacy than the ep, but i do recognize the full democratic legitimacy of the heads of state and government of the quinzies, meeting in nice, to represent their people and their nation, since they have come from elections in which the people participate in a more significant way. i therefore, without reserving anything for the treaty, am opposed above all to the sentiments expressed by the majority of the house, which unfortunately remains deaf to the lessons of the facts and persists in its approaches, which can only lead to further frustration.
- mr president, although this parliament is once again bringing southern africa to our agenda and particularly mozambique, one of the poorest countries in the world, as we know, devastated a year ago by an immeasurable natural disaster. as you know, mozambique is situated in one of the hottest and most sensitive spots on the african continent where any spark could trigger a new war with unimaginable consequences. the international community must exercise its right of democratic surveillance, not as interference in the internal affairs of an independent country, but as a precautionary and preventive political procedure. this joint resolution, which is objective, clear, concerned and not demagogic, is therefore strongly supported by my political group, which shares an economic, social and political diagnosis in which concerns are legitimate and does not allow any other interpretation in its text that divides the mozambican people. this parliament agrees not to overlook any factor which could endanger the peace and democratic future of mozambique. but, responsibly, he does not take sides for factions of power or position, but takes the side of mozambicans and their right to better days. we are all learning something over time and, in this exercise of political common sense, the european parliament, while forgetting nothing, remembers everything and believes that the mozambican officials will be able to read the substance of this resolution, with the certainty that we remain ready to help them without any kind of paternalism. this resolution, ladies and gentlemen, is to be read and not to be read, which would always be a perversion of the genuine concern to defend the essential principles which are at its origin. the opposite of this would be to encourage violence and to jeopardize peace against the security and democracy that mozambicans, for their own sake, have a right to achieve every day.

- thank you, chief patten.
- mr president, i would first like to congratulate the rapporteur, mrs sbarbati, on her excellent report. it is a serious and profound reflection on the problems of older people, but it is also a courageous and innovative report in its proposals to promote the active participation of older people in working, social and cultural life. the ageing of the population is now an uncontroversial fact and a significant factor of pressure on the social security systems of some member states. active and healthy ageing is a major challenge of our times and calls for a balance between community policies and national social security and public health systems. it is not just a question of limiting the issue of solidarity between generations to the financial field, but of promoting a global approach to supporting older people and their family structures, in particular women who provide care for older people. the current model of society makes the cult of youth. this is a deep-rooted idea which runs horizontally through all sectors of social life and which must be combated by the effective practice of solidarity between generations. in the context of a significant increase in life expectancy coupled with the enormous medical progress that has been made, special attention must be paid to demographic trends in the population. a coordinated strategy for employment must be developed in conjunction with measures against age discrimination, and pension and health systems must be modernised. there is an urgent need to develop specific policies and programmes for the various categories of older people. these measures will have to be adapted to the different situations and characteristics of the quality of life and economic self-sufficiency. and because we cannot treat unequal things in the same way, member states must respond to the situation of the elderly who are completely destitute and dependent on the assistance and care of their families or other persons, and on the other hand they must ensure that insurance is created to ensure that the elderly end their lives in dignity and serenity.
